# Pip & Bo: game notes

Read this before building a new world, level or feature, then read the code files named under "How the game is built". Update this file in the same push whenever a design decision, level or convention changes.

Play: https://gavyn-nygren.github.io/2d-VideoGame/ (GitHub Pages, updates itself on every merge to main)
Older demo: https://claude.ai/artifact/85NPz4hhcdi429juHSXUx7 (claude.ai artifact, near its 16 MB limit)

## Gavyn's standing decisions

- A 2D local co-op game for two players on one screen, for friends and family. Never repetitive: every level has its own twist.
- Mostly teamwork levels, a few head-to-head. Levels come in worlds of about 4, with codes like 2-3.
- Head-to-head levels are best of 3, 5 or 7 rounds. Every level lasts a few minutes.
- Gavyn picks the levels. Offer him a list of ideas and build the ones he chooses, with his rules. Keep his names (he kept "Castle Quest" over a suggested "Castle Capers").
- Keyboard, controller (Xbox mainly, others ideally) and touch must all work on every level.
- Family-friendly. World 8 uses pretend tickets and chips only: no money look, no betting, nothing that rewards risking your score. World 9's disasters stay cartoony: nobody gets hurt and nothing is scary.
- Publish each finished world to the same demo link, push it to this repo, and update the README world list.
- Build each new world or major feature in a fresh thread, briefed to read this file first.
- 1-player mode (Nov 2026): one person plays Pip or Bo and the computer plays the other. In team levels the computer is a helpful teammate; in versus levels it is the opponent (Easy, Normal or Hard). Every new level needs a computer brain too (see "Computer partner" below).
- A boss world every 10 worlds (Gavyn, Nov 2026): World 10 is the Grand Collector, World 20 is the Volcano Dragon, World 30 is the Time Thief, and so on. Worlds 11 (Summer Games) and 12 (Winter Games) come after World 10.
- World 25 was built early (Gavyn, Nov 2026) with gaps still to fill; a world can add itself from its own file (see World 25). World 24 was added that way too.
- Clean controls (Gavyn, Nov 2026): the game sees what each player is using (keyboard, Xbox, PlayStation, Switch or another controller, touch) and shows that player's own buttons everywhere. Every level opens on a ready card you can read first, and with two people both must ready up before it starts (in 1-player mode only the person does). See "Controls and the ready card" below; every new level uses it.
- Outfits (Gavyn, Nov 2026): Pip and Bo wear outfits unique to each level where it fits (space suits, pirate gear, knight armour...). Every new world and level gets its outfits while it is built (see "Outfits" below). Opus designs them; a Fable 5.2 review checks they look good and fit the theme.
- No hand-ups or head boosts for walls (Gavyn, 2026-11-15: "you help your tm8 up with x... makes no sense"). To get a partner over something too tall, use a thing in the world instead: mainly a lever lift plus a rope ladder or vine, with some variety (a seesaw, a winch bucket, a springy blade and a dropped string). 1-2 Boost Me Up keeps its head stacking, since that is the whole level. Where a head boost would skip the new helper, the room sets `passPartners` (partners walk through each other).
- Medals (Gavyn, 2026-11-14): every team level has Bronze, Silver and Gold goals the players can see. The ready card lists them; the results card shows a ladder of the three medals (earned ones in colour with "Earned", the rest grey with a lock, each goal ticked or showing your result) and what the next medal needs; the world page shows each level's best medal. Earning Silver also earns Bronze. Every new team level writes its goals in the shared format (see "Medals" below). Versus levels have no medals.

## Controls

| | Move / jump | Action | Other |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Pip** (orange, player 0) | W A S D, Space also jumps | F or E | Esc pause, R restart, M mute |
| **Bo** (blue, player 1) | Arrow keys | / . , or Enter | |

- Controller: the first controller pressed plays Pip, the second plays Bo. A or B jumps; X, Y, RB or RT is the action; Start pauses; View on the title or pause card swaps players.
- **Full screen (Gavyn, 2026-11-14).** There is no header bar. The 16:9 game screen is as big as the window allows (black bars on the spare sides) and every menu is laid over it. The only page button during a level is the small Menu button in the top-right corner (`#menuBtn`, placed by `fit()`: in the spare space above or beside the game when there is some, otherwise a small icon on the corner). Clicking or tapping it, the controller's Menu / Start button, or Esc opens the pause menu, which has the level's controls for each player's device, Players and a Settings button (see Settings below). On phones the see-through touch pads sit in the bottom corners and may cover up to 40px of the game's side edges (`PAD_OVER` in js/main.js) so the game uses the full height. Don't put level HUD in the top-right 50px corner. Controls are no longer shown during play outside the ready card and pause menu.
- Touch: ◀ ▶ ▲ ▼ for each player, plus a round action button in levels that use the action.
- The action button only exists in levels that set `def.act`. Its word changes per level (punch, honk, fire, bump...).
- Each player's device is detected on its own (see "Controls and the ready card"), so a keyboard player next to a controller player each see their own buttons. Xbox shows A / X, PlayStation ✕ / □, Switch B / Y, and a controller the browser can't name shows button numbers.

## Worlds and levels

Rules below are as built. "(Gavyn)" marks what he asked for himself.

### World 1: Sunny Meadow (team)
- **1-1 Hold the Door**: Button `a` opens gate `A` and button `b` builds bridge `X` over spikes, each only while someone stands on it. There is a button on both sides, so whoever crosses first holds it for the partner.
- **1-2 Boost Me Up**: Stand on your partner's head to reach ledges. A rider is carried and can jump again at the top of the carrier's jump. Key `k` unlocks the door. Also a gate button and a bridge button.
- **1-3 Your Color, My Color**: Orange `o` blocks hold only Pip and blue `u` blocks hold only Bo, so each carries the other over their colour. The flag needs both players (`flagNeedsBoth`).
- **1-4 Rope, Chain & Bungee**: Players are tied together (Gavyn). Three rooms, one cord each. Rope is slack (hold jump while dangling to climb). Chain is rigid (the standing player holds the other out, the hanger swings; the swing eases in and out and a caught fall keeps some swing). A hanger climbs the chain by pushing toward the holder (or jump while hanging below them) and steps out onto a ledge at the top; a holder who walks away from the edge hauls a hanger up a wall and over the lip. After the key the computer holder hauls its partner out, and a computer hanger climbs back up. Bungee is stretchy (jump while stretched to bounce higher). Partners pass through each other, and one fall respawns both.
- Team levels end when both players stand in the door `E` for 0.35 s (take the key first if there is one). A fall (spikes or off the bottom) respawns at the last flag `F` after 0.8 s. Falls are counted but add no time. Partners are solid (stand on heads, get carried) except in tether levels.

### World 2: Block Party (mixed)
- **2-1 Brick by Brick**: Reverse Tetris (Gavyn). The builder has no body and drops pieces (◀▶ move, jump rotates, ▼ drops fast). The climber climbs them to a flag 10 tiles up, then they swap (Gavyn). Timed, 2 rounds, Pip builds first. A piece stops above the climber instead of moving into them.
- **2-2 Sky Tower**: Build straight up a narrow shaft to a high ledge. Falling blocks can crush or trap the climber (Gavyn). 4 rounds so it lasts a few minutes. A squash, or 5 s trapped, costs +3 s and pops the climber out sideways, never higher than a step. Jump into a block twice to break it. Computer builder pace (Nov 2026, Gavyn found it placed blocks far too fast): in the tower it now plays at a person's pace, about one piece every 2.2 s instead of 1.1 s (a longer look at each new piece, one tap per turn or column, a short pause before it drops), and hurries only when the piece would land first or the climber is under it. Its drop check now covers every row the piece falls through, and the computer climber no longer hops down into a column the stairs still need (that shut it in a shaft by the ledge). Bots: computer pair 300/300 clear, avg 70 s (was 51 s), 84% Gold; clumsy partner 210/210; idle never clears.
- **2-3 Robo Pong** (team vs the computer, first to 7, Gavyn's spec): Pip's paddle covers the top half and Bo's the bottom half (Pip W/S, Bo ↑/↓). Robo's paddle is 50% longer and covers its whole side. The ball speeds up 1% per paddle or wall hit, compounding, and resets each point (Gavyn first said 0.1%, then agreed to 1%). Every serve goes to Robo, which returns it gently. Bot-tuned so adult pairs win about 64-78%, parent and kid about 50%, two kids about 35-40%; games last 2-4.5 min. A loss offers Rematch or Next level.
- **2-4 Crown Keep** (versus, one 90 s game): First to hold the crown for 15 s in total wins, or most crown time at 90 s. Stomp the holder to steal it. The crown is heavy (Gavyn, 2026-11-15, because the first to grab it usually won): the holder runs at 75% speed and jumps at 84% (about 70% as high, 77 px), set in `CROWN_HOLD` in js/core.js. So the map is built in steps exactly 2 tiles apart (floor, low side planks and the stone lump, middle planks, the centre crown plank, upper planks, top corner planks), and each plank overlaps the one under it, so every way up is a hop straight up through a plank; no running leaps across gaps. The computer plans with the holder's own speed and jump (cpuNav opts `run` / `jump`), and as the chaser steers down onto the holder's head when it drops off a plank over them. `node tools/crown-keep-check.js` checks every spot is reachable for both (path-finder physics and real hops with the crown) and plays computer matches: first grabber won 18 of 40 (was 15 of 20 before), about 6 steals a match, longest hold about 11 s. It was built before the best-of rule and has no rounds.

### World 3: Fairway Fun (team golf)
- Controls: the four golf levels share `GOLF_LEGEND` (level-list.js) so the ready card, pause card and top bar list aim, hold-and-release swing, cancel and the ball's boost.
- **Wide (scrolling) holes** (`def.wide`, 3-4 only): a map row can be any width; `parseLevel` sets `L.cols` and `tile()` uses it. `golfCam` (js/levels/golf.js) sets `G.camX`: a flyover from the cup to the tee while the hole's card is up, the ball near the back of the screen while aiming, following the ball in flight and the golfer on the walk; it never lets either leave the screen. `render()` draws the world shifted by `G.camX` (0 in every other level; the hills slide a little for depth) and the HUD unshifted. `drawGolfMap` shows the whole course in a strip at the bottom (ground line, portals, flag, ball, golfer, the visible part). A room's `cave: [[x0, x1, y0, y1]]` paints a dark cave wall behind open tiles (the rabbit-hole tunnel). The computer golfer's course map and shot simulator use the course width too.
- Each level has 2 holes. Pip golfs hole 1 with Bo as the ball, then they swap (Gavyn). The golfer aims with ◀▶ and holds jump to charge a meter that rises and falls, then releases to swing (▼ cancels). The ball has a small in-flight boost bar (Gavyn): hold a direction to push, 0.4 s per shot. Water, spikes, off-screen or 14 s in flight = +1 stroke from the same spot. 10 strokes = pick up. Score is strokes against par.
- **3-1 Tee for Two** (par 3+4): Pond Hop over a wall and water to a raised green; Cave Run under or over a big rock past a spike pit.
- **3-2 Bounce House** (par 2+3): Bounce pads (Gavyn's idea) and bumpers. Spring Step rolls onto 5 springs; Boomerang uses an angled pad that throws the ball back.
- **3-3 Windmill Way** (par 2+3): Windmill sails bat the ball, so time the gap. One 3-sail mill, then twin mills spinning opposite ways.
- **3-4 Portal Park** (par 5+5, a long scrolling course; Gavyn, 2026-11-15: "the final golf level should be scrolling for a longer last level in that world"): each hole is about three screens wide (`wide: true`, maps 100 and 104 tiles) and uses every World 3 trick as the world's finale. Portals keep the ball's speed; no boost while the ball rolls. **Warp Zone** (Pip golfs): over a pond, putt into the portal at the foot of a tall wall (or lob over it), out on top of a mesa, past a windmill on a floating block (a rolling ball passes under it), over a spike pit with a gold boost ring above it, onto a raised green with sand at both ends. **Rabbit Hole** (Bo golfs): putt into the rabbit hole on the mesa, roll through a dark tunnel under a lake (sand, spikes to chip over, bumpers in the roof), and the tunnel-end portal pops the ball out above the far island; a brave lob over the lake can grab the boost ring halfway. A windmill on a floating block guards the green. Bot times: computer pair about 45-60 s (normal), 50-95 s (easy), scores about par to 4 under; people take a few minutes. Medals: Gold par or better, Silver 3 or fewer over. Its records start fresh under `recordName: 'Portal Park (long course)'`; the old one-screen holes (Warp Zone par 3, Rabbit Hole par 3) are in git history before Nov 2026.
- Holes were bot-tested so a decent player scores about par, there is no fixed-shot ace, and no lie is inescapable. Boost rings (`@`, refill the ball's boost) are used in 3-4 and 20-1's Gong Golf.

### World 4: Rumble Park (versus)
- **4-1 King of the Hill** (best of 5): Stand alone on the hilltop to fill your meter (both on top scores nothing). First to 10 s, or most at the 70 s bell; from 45 s hill time counts double. Boxing glove: tap = jab, hold = charge, power grows the longer you hold (Gavyn). Full charge at 1 s is a MEGA punch. Big hits knock time off the meter.
- **4-2 Snowball Fight** (best of 7; reworked 2026-11-15 for Gavyn: aimable throws, no damage from the sky, real forts, a clear catch): 4 hits make a snowman; at the 60 s bell, fewer hits taken wins. Tap throw = quick throw (gently auto-aimed, flies over someone ducking). Hold throw = aim: you plant your feet, a dotted line shows the exact flight, ▲ ▼ tilt it (jump and drop wait while aiming), let go to throw; every aimed throw has the same speed, so the angle sets the distance, and the last angle is remembered; after 3.2 s the arm tires and throws. **Catch:** when the other player's snowball is under 0.45 s from your front, a gold "Catch!" bubble with your throw button shows over your head (the ball gets a gold ring); a press then opens your mittens for 0.45 s (even with a snowball in hand), and a catch gives you the snowball and shakes 1 snow off you. MEGA snowballs can't be caught. A tip bubble after the first hits says how until you have caught one; the round card (rounds 1-2), the level sign and the ready card say it too. **Forts:** a low wall 3 bricks long with battlements, the owner's flag and a snowball pile behind it (standing at your pile refills an empty hand in 0.15 s); your own snowballs fly over your own wall, theirs stop on it (3 hits a brick, a MEGA smashes through). From 40 s the sun comes out and both forts melt (a chip off each top brick every 1.3 s). The old avalanche and "hid too long" snow are gone; the falling flakes are only weather. ▼ ducks and packs snow. A player 2+ rounds ahead starts with 1 snow on.
- **4-3 Hot Potato Volleyball** (best of 7): Hit a potato bomb back and forth; if it's on your side when the fuse runs out, you lose (Gavyn). The fuse (22-34 s) shows on the potato and in the HUD. Each landing costs 1 s, and lying on the sand burns it 3× faster. Tap = bump, hold = power shot, jump at the net = spike, ▼ + hit = set. Where the potato meets you aims the hit (Gavyn, Nov 2026): `potContact()` measures the angle from the forehead to the potato; from under it (70° or steeper) it pops up high (flight time x1.35) and lands shorter, from the side (25° or flatter, beside your head) it flies flat (x0.65) and a quarter of the half deeper (`POT.cLo/cHi/cD/cT`, shared through `potShape()` with the brain). An arrow on a potato in your reach shows where a hit right now would send it, and HIGH! / LONG! pop on the hit. Potatoes rotate per round (`POT.twists`: Classic, Feather Spud, Bouncy Spud, Big Spud, Two Spuds, Feather, Bouncy; physics in `POT.kinds`): the Feather Spud is pale gold with a feather (gravity 560, flights x1.28, hits land 8% deeper, bumps fly 25% harder); the Bouncy Spud is rubbery green with a band (floor bounce 0.82, livelier walls and net, squashes on landing, only its first landing after a touch costs 1 s). The brain picks a contact (under / middle / side) with each shot: for middle and side it stands just to the net side of where the potato comes down (`contactSpot`) and swings once it has dropped to that height. Ladder after the change: Hard beats Easy 12/12, Hard beats Normal 5/6, Normal beats Easy 6/6, Normal v Normal even.
- **4-4 Paint the Floor** (best of 5): Walking paints the tiles; most tiles at the 45 s bell wins. Action = splat dive (bigger splash from higher, landing on a head makes them dizzy). Less splatting (Gavyn, 2026-11-15): a splat recharges in 3 s (was 0.95 s) and splashes are smaller, so walking is the main way to paint (bots: about 60% of paint from walking, was 35%). Every round has its own floor (`def.floors`, in order, one map each; Gavyn asked for more maps with helpful objects): Paint Studio (the original, plain), Bounce House (pink bounce pads, map `J`, fling you about 7 tiles up), Warp Gallery (portal pairs `O`/`Q` and `U`/`V` in the corners: your middle in one sends you out of its twin; step out before it works again), Roller Rink (paint rollers, map `y`: one at a time, about as far from both players; grab it to run 40% faster for 6 s painting a wider strip over anything, even Super Paint; plus Speedy Paint, your own colour is a fast lane) and Funhouse (all three). The pill under the level name and the round card name the floor. Behind by 6%: Super Paint (bigger splashes that recharge faster). With 12 s left a golden bucket drops near whoever trails. The brain's map (`cpuNav` opts `pads` / `portals`) knows pads and portals, and it goes for rollers like the bucket. Checks: `node tools/paint/check.js` (per floor: wins per side, paint share walk/splash/roller, bounces, warps, rollers, reachability), `node tools/paint/shots.js` (a screenshot of each floor).
- Whoever trails gets catch-up help, always shown in gold (Comeback Glove, bobble hats, Big Mitts, Super Paint). Round 1 is plain; later rounds rotate twists. A tied round replays; from the 2nd tie in a row the level's tiebreak or a sudden-death replay decides; after 4 ties in a row the match is called.

### World 5: Road Trip (team)
- **5-1 Two-Seat Car** (top-down): One player steers, the other does gas and brake/reverse (Gavyn). Action = honk. Seats swap every room. Park in the box to clear a room. A splash goes back to the last flag; a tow truck comes after 8 s stuck. Steering fix (Nov 2026, Gavyn: "it won't go certain ways sometimes"): a seat change (every room and the SWAP pad) now locks only the buttons a player is still holding, until each is let go; before, holding any one button (often the old gas key, which is also steer-up) froze the whole wheel or pedals, so the car would not turn at all. On a controller the steerer's stick now points the nose at its exact angle (`carStickAngle`, from `ax`/`ay` that `readPad` now returns): the 8-way flags need a 60% push up or down but only 35% sideways, so a soft push up or down did nothing and soft diagonals read as plain left or right.
- **5-2 Pizza Delivery** (reworked Nov 2026, Gavyn): Both carry one tray of pizza boxes up and down a hilly town; it only moves when both walk the same way. No hold button and no hop: ▲ raises your own end and ▼ lowers it (it stays where you leave it). Uphill the back one raises and the front one lowers, downhill the other way. A tilted tray makes the stack slide toward its low end, and a pizza is only lost by sliding off (a spirit level on the tray shows the tilt). Low awnings need both ends lowered (bumping one stops you, no pizza lost), jumping dogs need both raised, and the wind on the last street needs the tray tilted into it. Each room is a long scrolling street written as `course` pieces in its room def (slopes, stairs, humps, bridges, awnings, dogs, wind, signs; see the comment on `pzBuild`). Too few boxes restarts the room with a smaller order. Computer partner: levels the tray against the partner's real end and asks "Raise/Lower your end!" when it can't do it alone. Screenshots: `node tools/pizza/shots.js 4,30,60`.
- **5-3 Ice Slide**: Each move slides you until something stops you, often your partner (Gavyn). On ice you can only hop straight up; snow is grippy. A head-on crash bounces both back. Hint arrows show after 60 s in a room.
  - **Nov 2026 rework (Gavyn):** the rooms are a step harder: Slip and Stop (two gates, 6 moves), Hop Up (three floors, posts, a gate, 12), Ice Bridge (posts, a drop, a snow patch that stops a slide, water, 7) and Long Way Down (everything, 11); they were 4, 7, 7, 6. Medal: Gold 2:40, Silver 4:30. Nobody can get past a partner in the air any more: a partner who is hopping or dropping counts as standing on the spot they will land on, so a slide stops next to it (it used to slide underneath, which jumped you over each other and muddled the rooms), and a jump off the snow goes straight up (no steering in the air). The "who moves next" arrows now come from the computer partner's solver (`CPU.brains.ice.model.guide`), so there are no hand-made guide tables to redo after a map change. The computer partner moves after 0.15 s, gives a person 0.45 s on a move either could make (was 1.2 s), takes its own helpful move when the person hangs back for 1.6 s, and says "Your turn!" after 2.5 s and which way after 8 s. New look: rock as packed ice blocks (drawn once per room into a canvas), far snowy mountains, a band of light sweeping over the ice, reflections in the ice, twinkles, and glints on the water.
  - Tools: `node tools/w5/ice-solve.js` solves every room with the computer's copy of the rules (fewest moves, one solution, spots that need a go-back); `node tools/w5/ice-people.js` plays the computer with person-like partners (good, waiter, wrong, jumpy); `node tools/w5/ice-shots.js [--phone]` saves a screenshot per room.
- **5-4 Rising Lava** (reworked Nov 2026, Gavyn: "I like all the mechanics just not the level design ... lava that scrolls faster and levels that scroll upwards making them longer"; mode `lava`, `ownStep`, `roomWord: 'Tower'`): four tall towers that scroll upward, each button builds steps in the partner's colour (you pass through your partner's colour). Towers: Warm Feet (45 rows: two shafts, then routes that swap, then leapfrog), Criss-Cross (55: press together and cross, a zipper of take-turn flights, a second crossing, a double helix), Hold Tight (53: hold plates, a three-trade relay, then hold-and-dash bridges with the button on the bridge; surges every 8 s), Volcano Top (68: quick leapfrog, a plate relay with surges in rows 15-31, a crossing and helix, then both star buttons open the hatch round the door). Code: js/levels/world5/rising-lava.js (the mode), js/levels/world5/rising-lava-map.js (the towers, made by `node tools/w5/lava-towers.js`; the stub in level-list.js keeps the level's place). Map chars are in the mode's header.
  - Camera like 17-3 (eases up, the lower player always on screen), leash 13 tiles ("Wait for me!"), climb strip on the right edge, a glow and "Lava N m below" when the lava is off screen. Lava (px/s): 13 / 16 / 15 / 19 (raised from the plan's 10/13/12/16 so it gets close for a steady pair), delays 6/5/5/4 s, catch-up: more than 230 px under the lower player it rises 3x, never more than 330 px under. It starts 150 px (5 tiles) under each tower's start floor, so the floor is safe for about 12-17 s (review: at 60 px the floor was gone after 7 s in tower 4 and a slow pair burned there over and over).
  - Lava ouch (Gavyn's addition): a short "Hot toes!" beat, then BOTH go back to the last team checkpoint (lit only once both stood on its row; the tower start before that). Everything above it resets (latch buttons, steps, hold steps, ice pops, hatch, stars), the lava drops to 120 px under the checkpoint and waits 2 s. Each ouch slows that tower's lava (x0.85, at least half speed) and 2 ouches in 20 s give a breather. Holding ▼ for 1 s hops just you back to the team checkpoint (no reset); once the lava is within 45 px of that checkpoint the hop is refused ("Too hot down there! Climb!"), because it would drop you straight into the lava and send both back. A hold plate's owner is safe and the lava sinks to 90 px under them (60 px/s), so stepping off the plate leaves 5-6 s to get away (review: was 30 px, 2 s). Medals count team ouches.
  - Medals: Bronze "Climb out of the lava"; Gold 4:10 and at most 2 ouches; Silver 6:30 and at most 8. (Review: the build's 3:30 / 5:50 came from the computer pair's 3:03, which no person-like run reached: a careful person with the computer takes 3:45-4:05, two careful people about 4:50, two kids 5:20-6:00.)
  - Brain (js/cpu/levels/world5/rising-lava.js): cpuNav on a window of rows round the two players (cpu-nav.js got `rows/r0/r1` options; other levels unchanged); the window only moves while standing; a jump in the air keeps flying at its old target if the tiles change mid-jump; holds a plate until the partner stands above its steps (a hop on the top step doesn't count; a fall back below does); never waits by steps it already climbed past; no polite waiting with the lava within 150 px; drops its plan after every trip back.
  - Bot results: computer pair 175-183 s, no ouches (towers about 37/35/47/55 s), Gold. Clumsy partner (cpu-test --who=noisy, either side) 300-390 s with 3-7 ouches, one 529 s / 18 (Hold Tight is the hard one). tools/w5/lava-people.js: careful person 223-234 s, 0 ouches; slow person 240-263 s, 1-2 ouches; eager kid 333-542 s, 7-18 ouches; all clear. Idle partner never clears. Held-key flips: each key at most 8 a second, all keys together 12-14 only in short steering moments at take-off points. Review runs with TWO person-like players (no computer partner): two careful players 4:51 with 2 ouches, two kids who stop to look after every landing 5:23 with 0, careful + slow 4:50-5:20 with 0-2, two slow players 5:05-6:00 with 0-4, careful + excitable kid 6:00-11:40 with 4-17; the lava comes within 100 px 0-3 times in towers 1-2 and 3-10 times in towers 3-4. Forced burns at random moments (every 4-18 s, all checkpoints): nobody ever ends up inside a block, off the checkpoint row or within 55 px of the lava, and the pair always finishes.
  - Tests: `node tools/w5/lava-check.js` (each tower on paper: every button reachable, both reach the door, no group of steps can be skipped, checkpoint spots are standing room), `node tools/cpu-test.js 5-4 --who=pair|noisy|idle`, `node tools/w5/lava-people.js`.
- 4 rooms (Rising Lava: 4 towers) per level. Failure is soft with automatic rescue. In Ice Slide and Rising Lava, hold ▼ for 1 s to go back.

### World 6: Backyard Bash (mixed)
- **6-1 Monster Feeding** (team): One throws food (hold and release), the other catches it in a basket and feeds the monster before it gets grumpy (Gavyn). 4 rooms, roles swap. A tantrum spills a nearby catcher's basket. Stars per room by tantrums. Short throws (Gavyn, 2026-11-15: "the AI throws way too many items and doesn't come to get items... make it so I can't throw food all the way to the end"): a full throw lands only about halfway to the monster (`throwRange` [120, 300] in rooms 1-2, [120, 265] in Peek-a-Boo so it stops short of the plank, [120, 150] in Twin Trouble, whose treehouse can't throw shorter) and the toss reach is 120 px (was 160), so the catcher runs out to catch and carries the basket over. The computer chef throws one food at a time (the next charge starts only once the last food is coming down, after a short beat), waits while the catcher walks food over, and says "Come and get it!" to a catcher out of reach (one food at the nearest spot every 6 s if they never come). The computer catcher waits just inside the far end of the throw, carries a full basket (or a part-full one when the monster passes 68 grumpy or no food has come for 2.5 s) and comes back. Gold time 3:45 (was 3:00). Bot times: computer pair about 2:27, noisy partner 2:43-2:49, easy-skill pair about 2:34, all 12 stars.
- **6-2 Tug of War** (versus, best of 5, 60 s rounds): Mash to pull, power-ups fall in the middle (Gavyn). Every press tugs; a press on the drum beat heaves harder. Jump lets go of the rope to grab a falling crate; action at your rope end grabs it again. Mud spreads from 20 s so rounds always end. Rebalanced Nov 2026 (Gavyn: all Perfects still lost to the computer): the beat is the real strength now. A tug is 1.5 px (a fast mash ~11 px/s), a Perfect heave 22 px and each Perfect in a row +3 px more (up to +12), so hitting every BOOM beats any amount of mashing. A beat track in the sky shows the timing: Pip's notes slide in from the left, Bo's from the right, and you pull when yours is in the gold ring at the drum (white band = Good). The beat speeds up through a round (0.85 s to 0.50 s over 45 s; each round starts 0.04 s faster) and the Perfect/Good windows shrink with it (a "Faster!" pop at each third). Big drum crate: your next 3 BOOMs are yours (any press in the window is a double Perfect, red drum notes) and the other player gets no heave on them (grey crossed notes). Dug in (gold zone by your mud) any press in the window counts Good, mash or not. Check balance with `node tools/w6/tug-check.js --skill=normal` (scripted person styles vs the computer): perfect and good beat Normal and Hard 10/10 (good beats Normal, loses to Hard), sloppy/kid/mash lose to Normal, everything beats Easy.
- **6-3 Train Tracks** (team, top-down): One lays track ahead of a moving train, the other flips junction levers to route each passenger home (Gavyn). 4 rooms, roles swap. Nothing fails: the train waits at gaps, a wrong lever means a detour lap, and a gold bird helps when you're stuck.
- **6-4 Sumo Bounce** (versus, best of 7, 50 s rounds): Bouncy balls on a round mat over a pool; knock the other in (Gavyn). Action = boing dash (hold to charge), ▼ = squat heavy. The first splash pops your floatie, the second loses. The mat shrinks from 25 s; later rounds add twists, and round 7 is always Tiny Mat.

### World 7: Castle Quest (mixed)
- **7-1 Cannon Crew** (team): One loads the cannon (powder and a ball), the other aims and fires to knock down a castle (Gavyn). 4 castles, roles swap. Flags drop when the block under them breaks or moves. Stars by shots against par; after par + 3 shots the castle gets wobbly so every castle ends. The loader's action button shouts FIRE.
- **7-2 Seesaw Climb** (team): Land on the high end to launch your partner up the tower (Gavyn). Higher drop, higher flight; jump just as your partner lands for a SUPER launch. 4 floors, optional gems. A head jump off a standing partner is only a small hop, so there are no solo skips.
- **7-3 Laser Vault** (team): Only the lookout's lantern shows the hidden lasers (Gavyn); the lookout also flips fuse boxes that shut laser walls for 7-8 s while the runner jumps, ducks and sneaks to the treasure. 4 rooms, roles swap, ending in an alarm escape. Stars per room by zaps.
- **7-4 Bumper Cars** (versus, best of 7, 60 s rounds, top-down): Bump the other car into the moat (Gavyn). Action = boost. 2 flags each; a splash costs one. Driving off by yourself only teeters ("Phew!"). The floor crumbles from 30 s. 2+ rounds behind: a gold extra flag and a super boost.
- In seated roles that never jump (gunner, lookout), controller A also does the action.

### World 8: Lucky Casino (mixed)
- **8-1 Plinko Drop** (team): One player is the chip falling through the pegs, the other slides paddles that kick it toward the JACKPOT slot; roles swap every drop (Gavyn). 3 boards of 4 drops, no fail. Tickets by slot; each board gives 0-3 stars, and the level is scored by stars, not time (`noTime`). Gold Lucky paddles help after missed jackpots, but only when both players took part in the drop.
- **8-2 Roulette Wheel** (versus, best of 5): Stand on a spinning wheel, race to the colour the ball lands on and bump the other off it (Gavyn). Action = bump. At LOCK the player on the lit colour closest to the ball wins a chip. First to 3 chips wins the round.
- **8-3 Slot Machine Run** (team): Giant slot reels are platforms; the stopper stops them so the runner can jump across (Gavyn). The payline picture is the platform (BAR step, 7 tall step, bell step with tickets, cherry trampoline, lemon nothing). 6 rooms, roles swap.
- **8-4 Coin Pusher** (versus, best of 5, 60 s rounds): Drop coins onto a moving shelf to push the pile off your side for points (Gavyn). Grab coins from the fountain, then press the action standing on the glass on your colour (a low hop is fine; higher is refused). First to 32, or most at the bell. Bonus Cup = 2 points and a Big Shove on your side. Whoever is behind in rounds starts with 2 coins in their tube (Lucky start). The game pauses itself after 40 s with no input.
- Pretend tickets and chips only; coins are drawn as star tokens; no money or betting words (Gavyn). Gold catch-up help is named "Lucky ..." (Lucky paddles, Lucky chip, Lucky slow-mo, Lucky shove).

### World 9: Great Escapes (team)
Famous natural disasters from history, kept light and cartoony: nobody gets hurt, lava is a "Hot foot!", ash is fluffy, quakes rattle teacups. Gavyn picked the four levels and their order; the world name was Claude's pick. Each level has 4 rooms and a small history sign (79 AD, 1906, 1883, Ice Age).
- **9-1 Pompeii Escape**: Ash falls and lava creeps in; help each other climb the rooftops to the harbor boat (Gavyn). Every teamwork wall (4-5 tiles) has a wooden crane lift and a rope ladder (Gavyn, 2026-11-15; they replaced a head boost and an arm that pulled the partner up): one stands on the plank deck at the foot of the wall, the other presses the action at the lever beside it and the deck hoists the rider onto the roof; up there the rider presses the action at the edge to unroll the rope ladder, and the lever-puller climbs it (jump or ▲ to grab, hold to climb, ▼ down). Nobody can ride alone (the lever is out of reach of the deck), and partners pass through each other (`passPartners`) so a head boost can't skip it. Jobs swap at every wall (`def.leads` per room: who rides; 10 walls), shown by "Ride the lift!" / "Pull the lever!" badges. The lava creeps sideways along the street, never closer than 56 px behind the rearmost player; walking back into it or into a pool is a hot foot: back to your own banner, the lava backs off and slows. Ash clumps with landing shadows make you sneeze (a small hop, never during teamwork). Set pieces: a wobbly column that tips into a bridge once you both stand on it (Room 2), a cart that rolls only when you both push (Room 4), and the ship that sails when you're both on deck. Gold help: Cool breeze (2nd hot foot). Medal by time and hot foots.
- **9-2 San Francisco 1906 Quake**: The ground cracks; hold up a wobbly bridge for each other to cross (Gavyn). Each crack has a plank worked from a crank on the OTHER street. The holder stands on the crank and holds the action (hold); left/right then balance a ball on a gauge over their head against the crosser's weight, sliding crates and the rumble. Too long in the red, or the ball at the end, is a gentle "Whoa!": the crosser slides back. Rumbles come in waves with a warning on the HUD seismograph, so patient crossers wait for the calm. Crossing is tiptoe speed whether walking or hopping, so jumping is never a shortcut, and the gate at the end opens only when every crossing is done. Roles swap every bridge (8 holds each). Rooms: Nob Hill, Cable Car Line (the holder pulls a cable car), Market Street (crates), The Ferry Building. Gold braces after 2 whoas on a plank (sooner for a holder who needed them before), gold cable for the cable car.
- **9-3 Ice Age Glacier**: Slide down a cracking glacier and swap who steers each time the ice splits (Gavyn). Both ride one ice floe on a scrolling run the mode draws itself. The steerer brakes (◀) for bump fields and pushes (▶) for big gaps, and picks the high or low shelf at forks (▲/▼); the hopper hops (▲) crevasses and ducks (▼) icicle arches. The floe cracks at each split: Pip steers rooms 1 and 3, Bo rooms 2 and 4. No action button. A missed gap is a snowball pop 460 px back; a bump at speed is a bonk spin. Mammo the mammoth packs and then bridges a gap only after tries both players took part in, so nobody is carried alone. 10 s with no input pauses the floe ("Brrr, chilly!"). Snowflakes x of 99; medal by bonks and flakes. The longest World 9 level (about 3-4.5 min).
- **9-4 Krakatoa Ash Run**: Ash hides the way; one clears a path with a fan, the other leads (Gavyn). Thick ash is drawn over the level. The fanner holds the action (fan) to blow a cone of ash away and blow off ash drifts; the leader follows the footprints, pulls levers (pull) that open bamboo gates and drop rope bridges for the fanner, and presses the action elsewhere to shout "This way!". At windsock gaps (7 tiles) only the fanner's gust carries the leader across (an updraft while over the gap, so a tap jump or walk-off floats too). Roles swap every room: Jungle Trail, Lava Rocks, Cliff Path, Beach to the Ship. Camps are checkpoints; hold ▼ 1 s to go back to camp. The act labels switch per role every room. Help: quick nudges at gaps and drifts, a parrot with hints, a gold Super gust after 2 misses at a gap, a gold Sea breeze when stuck. Lava is "Toasty toes!" here (9-1 says "Hot foot!").
- Every World 9 level was bot-tested on keyboard, a stubbed controller and touch (844x390 and 390x844), and with solo, idle and button-mashing bots: none can be cleared by one player, and none gets stuck. Bot times for a perfect pair: 9-1 about 50 s, 9-2 about 88 s, 9-3 about 172 s, 9-4 about 51 s; slower human-like bots take 2-4.5 min. Real playtests may show the medal limits need tuning.
- Graphics polish (Nov 2026, worlds 6-9): shared drawing helpers for these four worlds live at the top of `js/levels/world6/monster-feeding.js` (it loads first): `paShadow`, `paBox`, `paLayer` (a cached offscreen layer), `paBehindTiles` (pastes a backdrop layer with the room's solid cells cut out, once per room; its optional `over` painter bakes a room's still props onto the same picture, and only the boxes that hold anything are copied each frame), and the World 6 backyard (`paBackyard`). World 7's far castle (`paCastlesLayer`) is in `cannon-crew.js`, World 8's casino town (`paCasinoCity`) in `slot-machine-run.js`. All drawing only, with `hash()` for placement (never `Math.random()`).

### World 10: Collector's Vault (team boss)
One big boss level (Gavyn picked "The Grand Collector"); the world name is Claude's pick (it was "Grand Finale" until Gavyn asked for a rename in Nov 2026, since more worlds come after it); Gavyn said to keep "Collector's Vault" (2026-11-06). A goofy brass-and-purple robot took a piece of every world and keeps them in his glass belly-jar. The 9 pieces are his health bar (a HUD strip, top centre). He is never hurt: hits make him wobble and pop his monocle. At the end he naps, wakes up friendly, waves, and every piece flies home.
- **10-1 The Grand Collector**: 4 rooms, one phase each, every phase borrowing an earlier world's look and tricks. Every hit needs both players, and jobs swap inside each phase ("Swap jobs!", badges, per-role act labels).
  - **Castle Cannons** (7-1): the loader brings powder and a ball, the gunner aims and fires at his wall, then his belly. Pieces 7, 2.
  - **Jackpot Reels** (8-3): the stopper stops reels from the deck below, the runner hops up them; the top cherry bounces the runner onto his hat (pop the dome, then bop the button). Pieces 8, 3.
  - **Hot Floor Climb** (5-4, 9-1): buttons build steps in the partner's colour while cartoon lava rises; turn the steam valves together. Pieces 5, 9.
  - **Grand Finale** (4-3, 6-2): set and spike his balls back (whoever spiked sets next), then pull his plug together. Pieces 4, 6, 1.
- Soft failure only: his tosses and claw are telegraphed and cause a "Bonk!" (counted, never death). He waits during cards, cutscenes, celebrations and when nobody plays for 10 s. Gold help per phase (Lucky aim, Lucky slow-mo / magnet / hold, Cool breeze, Super steps, Big mitts, Floaty balls, Magnet balls, Super grip).
- Fairness rules from QA: in Room 1 a key held from before a swap is ignored until let go (or 0.4 s). In Room 2 the low good reels stay stopped after a dome pop, so a miss costs one climb, not all of it. In Room 3 the checkpoint never rises above your own button or valve floor, the lava never rises past floor 3, a hint says "Drop down to your button!" when you climb too high, and Super steps wait while someone stands under them. In Room 4 the ball rally is "BOING!" and "N more hits!", fast whiffs add a short lock (so mashing is slow), and the plug pull is tuned so a pair wins in about 10-13 s.
- Hints, HUD and cards: the per-room keys in the top legend and the in-world hints follow each player's own device (keyboard, controller or touch). The news line, health strip and pills fade while a player is under them. The results card is 4 tiles and 1 line, with no Falls tile. The ending is about 6.5 s (hops and confetti), and once he is beaten in this session, the final card (after the last level of the game) says "The end" with a "You beat the Grand Collector!" line (`showFinal` finds him by `mode === 'collector'`).
- Code: one file, `js/levels/world10/grand-collector.js`, in marked sections (GC CORE, then GC PHASE 1-4). Each room def names its `phase`; the core owns `MODES.collector` and calls `GC_PHASES[phase]` hooks. R restarts the whole level from phase 1.
- Bot times: perfect pair about 3:00, good pair 4:18-4:58 (phases about 55-74, 51-81, 52-58, 57-102 s), human-like parent + kid bots 4:51-5:40. Trophy: Gold up to 5:30 and 5 bonks, Silver up to 9:00 and 14 bonks. Real playtests may show these need tuning.

### World 11: Summer Games (mixed)
Summer sports, two team levels then two versus levels (Gavyn picked all four and their order; he chose them from a list of 8 ideas). Never use the word "Olympic(s)" or draw the five rings in game text or art (trademark); "Summer Games", medals, podiums, a torch and judges are fine.
- **11-1 Synchro Diving** (team, `noTime`, `noFalls`): Pip and Bo dive off twin boards on one tower, mirror images of each other, and the judges score them on matching the call and on being in sync (Gavyn). Six dives climb from the 3 m springboard to the 10 m platform: Warm-up (1 flip), Double flip, Mirror dive 5 m (Pip picks up to 2 moves, Bo copies), Mirror dive 7.5 m (Bo picks up to 3, Pip copies), Flip-twist-flip and Grand finale (10 m). A call card shows each dive. Both press ▲ to say ready (nothing starts until both are ready, so nobody dives alone), then a shared 3-2-1-GO. On the springboard, ▲ on each beat bounces higher (up to 3) for more air time; a press off the beat wobbles the board and the next beat gives no bounce, so mashing never bounces. In the air ▲ flips, ◀ ▶ twists and holding ▼ tucks so flips spin faster. Divers must be straight before the water: still spinning is a cartoon SPLOOSH! belly flop and still tucked is a CANNONBALL!, both soft. Three animal judges hold up cards: an owl for Pip, a frog for Bo (moves matched plus the entry) and a top-hat penguin for sync, which counts double (takeoff, the call's moves at the same time, the entry). Dive points = (Pip + Bo + 2 × sync) × difficulty, and the medal goes by total points: Gold 290+, Silver 210+, otherwise Bronze. A partner who only jumps, or two mashers, get Bronze. Gold help: after a messy dive, 'Gold air time' (+0.3 s). No action button. Hints follow each player's device; in 1-player mode the computer's panel reads '(computer)'. Bot times: computer pair about 89 s (358 points), noisy pairs 91-95 s, scripted people 92-100 s; an idle partner never clears.
- **11-2 Tandem Velodrome** (team, top-down, `noTime`): Pip and Bo ride one tandem round a banked wooden velodrome against made-up rival tandems (Gavyn). The pedaler presses ◀ and ▶ in turn on the beat of Bongo the bear's drum; a beat lane in their corner panel shows each beat and the key to press. Perfect or Good presses build pedal power; an off-beat press adds little, the same foot twice about half, and extra presses in one beat nothing (so mashing is slow). The leaner holds ▲ (W/↑/Space, controller A or stick up) to lean in and ▼ to sit up, keeping the needle in the gold wedge, which follows the bank and the speed. Too little lean drifts the bike up to the rail: a cartoon "Wobble!", the bike stops until the leaner leans in ("Phew!"). Too much drops it onto the blue apron at 62% speed, and slower still while the lean stays far too deep, so holding ▲ all race is no shortcut. 4 races, jobs swap every race (Pip pedals races 1 and 3): Warm-up Laps (3 laps), Draft Line (5 laps; riding just behind a bike gives +18%), Sprint Finish (on the bell lap the drum and the rivals speed up), Keirin Final (6 laps behind an owl pace bike nobody may pass; it pulls off after 3 laps with "GO GO GO!"). Places score 3/2/1/0 podium points out of 12: Gold 10+, Silver 6+, otherwise Bronze. Once every rival has finished, the current lap becomes your last, so a slow race stays short. Gold help: Steady wheels (wider wedge and slower slide, after 2 rail wobbles or 6 apron trips in a race), Big drum (wider beat windows and one missed beat forgiven, after 12 off-beat presses), Tailwind (+25% once 0.12 lap behind every rival). An idle leaner sits on the rail for good and an idle pedaler never moves. Corner panels and prompts follow each player's device; in 1-player mode the computer's seat is marked "(computer)" with no keys. Bot times: computer pair about 2:43 (12 of 12); scripted medium pair about 3:00 (12 of 12); parent + kid 3:15-3:25 (2-8 points); kid + computer 3:05-3:15 (6-11 points); two kids or a masher 3:30-3:50 (Bronze).
- **11-3 Hurdle Dash** (versus, best of 5): Race each other over hurdles; jumps timed right give a speed burst (Gavyn). Split screen: Pip's lane on top, Bo's below, each with its own camera and the other runner small in the far lane; a race map at the bottom shows who leads. Hold ▶ to run (◀ brakes), jump hurdles, and the action (dip) leans your chest at the line. A gold zone painted before each hurdle comes from your own speed: taking off in it gives a SPEED BURST (a gold trail, faster for about 0.9 s, with a "BURST xN" streak). Just outside the gold still clears; too early or too late clips the hurdle (it tips over, "Too early!" / "Too late!", you stumble); running in without jumping is a "Whoops!" and a bigger slowdown. Nobody is hurt. A hop needs a 0.12 s push-off step after landing, so mashing jump still runs, just slowly. Rounds: 110 m Hurdles first, then three of High Hurdles / Tricky Spacing / Steeplechase (water: landing short is a splash and you wade) / Windy Day (tail- and headwind stretches), then a 220 m Double Dash. The first chest across wins, timed within the tick: under 0.002 s is a dead heat (replayed), under 0.1 s gets a PHOTO FINISH stamp. The 2nd tie in a row goes to more speed bursts, else a 60 m Sudden Sprint; a safety bell gives a race nobody finishes to whoever is furthest. Whoever trails in rounds gets gold Golden spikes (a 1.5x wider gold zone, or 2x and longer bursts when 2 behind), announced on the round card and shown as a chip in that lane. No round clock (`ownStep`); hints follow each player's device and are never shown for the computer. Results: bursts, clips, best streak, splashes, plus the fastest 110 m line. Bot times: computer pair 139-163 s (3-2 matches), a 3-0 about 75 s; Hard beats Easy 8/8 from both sides, normal vs normal 8-8 over 16 seeds; a kid-like scripted player beats Easy about 4 in 5, a good player beats Hard about 1 in 3.
- **11-4 Javelin Throw** (versus, best of 5): Power meter, angle and wind flags; longest throw each round wins (Gavyn). Side-view stadium; Pip runs on the far runway and Bo on the near one, both at once, 3 throws each a round, with a 10 s shot clock per throw. Hold ▶ to run (◀ brakes). The power meter swings from GO and starts each throw at a random point, so watch it: its swing is capped by your speed, and pressing throw at the top at full speed is "Perfect!". A press before you run doesn't count ("Run first!"). Keep holding throw to raise the angle dial (12-75°), then let go. You slide while aiming (◀ digs in), and the front foot over the line while still holding the javelin is a whistle and red flag: "Foul!", no mark (the line glows red as you get close). Real ballistics with drag and wind: about 88 m for a perfect throw in calm air, best angle about 41-47°. A javelin that lands behind the line is "Too short!". Flags, windsock, clouds and torch flame show the wind honestly. Slow-mo on release, whoosh, thunk, then a distance pop-up and marker flags side by side. Round 1 Calm Day; rounds 2-4 are Big Tailwind, Swirling Gusts and Target Ring (+4 m in the gold ring at 74-84 m) in shuffled order; round 5 and sudden death are "Last Throw" (one each). Ties replay ("Dead heat: X m each!"); on a second tie in a row, the longer second-best throw decides, then most metres this match. Gold help, announced on the round card and at GO: 1 round behind gets a slower gold steady meter whose top 7% counts as full; 2+ behind also gets a gold best-angle mark on the dial. First fouls and flat tapped throws get a hint, never shown for the computer. Pauses after 40 s with no input. Results: Longest / Average throw, Perfect meters, Ring hits or Fouls, plus "Longest of the match". Brain: Normal and Hard time their start to the meter and dig in while aiming; Easy just runs. Average marks: Easy about 58 m, Normal about 76 m, Hard about 83 m. Matches take about 2-3.5 min.
- Each level was built and then QA'd by a second agent that tried to break it (mashing, holding one key, idle or clumsy partners, Easy vs Hard from both sides) on keyboard, a stubbed controller and touch at 1280x800, 844x390 and 390x844. Medal limits and computer skill are tuned on bots only; real playtests may show they need tuning.
- Code: `js/levels/world11/<slug>.js` and `js/cpu/levels/world11/<slug>.js` (synchro-diving, tandem-velodrome, hurdle-dash, javelin-throw); defs between BEGIN/END lines in the World 11 block of `level-list.js`.

### World 12: Winter Games (mixed)
Winter sports, three team levels then a versus finale (Gavyn picked all four and their order from a list of 8 ideas; he swapped Figure Skating Pairs for Ski Jump). Same trademark rule as World 11: no "Olympic(s)" and no five rings in game text or art; "Winter Games", medals, snowflakes and penguin judges are fine.
- **12-1 Curling** (team, `noTime`): A top-down curling sheet. The thrower aims with ▲▼, picks the curl with ◀▶, and holds the action to charge a rising-and-falling meter, then lets go to slide the stone. A release in the "too gentle" zone is refused, so no stone is wasted. The sweeper starts out of broom reach, skates to the moving stone and holds the action beside it to sweep. They have to keep skating along or fall behind ("Keep skating!"). Sweeping makes the stone go farther and curl less, and an unswept stone always stops short of the house, so both jobs are needed. There are 4 ends of 4 stones each, and the roles swap every end (Pip throws ends 1 and 3). The ends are: Draw to the Button, Snowman Guards, Robot Takeout (knocking a robot out is worth +2), and Snowy Patches (snow stops an unswept stone; sweep through it or clear it first). Rings score 3/2/1. An end with no stone in the house is replayed with gold "Lucky slick ice": stronger sweeping, a longer broom grab, a gold path and stop X, and the End 2 guards moved aside. Each end gives up to 3 stars, 12 in all, and the level is scored by stars, not time (`noTime`). The thrower and sweeper are Gavyn's brief. Bot times: computer pair about 2:20 (8-10 stars), clumsy pairs 3:15-4:15.
- **12-2 Bobsled** (team): Push off together, jump in, then lean the same way into every curve (Gavyn). Pip and Bo ride one two-person bobsled down an ice run, seen from just behind the sled (pseudo-3D; `def.ownStep` + `def.ownHUD`). There are 4 runs: First Run, Snake Bends, Hairpin Hill and Thunder Run. The pilot (front seat) and brakeman (back seat) swap every run. **Push start:** both push with the action on the ticking beat. On the beat is strong and off the beat is weak; both on the same beat is TOGETHER!. Each rider hops in with ▲ in the gold zone; anyone still out at the start line scrambles in and the sled loses speed. **Run:** every curve needs BOTH riders leaning its way and holding it (taps and ◀▶ flip-flops don't count). Both leaning gives a smooth line and a short boost. One leaning is a wobble up the wall. Nobody, or the wrong way, climbs to the lip ("Whoa!"), and the sled stays there until both lean. So one rider alone, or with an idle or button-mashing partner, can never finish. S-bends show "Flip". The pilot steers through snow rings on straights (speed boost) and sees the next curve early; their action CALLs it, which shows it in the brakeman's badge. The brakeman holds the action from the first red BRAKE board down to hairpin speed. Too fast means the lip and about 3–4 s lost; nothing ever crashes. Both hold ▼ in TUCK zones and press ▲ together before jump bumps (Big air!). **Gold help (announced):** after 4 wobbly curves, or 4 s stuck on the lip, LUCKY RAILS turn on. A lean counts for 0.9 s after you let go, the sled climbs slower and the hop window is wider; it still needs both riders. **Pause:** 10 s with no press from either rider pauses the sled ("Brrr!") until they press. **Score:** the four run times added up, against the summed pars (198 s). Gold is ≤1.07× and ≤3 Whoas; Silver is ≤1.35× and ≤10 Whoas. The results show Smooth curves, Snow rings, Best push start and Whoas. Bot times: perfect pair about 3:20 (Gold), parent + kid about 4:00 (Silver).
- **12-3 Ski Jump** (team, `noTime`): One jumps, the other times the wind fans (Gavyn). Side view of a big ski jump. One player is the jumper and the other is the wind crew, and the jobs swap every jump. The jumper presses jump or the action to leave the start bar, holds ▼ to tuck on the in-run, presses jump just before the gold edge, leans ◀ ▶ in the air to keep the pose needle green, and presses ▼ just before touchdown for a telemark. The crew starts in the hut at the left end of the catwalk, where no fan can reach. They run to the giant fans and press the action as the jumper flies over (a press is a short blast, holding keeps it going, and a held button also starts each fresh fan the crew reaches). A fan that runs too long puffs out, so blowing early wastes it. Even a perfect jump with no fan push lands short of the green line, so one player alone can never count a jump. A short jump is a "Fluff!" into soft snow and is tried again. After 2, 3 and 5 short tries the fans get announced gold help: Lucky, Super and Mega tailwind. The fans blow harder and reach farther, the cart tank gets bigger and the night gust calms. The help fades between the green line and the K line, so it can't carry a jump past the hill. There are 4 hills with 2 jumps each, and Pip jumps first on every hill:
  - **Normal Hill:** 3 fans.
  - **Large Hill:** 4 fans and snowflake rings.
  - **Night Hill:** a Gust-o-matic headwind over the red fan. Starting that fan early blocks the gust.
  - **Fan Cart Cliff:** a snow pit cuts the landing hill. The crew drives one fan on a rail cart and holds to blow once the jumper is in the air, using a small air tank.

  Score per jump is distance (60 at the K line) + style from 3 penguin judges + 5 per ring. Stars per hill run 1 to 3, 12 in all. noTime. Bot times: computer pair about 2:10, clumsy pairs about 3 min.
- **12-4 Ice Hockey** (versus, best of 5): One on one, with a goalie robot in each net (Gavyn). A top-down one-on-one game. Pip defends the left net and Bo the right, each with a goalie robot that slides along the goal line and reacts about 0.2 s late. Skating is slippery and you aim the way you skate. Keys and touch aim 8 ways and get aim help; a controller stick aims any way and gets less help. With the puck, tap to shoot or hold for a slapshot (full power at 0.9 s, fires by itself at 1.6 s). Without the puck, the action is a poke check that steals it. Bumps are bouncy ("BOING!") and knock a carried puck loose, and skating the puck into the other skater pops it loose ("BLOCKED!"). Skaters can't enter the creases, and the robot sweeps a slow puck out of its crease. A round is first to 3 goals or most goals at the 60 s bell; the clock stops for goals and faceoffs. A tie at the bell goes to 15 s golden-goal overtime with sleepy robots; if that runs out, the player whose attacking end holds the puck wins. Round 1 is plain. Rounds 2-5 are Big Puck, Double Puck, Bouncy Boards and Snowman Shuffle in shuffled order, and a tied round replays its twist. Catch-up: a player 2+ rounds behind gets a gold Lucky stick (longer reach, 10% faster shots) and a Big Glove for their robot. Stats tiles: Goals, Shots on goal, Steals, Robot saves. Bot times: computer pair 2:10-2:40; Hard beats Easy from both sides; a kid-like scripted player beats Easy, but a stronger kid script only 3 of 12 times (watch this in playtests).
- Each level was built, then QA'd by three reviewers (gameplay and fairness, input and screens, code), fixed, given a computer brain, then attacked by a fourth reviewer with scripted "people" (who take the computer's job, wait, wander, mash), and fixed again. Tested on keyboard, a stubbed controller and touch at 1280x800, 844x390 and 390x844. One player alone, an idle partner or a button-masher can't clear any team level. Medal limits and computer skill are tuned on bots only; real playtests may show they need tuning.
- Code: `js/levels/world12/<slug>.js` and `js/cpu/levels/world12/<slug>.js` (curling, bobsled, ski-jump, ice-hockey); defs between BEGIN/END lines in the World 12 block of `level-list.js`. All four levels run their own step (`ownStep`).

### World 13: Spooky Hollow (mixed)
Halloween, kept silly-spooky: friendly ghosts, a goofy skeleton, sleepy owls, bats that only squeak. Nothing is scary and nobody gets hurt (fails are "Sploosh!", "Boing!", "Boo-hoo!" and a puff of purple smoke). Three team levels then a versus finale. Gavyn asked for a Haunted Mansion (his own idea) plus a few more; he picked Pumpkin Patch Roll, Graveyard Bone Hunt and Broomstick Race from 5 ideas (he skipped Witch's Potion Lab and Trick-or-Treat Dash). The order and the world name are Claude's picks.
- **13-1 Pumpkin Patch Roll** (team, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`): One pushes a giant pumpkin, the other rides on top and leans (Gavyn's pick). 4 rooms (Patch Hills, Pond Bridge, Scarecrow Ridge, Hayride Hill); the jobs swap each room. The pusher walks into the pumpkin from behind to push it uphill and hops rocks (▲). On the crest before a steep drop to a pond or mud ditch, the pusher leapfrogs (▲ while touching, only when the pumpkin is slow) to the front and walks ◀ into it to hold it back down the hill and over the bridge. The rider balances on a bar: the roll carries their feet, ▶ leans uphill, ◀ brakes downhill, ▼ hugs to soften rocks and gusts (arms tire after about 1.2 s, so hugging can't replace balancing). Room 3 has warned gusts. Onto a bridge faster than 130 is a cartoon splash or splat, back to the last hay bale; falling off is a tumble (back on the spot on level ground). Steep climbs need both the push and the lean; a lone pusher or rider stalls at the first climb. Gold help: gold planks after 2 splashes at one bridge (safe to 205), super gold after 3 (250), Mister Straw piles hay on the bridge after 5; sticky boots after 3 and 6 rider tumbles; Mister Straw helps on a climb that keeps stalling; gusts weaken on replays. Ends with the pumpkin rolled onto the hayride wagon and carved. Medal: Gold ≤ 240 s with ≤ 2 splashes and tumbles, Silver ≤ 390 s with ≤ 8. Bot times: pair 178 s, noisy pairs about 5 min; idle and solo never clear.
- **13-2 Graveyard Bone Hunt** (team, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`): Dig up Mr. Bones' lost bones; each room's bones make him a better helper: stepstool, ladder, bridge (Gavyn's pick). Action "dig": on a mound to dig (3 digs small, 6 big), at a big bone's end to grab it, on a chalk X to call Mr. Bones there; hold it at the crank or a bell rope. A small bone rides over your head; a big bone needs both players, one per end (a jump is shared; it clatters down if they pull apart, more than 70 px in height, or cross over). He won't move while someone stands on or climbs him ("Hop off me first!"). Room 1 Front Gate: a tipping tombstone seesaw for the leg bone on the crypt roof (Gavyn, 2026-11-15, replacing a head boost): one stands on the low end, the other jumps onto the high end and flings them up; it tips back 0.8 s after nobody is on the high end, and partners pass through each other in that room so a head boost can't skip it; carry the backbone together, exit via the stool. Room 2 Crooked Crypts: stool plus a head for the hip bone; one holds the creaky crank while the other runs through the gate. Room 3 Pumpkin Plot: climb him as a ladder up the old tree; the giant ribcage only counts digs made by both within 0.5 s (5 pairs). Room 4 Old Bell Tower: bridge the grave, stack on the stool for the arm, climb the ladder and both pull the bell ropes for 1.3 s; BONG, a top hat flies down and he dances. An open grave has a springy mattress: "Boing!", back to your own last jack-o'-lantern; hold ▼ 1.2 s to hop back there. Gold help: after 30 s stuck a gold arrow and a gold shovel; gold glue after 3 clatters; the chalk X glows gold after 14 s. Medal: Gold ≤ 5:00 with ≤ 3 boings and clatters, Silver ≤ 8:00 with ≤ 9. The computer partner first helps with whatever job the person has started (other end of a bone, crank or runner, other bell rope), then follows `def.plan`. Bot times: pair 86 s, noisy about 105-110 s, scripted people 99-155 s.
- **13-3 Haunted Mansion** (team, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`), Gavyn's own idea: a flashlight reveals ghost platforms, the other player can only move while the light is off them, portraits swap players, and it ends with a ghost race. Each room has a HOLDER and a MOVER; jobs swap every room. The holder's action ("light") toggles the flashlight; while it is on, ▲▼ tilt the beam (so the holder can't jump) and the holder has careful feet: they teeter at a ledge and never walk off, and aiming down near an edge leans the lamp over it to light what's below. Turn the light off to hop down. Sleepy ghost platforms (map `g`, dotted outlines) wake minty when lit and hold the MOVER only, for 3 s after the beam leaves (6 s with gold help); the holder drops through them ("Tee-hee! Not you!"). The beam stops at stone, ghosts, the ghost book and the mover. While the beam touches the mover they freeze ("Eep!": no steering, gravity still on, free 0.25 s after). The mover's action ("swap") at a haunted portrait (map `p`) swaps Pip's and Bo's places in purple smoke; anywhere else it calls "Light, please!". Candles are map `d`; the mode runs its own door. Rooms: Foyer, Library (a floating ghost book lifts the mover while awake), Portrait Gallery (two portraits leapfrog the light up three tiers), Attic Race: Bertie the friendly sheet ghost flies a fixed route for 40 s and both must reach the attic door first; a loss gives a gold head start (+5 s per miss, Bertie slower from the 2nd miss). Flap the bat points the way after 22 s stuck. Falls ("Boo-hoo!") return you to the last stone safe spot. Gold help: wide-awake ghosts after 2 falls or 55 s stuck, a narrower beam after 5 Eeps. Results: Eeps per player, the race, medal (Gold ≤ 4:30 with Eeps + falls ≤ 12, Silver ≤ 8:00 with ≤ 30), portrait swaps and ghosts woken. Bot times: pair 69 s, noisy 79-149 s; idle and solo never clear.
- **13-4 Broomstick Race** (versus, best of 5, `ownStep`): Fly witch brooms through rings, grab speed stars, dodge friendly bats (Gavyn's pick). Brooms fly right on their own over a sleepy town; ▲▼ climb and dive, ▶ fast, ◀ slow (faster means slower climbs). A fresh course of about 9600 px every round. Clean ring passes add speed, and rings in a row build a burst (up to +260); clipping a rim is a "Clink!" slow-down. Speed stars sit off the racing line; hold one and tap the action ("zoom") for +300 for 1.15 s, which also bowls bats over. Bumping a bat is a "Squeak!" spin at half speed for a moment; roofs and treetops bounce you up ("Boing!"); brooms bump a little. First through the giant jack-o'-lantern gate wins (crossings within 0.02 s are a dead heat and replay; tiebreak most clean rings; a 75 s cap goes to whoever is farther). Round 1 is Moonlit Dash, then a shuffle of Windy Night, Foggy Hollow (only bats' eyes glow), Ring Trains and Bat Swarm; sudden death is a short Photo Finish. Gold catch-up: a tailwind (up to +55) for a rider more than 260 px behind, and a Gold star (+400 for 1.6 s) at round start for a player 2+ rounds down. Brain: Easy reacts slowly, misses bats and zooms late; Normal reacts in about 0.3 s; Hard plans 600 px ahead and dodges where bats will be. Rounds about 21-26 s, matches about 1.5-2.5 min. A kid-like script beats Easy about 6 of 10 matches and rarely beats Normal; a sharp adult script beats Normal 7 of 10.
- Each level was built, then playtested by a separate reviewer (spec, cheese attempts with a single scripted player, softlocks, keyboard / stubbed controller / touch at 1280x800, 844x390 and 390x844, screenshots), then fixed and re-tested. Medal limits come from bot times and may need tuning after real playtests.
- Code: `js/levels/world13/<slug>.js` and `js/cpu/levels/world13/<slug>.js` (pumpkin-patch-roll, graveyard-bone-hunt, haunted-mansion, broomstick-race); defs between BEGIN/END lines in the World 13 block of `level-list.js`. World-page previews are in `js/previews/world13.js` (one BEGIN/END section per level, helpers prefixed `pw13`).

### World 14: Dino Valley (mixed)
Prehistoric dinosaurs, sunny and friendly: a jungle valley with a sleepy smoking volcano far away. Every dinosaur is a helper or a pal (Mama Dino, Terry and Petey the pterodactyls, Petra, Gerty the brontosaurus, Zippy and Noodle the raptors). Nothing is scary and nobody gets hurt (drops are "BOING!", "Bloop!", "Plop!"). Three team levels then a versus finale (Gavyn picked 4 of 7 ideas, Nov 2026; he skipped Long-Neck Lift, T. rex Tag Team and Stone Wheel Roll).
- **14-1 Egg Rescue** (team, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`): Carry a giant wobbly egg home on a leaf stretcher, one at each end (Gavyn's pick). The leaf only stretches 110-150 px, so nobody walks off alone. The egg rolls to the lower end. The action (lift) raises your end 24 px and ▼ carries it low by 16. Prompts ask for whichever mix levels the leaf: one job on gentle hills, both on steep ones. ▲ gets you ready; when BOTH are ready you make a together hop over logs, a sleeping dino's tail, creeks, stepping stones and ledges ("Together!"). A lone tap becomes a small solo hop after a short grace and jolts the egg. Each hop ends with a 0.42 s rest, so hopping everywhere is no faster than walking. A drop is "BOING! Dino eggs are tough!" or "SPLOOSH! Dino eggs float!", and Terry the pterodactyl flies the egg back to the last nest checkpoint. 4 rooms, with the ends swapping each room (Pip leads rooms 1 and 3): Fern Path (logs, hills, a creek, a sleeping dino's tail), River Stepping Stones (stones bob and dip when stood on), Geyser Field (vents bubble, then PUFF: a carrier floats, the egg pops up; two in a row, then Old Steamy), Mama's Nest (ledges and a saggy vine bridge; the egg hatches into a baby dino and Mama says "Thank you, Pip and Bo!"). The front's panel shows what's next; the back's watches the egg on a bar. A 260 s hatch clock makes the egg wiggle and crack more; it never fails you. Gold help: Gold vines (longer hop grace) after 3 solo hops in a room; Sticky / Super sticky leaf after 2 / 4 drops in a room that both players took part in; Sleepy geysers after 2 vent drops; Steady stones after 2 river drops. Medal: Gold ≤ 260 s (home before the hatch clock) with ≤ 2 drops, Silver ≤ 480 s with ≤ 7. Results: Drops, Together hops, Hatch clock (On time! / Just in time!), Medal. Brain: levels its end using the partner's real hands, follows or leads at leaf length, stops for a wobbling egg and for vents about to puff, holds ▲ at obstacles and waits. It joins a partner's ▲ at an obstacle, elsewhere only a deliberate hold. Once ready it keeps holding, so it never makes a solo hop. Bot times: pair 163 s, noisy 210-239 s, kid + computer about 200 s (0 drops), 2-player kid pairs 254-298 s (Silver). Idle, solo, one-key and masher partners never clear.
- **14-2 Pterodactyl Express** (team, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Route'`): One player flaps a pterodactyl on the beat to stay in the air; the other hangs from its feet, grabbing fruit and dropping rocks on switches below (Gavyn's pick). Petra the friendly pterodactyl carries both across Dino Valley (side view, scrolling); the jobs swap at the SWAP perch after each route (`m.flyer` per route def). The flyer watches a wing meter that rises by itself: a flap (action or ▲) at the gold TOP is PERFECT (big lift), just before it is "good" (small lift), early is a weak flop that starts the wings again, and a missed TOP droops them, so mashing sinks. The flyer sets the rhythm (each press restarts the wingbeat); ◀ ▶ slow and fast, ▼ dives. With no flaps she sinks gently; feet or belly on the ground is a soft "Plop! Boing!" landing, and only a TOP flap takes off again. The hanger swings on a pendulum (◀ ▶ pump it; her speeding up and slowing down swings it too). The action grabs fruit into a leaf basket (up to 3) or a rock from a pillar, and drops it along a dotted line into a hungry nest or onto a stone switch; fruit carried right into a nest feeds it too. A log gate opens only when all its switches are down; a shut gate is a soft BONK that sends Petra back to the rocks (`gate.backTo`). A canyon roof is a soft "Bonk!". Routes: Morning Delivery (learn the beat, fruit, nests, one rock gate), Canyon Gates (low roofs, a 1-switch and a 2-switch gate), Windy Cliffs (an updraft to ride, a timed downdraft, a warned headwind that also swings the hanger), Volcano Smoke Run (vents puff sleepy smoke clouds at lane heights; touching one is an "ACHOO!" slow-down; two gates, then the Big Nest). 20 babies in all. Courses are token lists (`pteroCompile`; the map is only the spawns); `MODES.ptero.needs(m)` gives prompts and brains the next task, the height wanted and the safe band. Gold help (announced, only for tries both players took part in): Gold wings, then Super gold wings, after 2 and 4 landings in a route; Gold switch, then Magnet rocks, after missed tries at a gate; Gold nests after 3 missed fruit drops; Grabby net (grab reach ×1.45, a gold ring) after 2 fruit or rocks go by close but ungrabbed; Calm breeze on Windy Cliffs after 2 landings; Gold breeze (smaller puffs) after 3 sneezes. Results: babies fed (x of 20), PERFECT flaps per player, bumps (landings + bonks + sneezes), medal, quickest route, gold help used. Medal: Gold ≤ 300 s with ≥ 75% fed, Silver ≤ 450 s with ≥ 40% (`PTERO_MEDAL`). Touch labels per route: flyer 'flap', hanger 'grab'. Brain: as flyer it picks the hanger's height (higher before downdrafts, over or under puffs); with a person hanging it centres its flap bob on the next fruit or rock and slows down for them. It flaps PERFECT when the roof leaves room, uses small flaps under low roofs, and waits out headwinds. As hanger it swings to meet fruit and rocks and lets go when the drop line ends on the target. It says short bubbles ("Grab the rock!", "Lower, please!", "Flap at the gold top!"). Bot times: pair about 270 s (Gold, 18-19 fed), noisy 5.3-8 min; scripted person + computer: decent 4-5.3 min, kid 5-7.5 min; idle, one-key, masher and fixed-rhythm players never clear. Sounds `Sfx.extra.ptero*`; preview `pw14p` in js/previews/world14.js.
- **14-3 Tar Pit Pull** (team, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`): Sticky tar slows you down and traps you; one swings across on vines and pulls the other out, and you take turns (Gavyn's pick). Side view of a sunny prehistoric swamp, 4 rooms: Sticky Steps (two tied vines and a sticky puddle), Vine Swing Gully (a hook-and-throw vine, then a vine that needs a "Push!"), Sinking Logs (logs sink slowly under one and fast under two, go "Glug!" and bob back up, lifting out anyone stuck above; bubble vents pop stuck friends up) and Gerty's Tail (a friendly brontosaurus lies down as a bridge only while someone holds the action at her leaf horn, then one last vine). Tar (map `t`): you wade slowly and are Stuck! after 0.65 s; wiggling (◀ ▶ in turn) slows the sinking. Fully under is a "Bloop!" back to your last safe rock, counted and never a fail. A fresh hop out of the goo is weak and stickier each time, and too low to climb a bank. A puddle holds you until you hop out at its edge, wiggle 5 times or wait 6 s; it always pops you onto the bank on the side you walked in. Vines hang out of reach: jump into the handle to grab, ◀ ▶ pump, jump or the action lets go (a press in the first 0.3 s is ignored). Tied vines re-tie only while nobody has crossed, and you can't grab a vine while your partner is a bank behind. Pass vines must be hooked: hold the action at a hook stump (`h`) to draw the vine in for your partner (the hooker can't ride it), and let go of a fully drawn vine to throw it back across. Lasso: a free player in reach of a partner in the tar presses the action, then walks away or keeps holding; the stuck one can press toward the puller to help, and SHLORP lands them on the puller's side. Every crossing takes a swinger and a helper or puller; both must stand in the cave (`E`). Other map chars: `z` bubble vent, `n` leaf horn. Room def: title, tip, act {label} (pull / hook / pull / call, shown on the touch buttons and legend), style, cross [{kind tied|push|pass|gerty, lead}], logs [{c,w}], deco. Gold help, always announced in a gold pill and HUD chips: Slow tar (2 bloops in a room), Long lasso (2 "Too far!"), Gold vines (2 falls off a vine; they drop you on the far bank and only let go over solid ground), Patient Gerty (2 slides off her), all of it after 55 s with no crossing; Petey the pterodactyl brings a hint after 24 s (1-player-aware). Medal by time and times stuck: Gold ≤ 4:30 and ≤ 12 stuck, Silver ≤ 8:00 and ≤ 28. Results: Times stuck (Pip-Bo), Shlorps!, medal line, swings/bloops/bubble pops. Brain: does its crossing job, swaps when the person does the other job (or offers after 11 s), walks to the edge before wading in, and swings over to pull from the far side when the person hops in first. Bot times: pair 56 s, noisy about 80 s, scripted people 56-179 s; idle, solo, a lone player, single-key and mashers never clear. QA'd by a second agent (puddle traps, double-tap vine drops and two computer stalls fixed).
- **14-4 Raptor Race** (versus, best of 5, `ownStep`): Ride friendly feathered raptors through a jungle, jumping logs and grabbing speed eggs (Gavyn's pick). Side-on, one shared course and camera; Pip rides Zippy (yellow and orange), Bo rides Noodle (lilac and blue). Raptors run by themselves: ▶ fast, ◀ slow, jump hops logs, rocks, compies, puddles and treetop gaps (hold for a full hop), ▼ ducks branches, vines and roots. A raptor drifts a little slower through its own hops and needs a 0.1 s push-off step after landing, so mashing jump is slower than hopping just the logs. 3 forks per course: jump (or stick up) by the sign for the Treetops (×1.08, but gaps: a miss is a "Whoops!" drop onto the bridge), hold ▼ for the Muddy Creek (mud, most eggs), or stay on the Jungle Trail rope bridge. Speed eggs are per rider (each rider takes each egg once), one carried at a time; dash = the action (touch label 'dash'), about 1.1 s: it smashes logs, skims mud and ignores branches. Stumbles are cartoon only ("Oof! Log!", "Bonk! Duck!", "Peep! Sorry!", "Boop! Big trike!"); raptors bump a little, and jumping over the other is a "Leapfrog!". First nose through the brachiosaurus arch wins, and the margin is shown. Round 1 Jungle Dash, then a shuffle of Trike Stampede (herds cross on a rhythm: wait or dash through a gap), Bouncy Ferns, Geyser Gully (geysers at least 460 px apart) and Firefly Night. A dead heat (≤0.005 s) is replayed; a 2nd tie goes to most hops, then fewest stumbles, then most eggs, else a one-fork Sudden Sprint; 75 s bell. Gold help, always announced: Gold Zoomies (up to +50 for a raptor 300+ px behind) and a Gold Egg (a bigger dash) at the start for a player 2+ rounds behind. Key chips in each player's own device keys in rounds 1-2, plus reminders after the first log or branch stumble, the first fork and an egg held 3.5 s, never for the computer. Results: Hops, Egg dashes, Stumbles, Best secs, plus the closest finish, the fastest time and favourite paths. `MODES.raptor.testTwist` forces a twist for tests; `MODES.raptor.test` exposes course helpers to the brain. Brain: Easy misses things, has sloppy timing (±52 px), holds ▶ 40% of the time and picks paths at random; Hard plans hops from the hop physics, times herds and geysers, saves its egg for mud. Bot times: rounds 25-33 s, matches about 1.5-3 min; Hard beats Easy 5/5 from both sides, normal vs normal 62-60 rounds over 30 seeds; a kid-like script beats Easy 7 of 8 from either side (a weaker kid 6 of 8) but rarely beats Normal, and mashing jump loses to Normal.
- Each level was built, then break-tested by a separate reviewer (scripted people who take the computer's job, wait, wander, mash or hold one key; screenshots at 1280x800, 844x390 and 390x844; stubbed controller and touch), then fixed and re-tested. Medal limits are tuned on bots and scripted players only, so real playtests may move them.
- Code: `js/levels/world14/<slug>.js` and `js/cpu/levels/world14/<slug>.js` (egg-rescue, pterodactyl-express, tar-pit-pull, raptor-race); defs between BEGIN/END lines in the World 14 block of `level-list.js`. World-page previews are in `js/previews/world14.js` (one BEGIN/END section per level, helpers prefixed `pw14e`, `pw14p`, `pw14t`, `pw14r`).

### World 15: Orbit Outpost (mixed)
Space, friendly and floaty: a deep blue-violet starry sky, a big ringed planet, grey moon rock with lilac dust. Helpers are friendly robots (Bleep the tow robot, Blip the station bot, Tow-Bot) and sleepy moon critters. Nothing is scary and nobody gets hurt (fails are "Whoopsie!", "Boing!", a tow back). Three team levels then a versus finale. Gavyn picked 4 of 7 ideas and their order (Nov 2026; he skipped Airlock Escape, Rocket Repair Rush and Zero-G Space Ball). The world name is Claude's pick.
- **15-1 Tethered Spacewalk** (team, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`): Pip and Bo float outside the space station, tied by a tether (Gavyn's pick). Zero gravity: nobody walks or jumps. Hold the action (grab) next to a handhold (station rails, asteroid grips, satellite arms) to hold on; that player is the anchor.
