The anchor spins the floater with ◀ ▶ (with momentum) and reels the tether with ▲ ▼ (40-150 px). The floater only has a tiny suit puff (a 0.6 s tank that refills fast only while holding on), so swinging is how you travel. Leapfrog: the floater holds grab as it passes the next handhold and holds on, then the one further back lets go and becomes the floater ('Let go of F!'). A handle you just let go of can't be grabbed straight back. A gold dashed ring shows the next handhold or panel. Broken solar panels: the floater holds grab beside one and keeps still (no puffing) while the partner holds on; after a moment it bolts (about 0.85 s), sparks, then glows with a smiley. Every panel fixed opens the airlock, and both float in to end the room. Rooms: Station Hull (2 panels), Asteroid Belt (rocks drift up and down carrying grips, 3 panels), Spinning Satellite (moving arm handholds and panels on its wings, 3), Comet Run Home (rocks, a smaller spinner and 4 panels, back to the left airlock). Nothing hurts: rocks, satellite cores and the hull bounce you ('Boing!', counted as bonks when fast). If both let go, Bleep the tow robot tows you back to the last station rail after 3.2 s, or right away if you hold ▼ for 1 s. Gold help (announced): Gold grip after 2 tows or 45 s with no progress in a room (bigger grab and fix reach, quicker bolting, a stronger hand brake on a panel), Super gold grip after 4 tows or another 45 s (also a longer tether). Medal: Gold ≤ 3:30 with bonks + tows ≤ 6, Silver ≤ 6:40 with ≤ 15. Results: panels fixed per player, bonks, tows, medal, handles grabbed. Job cards in the bottom corners and the prompts follow each player's device; in 1-player mode the computer's card says '(computer)' with no keys, and the cards fade while someone floats behind them. Bot times: computer pair 2:15 (Gold), noisy pairs 2:40-6:10, a scripted sloppy kid with the computer 5:30-8:30, a person who never spins about 6:30. An idle partner, a solo player, a masher or a grab-held-all-level partner never fix a panel.
- **15-2 Gravity Flip Station** (team, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`). A low-gravity station where your switches flip your PARTNER's gravity (Gavyn's pick and pitch). Pip's orange switches (`x`) flip Bo and Bo's blue switches (`y`) flip Pip. You press "flip" while standing at your own switch, on the floor or on the ceiling when flipped. Nobody can flip themselves and there are no free flips.
  - **Moving:** a flipped player walks the ceiling upside down. ◀ ▶ work as usual and jump pushes off the ceiling. Gravity is g 1150 and jump 560 (about 136 px high, about 1 s). Partners float through each other. Buttons `m` open fields `f`, and a field never closes on anyone.
  - **Rooms:** Docking Bay: go over the wall on the ceiling. Hydro Garden: a gap into space, plus a ceiling button and a floor button. Star Lab: jump off the ceiling before the goo and get flipped mid-air onto the island. Command Deck: run, jump and get flipped over the floor goo, then exit through the ceiling shuttle hatch. Each room needs its keycard, and the exit needs both players.
  - **Whoopsies:** goo or space sends you back to your own beacon ("Whoopsie!"). A beacon lights only from its own floor, or from the ceiling right above it with no hull in between. Holding ▼ for 1 s also goes back.
  - **Hints:** each room has plan chips that follow each player's device. A jumper on a jump step gets a "Wait here: X flips you" chip when a plain flip works, and a "No take-off spot: hold ▼ to go back" chip when it doesn't. The switch prompt shows only when flipping is your job. After 30 s, a player away from their beacon also sees "Stuck?". Blip points the way after 24 s on a step.
  - **Gold float:** turns on after 3 whoopsies in a room or 70 s on one step. It widens the mid-air flip window from about 0.3 s to about 0.55 s.
  - **Scoring:** 12 optional stars. Gold is ≤ 4:00 with ≤ 4 whoopsies; Silver is ≤ 7:00 with ≤ 12. The results screen shows Whoopsies, Stars, flips and ceiling time.
  - **Brain** (`gravflip`): plays in the mirrored view, times flips by predicting the physics and does the run-up jumps. It says "Not yet! Flip me when I jump!" when flipped early, holds ▼ when it has no way forward, and splits the exit with its partner.
  - **Times:** pair 61 s, noisy 79-85 s, 1-player scripted people 64-129 s. Idle and solo never clear. A person who mashes flip all the time can't pass Star Lab, by design.
- **15-3 Moon Buggy Crew** (team, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`): One drives a bouncy moon buggy over craters, the other works the grabber arm to collect moon rocks and clear boulders (Gavyn's pitch). Side view in low gravity: springy wheels, a starry sky, a ringed planet and the Earth. Both ride one two-seat buggy, and the jobs swap at an arch every room.
  - **The driver:** ◀ ▶ give gas, brake and reverse. With nothing pressed the buggy holds still, even on a slope. ▲ hops straight up. In the air ◀ ▶ tilt the buggy (a gentle gyro helps). The action honks: it wakes a sleepy critter napping on the track and opens the rocket hatch. Holding ▼ calls Tow-Bot back to the last flag.
  - **The arm worker:** moves the claw with ◀ ▶ ▲ ▼ or the stick, and presses the action to grab and to let go. Holding the action on a moon rock grabs it too. A dotted gold line shows the claw its next job.
  - **Boulders:** a boulder on the track stops the buggy ("Bonk!"). It can't be hopped over: grab it, swing it back behind the buggy and let go.
  - **Craters:** a fill crater is 340 px, wider than any hop or moss bounce can carry the buggy. It needs 3 boulders from the rock pile beside it. Hop craters (100-115 px) always need a hop. Jump ramps need full speed and a hop at the lip.
  - **Rooms:** Crater Flats; Boulder Alley (a fill crater); Dark Side (headlight and claw lamp, bouncy moss, a jump ramp); Rocket Pad (moss, a fill crater and a ramp). At the rocket the driver parks in the box and honks the hatch open, and the claw lifts the rock crate from the hopper into the hatch. Then a tractor beam, 3-2-1, blast off home. There are 33 moon rocks.
  - **Soft fails:** falling into a crater or tipping over is a "Whoopsie!". A crater still being filled counts as soon as the buggy dips into it. Tow-Bot carries the buggy back to the last flag, or sets it right on the spot on safe flat ground. Rocks and cleared boulders stay done.
  - **The crate:** a crate dropped over the buggy lands back in the hopper. One that ends up out of the claw's reach is put back by Tow-Bot.
  - **Stuck:** after 25 s with no headway, the driver's prompt says to hold ▼ for Tow-Bot.
  - **Gold help (announced):**
    - Gold springs after 2 misses at a hop crater, and a gold bridge after 3.
    - A gold boost at a ramp after 2 misses, and a bridge after 4. Only tries the driver took part in count.
    - A gold magnet claw after 20 s stuck at a boulder while the arm is trying.
    - Tow-Bot drops boulders into a crater the crew has been filling for 35 s.
    - A gold gyro after 3 tip-overs in a room.
    - A gold parking box after 15 s at the pad. It reaches further toward the rocket than back, so the hatch stays in reach.
    - A hatch magnet after 20 s parked.
  - **HUD:** two job panels with key chips for each player's device, and prompts over each head. In 1-player mode the computer's seat says "(computer)" and shows no keys, and the person's prompt sits above the computer's speech bubble. The touch and legend action labels switch between honk and grab for each role.
  - **Results and medal:** moon rocks, rocks grabbed per player, and Whoopsies. Gold needs 80% of the rocks within 4:30; Silver needs 50% within 7:00.
  - **Fairness:** one player alone, or with an idle partner, never gets past the first boulder. A random button-masher on the claw takes 3-6 minutes for rooms 1 and 3 and never clears rooms 2 and 4.
  - **Bot times:** computer pair 2:20 (25 of 33 rocks, Silver); noisy pairs 3:00-3:20; scripted people who only follow the prompts 2:30-5:30.
- **15-4 Planet Hop Race** (versus, best of 5, `ownStep`): Race across tiny round planets that each have their own gravity, jumping from one to the next (Gavyn's pick). Each round builds a new course of about 20 planets (27 in Tiny Moons, 8 in the sudden-death Sudden Sprint). Each planet has a gravity well, shown as a faint dotted ring that turns the runner's colour while it pulls them. In flight, the planet whose well you are deepest in pulls you. On a planet, ◀ ▶ run round it relative to your own up (▶ always goes clockwise, so it still feels right upside down), and ▲ jumps straight out, keeping your run speed. In the air, ◀ ▶ nudge you a little the way they point on the screen and ▼ dives (stronger pull, and no bounce on jelly). Action "boost" is one jet puff per jump, refilled when you land. It pushes the way you hold on screen, or forward and up with nothing held, and on the ground it is a jet hop. (The reviewer changed the air controls from "round the planet" to screen directions: "hold ▶ toward the next planet + boost" saved a short jump 14% of the time before, 45% after.) A star gives one extra puff (hold up to 3). Failures are soft. Drift outside every well for 1 s, fly for 4.5 s, or go 700 px from every planet, and Tow-bot, a friendly robot, carries you back to the top of the last planet you stood on ("Whoopsie!"). Both runners start on the same spot as see-through ghosts until they separate. After that they bump ("Bump!"), and a puffing runner bonks the other away ("BONK!", a short dizzy). First to touch the rocket on the checkered finish planet wins the round, and the rocket takes off with the winner in its window. Both touching it on the same tick is a dead heat, replayed. Round 1 is Hop Along. Then 4 shuffled twists come from Tiny Moons, Jelly Planets (bouncy; hold ▼ to stick), Spinny Planets, Moon Orbits (moving moons), Comet Shower (friendly comets knock you, "Boing!") and Wormhole (jump straight up from the top of one planet into a swirl that skips 3). The tiebreak is most stars, then the Sudden Sprint. A safety bell at 75 s gives the round to whoever is farther along ("Neck and neck at the bell!" if level). Gold help, announced on the round card and at GO: whoever trails in rounds gets Gold jets (2 puffs per jump, ×1.15 push), and 2+ rounds behind adds Gold boots (×1.08 run). Anyone 1500 px behind for 2.5 s gets a gold Comet lift to two planets behind the leader. Camera: one shared pan-and-zoom camera, not a split screen. It zooms 0.5-1 (0.58-1.12 on phones) to fit both runners plus the leader's next planet. If they can't both fit, it stays on the leader and an edge marker shows the other runner (the Comet lift keeps that short). Zoomed out, runners are drawn up to 1.7x bigger with a P/B pin. Before GO the runners stand left of centre so the banner doesn't cover them. HUD: puff panels in the bottom corners (gold edge with Gold jets), a course map at the bottom centre, a twist pill, and a round card with key chips in each person's own device's words before rounds 1-2. After a person's 1st and 3rd tow there is a tip in their own keys, never for the computer. Results: Planets hopped, Jet puffs, Stars, Tow-bot rides, plus the fastest race with its course name. Brain: a launch table for every planet, worked out once per course from the same flight rules, plus in-air fixes (8-way puffs, dive, nudges). Easy has wobbly timing and short pauses, Hard is near perfect. Bot results: computer rounds about 19-26 s, matches 80-140 s. Hard beat Easy 16/16 from both sides and Normal 8/8; Normal beat Easy 8/8. Normal vs Normal was 13-11 matches (53-52 rounds) over 24 seeds. A kid-like script beats Easy about 7 in 8 and takes rounds but not matches off Normal. Mashers, one-key holders and idle players lose every match. Tested on keyboard, a stubbed controller and touch at 1280x800, 844x390 and 390x844.
- Each level was built, then break-tested by a separate reviewer (cheese with one scripted player, idle and masher partners, softlocks, scripted 1-player "people" who take the computer's job, wait, wander or go the wrong way; keyboard, stubbed controller and touch at 1280x800, 844x390 and 390x844), then fixed and re-tested. Medal limits are tuned on bots and scripted players only, so real playtests may move them.
- Code: `js/levels/world15/<slug>.js` and `js/cpu/levels/world15/<slug>.js` (tethered-spacewalk, gravity-flip-station, moon-buggy-crew, planet-hop-race); defs between BEGIN/END lines in the World 15 block of `level-list.js`. World-page previews are in `js/previews/world15.js` (one BEGIN/END section per level, helpers prefixed `pw15`).

### World 16: Sugar Rush Factory (mixed)
Candy land and a candy factory, sweet and silly: a pink and peach sky with cotton candy clouds, chocolate ground with frosting on top, lollipops everywhere. Helpers are friendly (Boss Bonbon the factory boss, gummy bears on inner tubes). Nothing is scary and nobody gets hurt (fails are "Blech!", "Bloop!", "Bonk!", "Boing!"). Three team levels then a versus finale. Gavyn picked 4 of 9 ideas and their order (Nov 2026; he skipped Taffy Stretch Bridge, Cotton Candy Clouds, Gingerbread House Rush, Cake Decorating Duel and Candy Cane Sled Race). The world name is Claude's pick.
- **16-1 Conveyor Belt Chaos** (team, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`): one works the levers that change belt speed and direction, the other rides the belts and sorts candy into the right bins (Gavyn's pitch). Side view of a pink candy factory, one screen per room, and the jobs swap every room (Pip sorts rooms 1 and 3).
  - **Operator:** walks the catwalk along the top (no jumping up there) between lever stations. At a belt lever, the action flips the direction and ▲ ▼ go stop / slow / fast. At the lift lever, ▲ ▼ move it and the action toggles. At the trapdoor lever, the action or ▼ opens it, and holding keeps it open. The operator never touches candy.
  - **Sorter:** a platformer on the floor. Belts are one-way and carry you. The action grabs a candy in reach (it prefers kinds the order still needs), and the action again tosses it in an aimed arc into the bin in front; ▼ + action just drops it. Candy on a fast belt is "Too fast!".
  - **Candy:** chutes only drop candy while their belt runs, so an idle operator means no candy. Every kind has a colour and a shape: lollipop, gumdrop, chocolate bar, toffee, and the gift (a wrapped toffee).
  - **Soft misses:**
    - Candy off a belt end into a grate or the recycler comes back later ("Recycled!").
    - A wrong bin is "Blech! Wrong flavour!" and the candy pops back out.
    - Floor candy poofs back after 20 s.
    - An unwrapped toffee anywhere but on the wrapper's belt goes "Back to the wrapper!". A toffee in the gift box is a lesson, not a Blech.
    - Useless candy that fills the screen poofs back so the chutes never stall.
  - **Rooms:**
    1. Lollipop Line: one belt, 2 bins.
    2. Gumdrop Gallery: stacked belts, and a lift up to the high gumdrop shelf.
    3. Wrap & Ribbon: the wrapper only wraps on slow, and a trapdoor drops candy to the low belt.
    4. Rush Hour: everything, with two chutes, then Boss Bonbon's confetti finale.
  - **HUD and prompts:** the order board is at the top centre. Job cards sit in the bottom corners; they and the prompts follow each player's device, and the computer's card says "(computer)". Operator prompts cover walking to the right lever, start, slow, flip (toward the wrapper, or away from a wall pile), get ready at the trapdoor, open it, and lift up/down. Sorter prompts cover grab, toss, which way to the bin, the lift, hopping down, and dropping an unwrapped toffee. The action labels read grab / lever per role.
  - **Gold help (always announced):**
    - Gold grabber after 4 useful candies recycled in a room.
    - Gold labels after 2 Blechs.
    - Gold candy after 30 s without a sort.
    - Gold wrapper after 3 unwrapped rush-throughs.
    - Gold trapdoor (wider, open 1.6 s) after 3 good candies ride past the trapdoor.
  - **Medal and results:** Gold ≤ 4:00 with ≤ 3 Blechs, Silver ≤ 7:00 with ≤ 8. Results show candy sorted per player, Blechs, Recycled and lever pulls.
  - **Brain** (`conveyor`): the operator works out the wanted direction and speed for each belt, the lift and the trapdoor, and walks to the most urgent lever. The sorter uses the lift, drops through belts and jump-grabs.
  - **Bot times:** computer pair about 2:40. Noisy pairs 3:20-4:00. Prompt-following scripted kids with the computer 2:35-3:40.
  - **Fairness:** idle, solo and lazy (one press per room) partners never get past room 2. A lever-masher with a computer sorter can clear room 1 (the warm-up).
- **16-2 Gumball Machine Puzzle** (team, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Machine'`): One turns the crank to drop gumballs, the other flips levers so each colour rolls into its matching cup (Gavyn's pick). A giant glass gumball machine fills the screen like a marble run seen from the front. The cranker stays at the crank by the globe; a window shows the next 3 gumballs, each colour with its own pattern for colour-blind players (red stripes, blue dots, yellow star, green swirl, purple heart, gold shine). ◀ ▶ slide the nozzle to another chute, ▼ swaps the next two, ▲ calls out the next colour, and the action (crank) drops one; there is a short rest between cranks and at most 2 gumballs on the board. The gate keeper runs and hops one-way wafer ledges on both sides (rows 8, 11, 14 and the floor) and presses the action (flip) at a lever to work the board device with the same picture (star, heart, moon, leaf); away from a lever it shouts "Ready!". Devices: flap gates, a seesaw that also tips over by itself after every gumball (no flip while a gumball is on it), a spinner wheel, a conveyor, timed trapdoors (a press opens one for 3 s, a press while open shuts it, except in its first 0.6 s) and a pinball flipper (a gumball waits 2.4 s in its cradle: flick it into the far cup). Gumballs follow fixed candy tracks with real falls, pegs and mint marshmallow bumpers, so one always ends in a cup: the right one is "Yum!", a wrong one is "Bloop! Wrong cup!" and the gumball pops back into the globe. Fill every cup to clear a machine. 4 machines, jobs swap every machine (Pip cranks 1 and 3): Sweet Start (1 chute, 2 flap gates, 3 cups, fixed teaching order), Switch Tracks (2 chutes, seesaw, funnel gate, 4 cups), Spinner Swirl (3 chutes, spinner, two trapdoors), Jackpot Machine (3 chutes, gate, seesaw, conveyor, flipper, 5 cups, then the golden JACKPOT gumball: flick it into the gold star and 150 gumballs rain out). Every machine has colours that only reach their cup after a lever flip, so one player alone, or with an idle partner, never clears one. Prompts in each player's own keys: always in Machine 1; later after 8 s of waiting, a bloop of that colour, or with the gold path, and once on they stay on 8 s. Job cards in the top corners; in 1-player mode the computer's card says "(computer)" with no keys. Gold help (announced): Gold path (a dotted line where each gumball will roll, with a tick or cross on its cup) after 2 bloops in a machine or 45 s with nothing sorted; Gold slow-mo (65%) after 4 bloops. Medal: Gold ≤ 4:00 with ≤ 3 bloops, Silver ≤ 7:00 with ≤ 10. Results: Right first time (x of 32), Bloops, Cranks and Lever flips per player, plus Jackpot, Flicks, Swaps and Gold help. Brain (`gumball`) reads `MODES.gumball.needs` (the same plan as the prompts): the cranker picks the cheapest chute, swaps when that saves flips, and cranks only when the way is set (and the keeper stands at a trapdoor or flipper lever); with a person keeper it says which lever and answers "Ready!". The keeper runs to the most urgent lever it can still reach in time, opens trapdoors just in time and flicks; a gumball it can't reach is let go, and then it pre-sets devices for what is left. Bot times: computer pair about 2:06 (0 bloops), noisy pairs 2:20-2:30; 1-player scripted people who follow the prompts 2:55-3:30, kids 3:00-5:15, a person who cranks blindly 2:25-3:10, mashers and wrong-lever players 3:55-5:00, players who wait, wander or hold one key for 50 s of each machine 5:20-5:45. Idle and solo partners never clear. QA'd by a second agent (fast-cranker keeper stall, trapdoors that couldn't shut, hints vanishing mid-job, gumball-looking bumpers fixed).
- **16-3 Chocolate River Raft** (team, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Stretch'`): Pip and Bo ride a round cookie raft down a chocolate river, seen from above, and the camera scrolls with them (Gavyn's pitch). The PADDLER steers with ▲ ▼, uses ▶ for faster and ◀ for slower or against the current, and presses the action for a strong paddle stroke on a short cooldown. The HOOKER moves a candy-cane hook tip round the raft (reach 140 px) and presses the action to:
  - hook candy: wrapped sweets and lollipops are worth 1, gummy bears in inner tubes 3;
  - shove marshmallow rocks, wafer logs and bonbons out of the way;
  - crack log jams (2 shoves; a tip pushed all the way ahead counts);
  - open a toffee gate by holding the action on its pull ring.
  - **Why both jobs are needed:** a jam blocks the river until the hooker shoves it. A gate's ring sits on the bank and only pulls while the paddler holds the raft nearly still beside it, against a crossflow. Each stretch ends at a pier on the top bank where the paddler must stop (a side current pushes a drifting raft onto a net past the pier). The jobs swap at every dock: Pip paddles stretches 1 and 3, Bo 2 and 4.
  - **Stretches:** Cocoa Creek (lessons, one jam), Marshmallow Rapids (fast current, a rock wall, eddies), Caramel Swirls (whirlpools with orbiting candy, 2 toffee gates, a jam), Fudge Falls (a gate, a whirlpool and a jam, then the paddler must hit the fudge ramp straight and fast; the stretch ends with a party at the candy village). There are 67 candy points in all.
  - **Soft fails:** a bump is a "Bonk!" (a hard one spills a candy to hook again). A whirlpool's middle spits the raft out ("Wheee!"). Missing the ramp is "Splat! Fudge-tastic!", and Mallow the marshmallow duck tows the raft back to the last flag. Banks are soft ("Boing!"), and paddling far upstream is gently stopped.
  - **Gold help (announced):** Gold hook after 4 near misses or 14 s at a jam; Gold paddle after 4 bonks in a stretch; Sleepy caramel after 2 spit-outs from one whirlpool; Gold ring after 18 s at a gate; Gold fudge ramp after 2 splats and Gold boost arrows after 4; Gold pier after 14 s at a dock.
  - **HUD:** job cards in the bottom corners with each player's own keys ('(computer)' and no keys in 1-player mode), and prompts over each head that read the same needs function as the brain. The touch and legend action label switches between paddle and hook for each stretch.
  - **Medal:** Gold needs at least 75% of the candy within 4:30; Silver needs at least 45% within 7:30. Results show candy, candy hooked per player, bonks, medal, plus shoves, Wheees, splats and gold help.
  - **Brain** (`raft`): does whichever job it has from MODES.raft.needs. As paddler it steers with drift prediction, stops at jams, rings and the pier, strokes up the ramp and backs off when stuck. As hooker it brakes the tip onto targets and leads drifting ones. With a person it waits and says what the other job needs.
  - **Times:** computer pair 3:36 (Gold, 63/67). Noisy pairs 4:00-4:05. Scripted 1-player people who follow the prompts 3:56-4:27 (Gold). A person who first does the other job's moves takes 4:08-4:34. Idle, solo, hold-one-key and mashing partners never clear (they stop at the first jam or the first dock). A random wanderer on the hook with the computer paddling takes about 15 minutes.
  - **Review:** break-tested by a separate reviewer (scripted people, stubbed controller and touch at 1280x800, 844x390 and 390x844).
- **16-4 Sprinkle Catch** (versus, best of 5, `ownStep`): Catch falling candy in your basket while giant gobstoppers bounce you around (Gavyn's pick). One candy-land arena, side view: a chocolate floor and three wafer platforms. Each player runs their own small platformer: run, a jump that goes lower if you let go early, and ▼ to drop through a wafer. A smiling sprinkle-shaker jar slides along a licorice rail and drops candy: gumdrops and sprinkles 1, mints 2, cupcakes 3, golden candy 5 (one per round). Falling candy passes through the wafers. It must land in the basket on your head; candy that hits your body bounces off. Rounds last 35 s and most candy points wins. The action "bump" is a short dash: bump the other player and they spill 1 candy. The bumped one gets a 2.5 s shield and has the first 1 s to scoop the candy back. Two head-on bumps bounce apart. Players squeeze past each other, so nobody can be boxed in. (The reviewer added the gentle push, the longer shield and the scoop window: a player who only chased and bumped used to beat Hard.) Gobstoppers are warned by a "!" 1.2 s ahead, then bounce around. A hit is a soft "Boing!": you are stunned and spill 2 candies (3 for the Mega one). A gobstopper whose time runs out pops into 3 candies. Spilled candy rests for 6 s and either player can scoop it. Round 1 is Sweet Start, then 4 shuffled twists from Sprinkle Storm, Jelly Floor (bounces get smaller and stop; hold ▼ to land softly), Conveyor Floor (it flips, with warning arrows), Mega Gobstopper, Cake Tiers (a three-tier cake stand and shelves) and Gumdrop Rain. A tied round is replayed. On a second tie in a row the most golden candy this match wins, then the most candy; still level, a Golden Drop sudden-death round (first golden catch wins, 25 s cap). Gold help, always announced on the round card and at GO: whoever trails in rounds gets a Gold basket (60 px wide instead of 40), and 2+ rounds behind adds a candy magnet. With 12 s left, a player 6+ points behind gets a golden candy dropped over their head ("Golden candy for Bo!"). Key chips in each person's own device keys show before rounds 1-2, plus bump and gobstopper tips, never for the computer. Results: Candy caught, Golden candy, Bumps, Spills, plus the sweetest round and a gobstopper line. Brain: plans routes between surfaces, simulates jumps with the level's own rules, picks the catch worth the most points for the time, dodges gobstopper paths 0.7 s ahead, bumps a player carrying candy, hops over incoming bumps and holds ▼ on jelly. Easy reacts in 0.6 s and dawdles; Hard reacts in 0.12 s with near-perfect aim. Bot results: matches 2-3.5 min. Hard beat Easy 12/12 from both sides. Normal vs Normal 45-45 matches over seeds 31-120. A kid-like script beats Easy 12/12 and Normal 3/12, a weaker kid beats Easy 7/12. Chase-and-bump, masher, one-key, wander, jumper and idle players lose and never softlock. Reviewed by a second agent (chase-and-bump cheese, endless jelly bouncing, overlapping tips, golden-candy double count fixed). Tested on keyboard, a stubbed controller and touch at 1280x800, 844x390 and 390x844. Sounds `Sfx.extra.sprinkle*`; preview `pw16s` in js/previews/world16.js.
- Each level was built, then break-tested by a separate reviewer (scripted 1-player people who follow only the prompts, take the computer's job, wait, wander, mash or hold one key; idle and solo partners; keyboard, a stubbed controller and touch at 1280x800, 844x390 and 390x844), then fixed and re-tested. Medal limits are tuned on bots and scripted players only, so real playtests may move them.
- Code: `js/levels/world16/<slug>.js` and `js/cpu/levels/world16/<slug>.js` (conveyor-belt-chaos, gumball-machine-puzzle, chocolate-river-raft, sprinkle-catch); defs between BEGIN/END lines in the World 16 block of `level-list.js`. World-page previews are in `js/previews/world16.js` (one BEGIN/END section per level, helpers prefixed `pw16`).

### World 17: Jungle Jamboree (mixed)
A sunny, leafy jungle: a gold-green sky through the canopy, mossy brown earth, vines, an overgrown temple, a river and a big waterfall. Helpers are friendly animals (Mango the monkey, Cappy and Coco the capybaras, Hugo the hippo, toucans and frogs). Nothing is scary and nobody gets hurt ("Splash!", "Squeeze!", "Whoopsie!"). Three team levels, then a versus finale. Gavyn picked 3 of 6 team ideas (Nov 2026; he skipped Monkey Business, Rope Bridge Builder and Firefly Night Hunt) and asked for the vine swing himself: a swing-and-let-go-for-distance level inspired by a jungle swing minigame he likes, built in Pip & Bo's own style (no copied look, names or layout) with a scrolling camera. The world name is Claude's pick.
- **17-1 Temple Doors** (team, mode `temple`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`; js/levels/world17/temple-doors.js + js/cpu/levels/world17/temple-doors.js): standing on a pressure plate lifts a stone door, and stepping off lets it slide down SLOWLY. A column of glyph lights over the doorway and a HUD door timer ("Leaf door 1.0 s") count down the time left to get through. Getting through a closing door is a "Dash!".
  - **Moving/controls:** normal platforming. Hold ▼ for 1 s to go back to your own torch (each player lights their own). The action (F or / on keyboard, X on a controller, the round touch button) is "pull" in Room 3 (it tugs the idol a tile toward you) and "shout" in the other rooms ("Go, Bo!"). The touch button's word changes with the room.
  - **Rooms:**
    - Mossy Gate: one holds the plate while the other runs through, then the far plate brings the first one across.
    - Two-Way Plates: each plate opens one door and shuts the other. The holder hops from the leaf plate to the flower plate. The runner dashes through both doors (2.8 s to close) and over a 1-tile moat in a low tunnel. A full-speed run skims the moat; stopping or walking on it still splashes.
    - Idol Hall: the moon door needs both moon plates at once, or the plate behind it. A pushable stone monkey idol can stand on a plate. The big, slow star door opens from the far-left plate or the far-right one, and the idol on the far star plate holds it open for good.
    - The Sun Door: one player on each side. Star plates open the partner's star room. Six glyph plates must be pressed in the order carved on the door, woven between both sides, and the order is shuffled each play.
  - **Soft fails:**
    - "Splash!": water sends you back to your torch in 1 s.
    - "Squeeze!": a closing door stops at anyone under it and nudges them out. A jump into it just bumps your head.
    - "Too late! Bonk!": you ran into a door that just shut.
    - "So close! Try again!": you waited right by a door that closed again.
    - "Oops! Start again!": a wrong glyph. Before any glyph is pressed, a wrong one only says "Not first!". Someone already standing on a plate when it becomes the next one presses it.
    - If you fall somewhere you can't reach your job from (the Room 2 exit pocket, for example), your chip says "Can't get there? hold ▼ to go back to your torch". The computer does this by itself.
  - **Gold help (announced):**
    - Gold sand: doors close 1.4× slower. It comes after 2 misses (bonks or missed windows) or 60 s with nobody getting through a door.
    - Super gold sand: doors close 2.2× slower. It comes after 4 misses or another 120 s.
    - Gold lily pads: after 3 splashes in a room.
    - Gold glyphs: after 2 Sun Door restarts. The next plate glows and wrong ones stop restarting.
    - Mango the monkey cheers dashes, and after 24 s with no progress he points at the next step from just above it. In 1-player mode he always points at the person's own job.
    - Hint chips show each person's job. "Stuck?" shows only when you are away from your torch.
  - **Scoring/medal:** time plus whoopsies (splashes and wrong glyphs). Gold is ≤ 2:00 with ≤ 3 whoopsies; Silver is ≤ 4:00 with ≤ 10. Results tiles: Whoopsies per player, Golden bananas (12), Dashes per player, plus a line with plates held, squeezes, idol pushes and the help used.
  - **Brain:** plays the job that templePlan(st) gives it: hold, wait, cross, via, press, back, drop or exit. These are the same jobs the hint chips show, worked out from where both players stand, so roles swap on their own.
    - Shut doors count as stone on its walking map. It hops the moat and leaves the far star plate only when the door's time left beats the run.
    - In Room 2, with a person running, it waits a second ("Ready...") before hopping ("GO! Dash!").
    - Once in the exit it stands still.
    - In 1-player games it nudges the person with bubbles ("Stand on the plate, Pip!", "Go! I've got the plate!").
    - It can't clear a room alone, because the exit needs both players.
  - **Bot times:**
    - Computer pair: about 56 s (also on easy and on hard).
    - Noisy partner: 68-71 s.
    - Scripted people with the computer: 56-60 s when they follow the hints, 85-129 s when they react 1.5-3.5 s late, 60-92 s for wanderers who press wrong glyphs.
    - An idle partner, a solo player or a one-key holder never clears. A random masher with the computer sometimes clears Rooms 1 and 3 after 1-3 minutes, but never the whole level.
- **17-2 River Rapids Canoe** (team, mode 'canoe', `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Stretch'`; `js/levels/world17/river-rapids-canoe.js`, brain `js/cpu/levels/world17/river-rapids-canoe.js`). Gavyn's pick: one paddles left and right, the other bails water and pushes rocks away with an oar. Both ride one canoe down a jungle river seen from above. The stretch's def says who paddles (`paddler`), and the jobs swap at the calm pool after every stretch. Courses are token lists (fN advance, b/p rocks, l logs, j jams, i islands, m fruit, R/RR rapids, F flags, S signs, W waterfall lip, P/E pools), compiled in canoeCompile. A sign written 'text||text' uses the second words when that job is the computer's in 1-player mode, so it never shows keys for the computer.
  - **Moving and controls:**
    - The paddler (at the back) strokes with ◀ ▶. Strokes on alternate sides 0.25-1.1 s apart are Swish strokes, the fastest and straightest. The beat bar is in their corner panel.
    - Mash strokes (under 0.2 s apart) only splash: the canoe slows, a little water comes in, and they neither steer nor push off a bank.
    - Holding ◀ or ▶ steers hard, and ▼ back-paddles (less splash in white water).
    - The bailer (at the front) scoops with the action button ("bail") when the bucket glows gold. A bit early is a small scoop; long before it is full bails nothing.
    - ◀ ▶ aim the oar one notch at a time, and jump/▲ pokes along the dotted line. A poke that hits nothing brings the oar back slowly (0.8 s).
    - The act label follows the job (`act.labels` and `[data-actlabel]`). Pad and touch key names come from canoeKey.
  - **Stretches:**
    - **Lazy Bend** (Pip paddles).
    - **Rocky Rapids** (Bo): boulders, wobbly rocks, rapids that splash water in.
    - **Log Jam** (Pip): drifting logs, and branch and log jams that take 1-2 pokes.
    - **Big Waterfall Drop** (Bo): a gold gap in a line of rocks, the lip, a drop with a rainbow and a splash, then the party pool with jungle animals.
    - Each stretch has an early sandbar, so an idle paddler beaches. A jam stops the canoe until it is poked open; at a jam the oar's job is always the branch right in front of the canoe. A slow leak plus rapid splashes swamp a canoe nobody bails.
  - **Soft fails:**
    - Running aground ("Bump! The bank!" / "A sandbar!") just stops the canoe until a real stroke away from it, or a 0.35 s hold away from it.
    - Bonking a rock adds water.
    - Swamping ("Glug! Swamped!"): Cappy the capybara swims over, tips the water out and takes the canoe back to the last flag.
  - **Gold help** (announced with a message and a gold panel border; swamps and beachings only count while both players pressed something recently):
    - Gold bucket after 2 swamps in a stretch (big scoops come sooner and bail more), then Calm water after 3 (half the splashes).
    - Gold paddle after 3 beachings (stronger steering, any stroke pushes off).
    - Gold oar after 3 missed pokes at a jam, or 14 s stopped at one while the bailer is trying (longer reach and a gold arrow).
  - **Scoring and medal:** results tiles are Time, Record, Fruit "x of 26", Swamped, Swish strokes per player and Medal, plus a line with big scoops, pokes and a "Gold help" count when help was used. Gold is ≤ 3:50 with ≥ 60% of the fruit and ≤ 1 swamp. Silver is ≤ 5:00 with ≥ 35% and ≤ 4 swamps. Otherwise Bronze.
  - **Brain:** reads MODES.canoe.needs (the planned lane, the jam ahead, the next poke target with its notch and whether it can be reached, rapids ahead).
    - As paddler it strokes on a beat toward the lane. It keeps still at a closed jam, and slows in splashy rapids, when the canoe is full, and before things a person bailer still has to poke.
    - As bailer it scoops on gold, aims one notch per press and pokes when the target can be reached.
    - With a person it says what it needs in short bubbles ("Poke the branches! W", "Paddle away from the sandbar!"), with rate limits. It never does the person's job.
  - **Bot times:**
    - Computer pair about 3:30 (Gold); noisy pairs 3:35-3:50.
    - Scripted kid + computer 3:25-3:50 (Gold/Silver); two scripted kids 4:00-4:15 (Silver); a slow sloppy kid + computer about 4:05-4:35 (Silver).
    - A person who stops for 25 s of every minute takes about 4:50.
  - **Fairness:** one player alone, an idle partner, one-key holders and act/jump or left/right mashers never get past stretch 1 or 2. A random button-masher with the computer takes 5:00-5:45 (Bronze). Two random mashers take 7:20-7:40 (Bronze).
- **17-3 Waterfall Climb** (mode `waterfall`, team, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`; js/levels/world17/waterfall-climb.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world17/waterfall-climb.js)
  - **Moving:** normal run and jump. Each wall (5-6 tiles; a plain jump is about 3.6 tiles, a jump off a standing partner's head about 4.5) has a **bamboo lift** at its foot (Gavyn, 2026-11-15; it replaced bracing and a boost off the partner's hands): one stands on the deck, the other presses the action at the lever 58 px out from the wall, and the deck hauls the rider up onto the ledge. Nobody can ride it alone. At a vine post, tap help to lower the vine and hold help to heave. To grab the vine press ▲ or jump into it. Hold ▲ or jump to climb (keyboard and touch have no ▲ of their own, so W, ↑ and touch ▲ climb). Jump plus ◀ or ▶ away from the wall hops off. A player carrying the partner can't jump ("Oof!"), so stacked jumps are no shortcut. Partners walk through each other. A vine stays down once dropped.
  - **Rooms:** one tall scrolling climb, 103 rows, built from `def.climb`. The camera follows the pair and always keeps the lower player on screen. The higher player can't get more than 13.5 tiles above the partner ("Wait for me!").
    - Mossy Steps: slippery wet rock, springy moss.
    - Log Jam Falls: a stream that flows on a timer and pushes you down (a log jams it now and then), stepping stones, a drifting log over a pool.
    - Misty Geysers: mist vents that puff you up, a frog bounce.
    - Toucan Top: Tico the toucan's treasure nest. Both must stand by the nest to finish.
    - In all: 7 lift walls and 8 checkpoints. `def.route` describes each ledge, for the hint chips, Tico's hints and the computer.
  - **Soft fails:** falling into water, or more than 2.5 tiles below the team checkpoint, is a "Splash!". A bubble floats you back to the team checkpoint. A checkpoint lights only once both players have touched it, so nobody lands below their partner's checkpoint.
  - **Hints:** chips over each person, in their own device's keys: who pulls the lever (with two people, Pip on even walls; in 1-player mode the computer), stand on the bamboo lift, pull the lever, drop the vine, heave, grab and climb. Also "Hop off Bo's head" and "carrying" chips. The computer's chip is never shown. Tico flies over with a hint after 22 s with no progress.
  - **Gold help (announced):**
    - Big log jam (the water stays off longer) after 3 stream wash-downs.
    - Gold raft (a bigger, slower log) after 2 splashes at the log.
  - **Scoring:** results tiles: splashes per player, lifts given (lever pulls) per player and gems (0-8). Lines: the medal, vines dropped, frog bounces, geyser rides and any gold help. Medals:
    - Gold: 4:00 or less with at most 5 splashes.
    - Silver: 7:30 or less with at most 14 splashes.
    - Bronze otherwise.
  - **Brain:**
    - At a lift wall: if the partner is on the deck it pulls the lever; if the partner is at the lever it stands on the deck; with a person it takes the lever and asks "Hop on the lift!". Two computers split the job by wall number (Pip pulls at walls 1, 3, 5, 7).
    - With a person, the computer braces when the person reaches the foot of the wall and rides when the person braces first. If both keep bracing, it gives way after 1.5 s.
    - On top it always drops the vine and waits at the post, even if the person wandered off below the wall or splashed. It heaves while they climb.
    - It lets the person jump first from the small stepping stones. It hops off a partner's head when not at a wall, and turns round if a low ledge blocks the way. When carrying the person away from a wall it stands still and asks "Hop off my head, please!". It steers off a frog it fell onto.
    - Bubbles appear only in 1-player mode.
  - **Fairness:** one player alone, an idle partner, a masher or a one-key holder (◀, ▶, jump, ▼, help) never gets past the first wall. The partner is left behind and the 13.5-tile limit stops the climb.
  - **Bot times:**
    - Computer pair: 83 s.
    - Noisy pairs: 111-180 s, average 136 s.
    - Noisy pairs with Bo clumsy: 102-168 s.
    - Idle partner: never clears.
    - Scripted person with the computer: 78-95 s, with either one bracing.
    - Scripted kid with the computer: 107-257 s, 2-17 splashes.
    - Person who wanders or walks away from the wall: 103-202 s.
    - Person who waits 20 s before dropping each vine: about 3:45.
    - Full climbs through the real keyboard, controller and touch input path, and with two people on one keyboard: 80 s each.
- **17-4 Vine Swing** (versus, best of 5, `ownStep`; mode `vine`; js/levels/world17/vine-swing.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world17/vine-swing.js): Pip and Bo each hang from their own vine off the treehouse branch and swing at the same time. Longest landing wins the round.
  - **Moving/controls:** ◀ ▶ pumps the way you are swinging; pushing against the swing brakes. **Momentum and whip (Gavyn, after his jungle-swing picture):** pumping builds the swing fast, up past the branch like the picture. A soft cap fades the push between 60° and 135° of swing, so a steady rhythm beats mashing or holding one key: about 7 s of good pumping gets near the top. The bigger the swing, the harder the vine whips you off it: from 70° up the let-go speed grows to +20% at the cap, and a big one cracks with sparks and a snap sound. The vine bows out behind you as you swing and droops slack near the top of a big swing (looks only). Jump lets go, but only on the forward swing ("Wait for the forward swing!"). A pop rates the let-go: Perfect (within 6° of the best spot), a bit early, a bit late, or too late (high). In the air ◀ ▶ nudge, ▼ tucks (falls faster, cuts through a headwind, bigger boings), and the action ("glide") opens one leaf glide per flight, best just after the top. One shared camera pans and zooms to fit both, with edge markers and P/B pins when zoomed out. Posts every 5 m show metres. Before rounds 1 and 2 the round card shows each person's own keys (none for the computer), and in rounds 1-2 a chip under each person says "▶ pump" or "W let go!" at the right moment.
  - **Rounds:** each round is 2 swings, and the longer one counts. **There is no swing clock (Gavyn: never let go of you automatically).** Each swinger lets go when they choose; the chip under the match pill says "Swing 1 of 2 · Let go when you're ready!", and anyone still swinging after 15 s gets the "▶ pump / W let go!" reminder chip in any round. A person who never lets go holds the match (pause or quit to leave). Round 1 is a plain Morning Swing with a dotted arc and a sweet-spot marker for people. Then come 4 shuffled twists from: Windy Day (tail- or headwind, shown by blowing leaves), Stretchy Vine (bungee vines), River Crossing (Hugo the hippo's belly bounces you), Bouncy Mushrooms, Toucan Taxi (fly into a toucan for a short ride) and Night Fireflies (swarms give a sparkle push). Within 0.1 m is a tie and is replayed. A second tie in a row goes to the longest swing of the match; otherwise there is a one-swing sudden-death "Last Swing". After 4 ties in a row the match is a draw.
  - **Soft fails:** mud is "Splat! Still counts!". The river is a splash, and Coco the capybara paddles you to the near bank; the distance up to the bank counts. Letting go too early is a "Plop!" on the deck (0 m). Nothing is ever lost.
  - **Gold help (announced on the round card, at GO and with a gold vine and HUD edge):** whoever trails in rounds gets a gold vine (1.35x pump, an 8° higher cap and a bigger whip, about 5 m more at the top). Two rounds behind also shows the arc hint and the sweet spot. It is off in sudden death.
  - **Scoring/medal:** no medal. The results card has Longest swing, Perfect let-gos, Boings & rides, Splats & splashes, the longest swing of the match and the jungle record (saved in the browser under `vinebest:<name>`).
  - **Brain:** pumps by the sign of its swing as it saw it a reaction time ago. It aims once the swing is high enough (Easy 95°, Normal 122°, Hard 131°), once the swing stops growing, or after its patience (Easy 12 s, Normal 20 s, Hard 30 s of swinging). It lets go at the level's best-release angle (worked out at the start of each forward swing with the same swing and flight rules, without the leaf), off by its aim. In the air it re-plans nudge, tuck and leaf timing every think tick with the same flight rules; the two computers plan on alternating ticks. Easy is slow and sometimes pumps the wrong way, pauses, aims ±17°, lets go at a smaller swing and often forgets the leaf. Hard makes no mistakes and aims ±2°. Bubbles only in 1-player mode.
  - **Performance:** the best-release search is coarse then fine (about 70 flights, 0.2-0.8 ms), with at most one per tick. Steps take under about 3 ms with brief 4-5 ms peaks at the start of a round.
  - **Bot times (after the whip and no-clock change, 2026-11-06):** matches take about 145-265 s of sim time. Hard beats Easy 8/8 from both sides, Normal beats Easy 4/4, Normal vs Normal 13-11 over 24 seeds. Computer landings are mostly 60-100 m. The field is spread 2x (`VINE.fieldK`, river width too, mud and logs out to about 100 m, Tilly the tapir at 105 m) so the twists still matter. The swing is braked past 150° (`VINE.maxA`) so it can never loop over the branch (a gold Stretchy Vine did before the brake). An idle or never-letting-go player now holds the match by design. While anyone is still swinging the camera keeps the tree in view and a far flyer or flag gets the edge marker. Earlier numbers (old 10 s clock): Hard beat Easy 32/32 from both sides; a kid-like scripted player beat Easy about 3 in 4.
- Each level was built, then break-tested by a separate reviewer (one player alone, idle and masher partners, one-key holders, softlocks, scripted 1-player "people" who take the computer's job, wait, wander or go the wrong way; keyboard, stubbed controller and touch at 1280x800, 844x390 and 390x844), then fixed and re-tested. Medal limits are tuned on bots and scripted players only, so real playtests may move them.
- Code: `js/levels/world17/<slug>.js` and `js/cpu/levels/world17/<slug>.js` (temple-doors, river-rapids-canoe, waterfall-climb, vine-swing); defs between BEGIN/END lines in the World 17 block of `level-list.js`. World-page previews are in `js/previews/world17.js` (one BEGIN/END section per level, helpers prefixed `pw17t`, `pw17r`, `pw17w`, `pw17v`).

### World 18: Pirate Cove (mixed)
A sunny tropical cove with friendly cartoon pirates: turquoise sea, striped sails, palm islands, foam swords and pretend gold coins. Helpers are friendly animals (Captain Polly the parrot, Dot and Dolly the dolphins, cheeky crabs, gulls). Nothing is scary and nobody gets hurt ("Splash!", "Bonk!", "Whoa!"). Three team levels, then a versus finale. Gavyn picked 4 of 8 ideas on 2026-11-07 (he skipped Deep Sea Dive, Treasure Map Split, Kraken Tickle and Barrel Roll Race) and asked for more scrolling levels: 18-1 and 18-2 scroll. The world name is Claude's pick. Each level was built by one agent and then reviewed by a second agent that tried to break it with scripted people and fixed what it found; per-level test tools are in tools/w18/.
- **18-1 Runaway Ship** (team, World 18 Pirate Cove, `world: 17`; mode `runship`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Stretch'`; js/levels/world18/runaway-ship.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world18/runaway-ship.js). Gavyn's pick: the little pirate ship broke loose and sails itself out of the harbor, seen from the side. One player steers, the other runs the deck, and they swap jobs at every buoy. At Treasure Cove both drop the anchor together.
  - **The helm** stands at the wheel on the quarterdeck:
    - ▲ / ▼ move the ship one lane at a time between three sea lanes (far, middle, near). The far lane is drawn smaller and higher.
    - ◀ eases off to half speed, for creeping through fog. A rock hit slowly makes no leak.
    - The action rings the bell. Ring it as a big wave hits the bow (a 0.42 s window, up to 0.28 s late) and the ship rides over: "Over the top!".
    - Ringing in the 0.45 s just before the window uses up the bell for that wave ("Too early!"), so mashing doesn't work. Ringing earlier than that is only a warning.
    - **Hazards:**
      - A rock in your lane is a "Bonk!": the ship slows, takes on water and springs a leak.
      - A reef runs the ship "Aground!" until the helm steers into another lane.
      - A wave without the bell is a "Whoa!": +10% water, the coins on deck wash away, and a deckhand who isn't holding on goes overboard.
  - **The deckhand** runs and jumps around the deck, hops up onto the quarterdeck and the forecastle, and climbs the rope ladder with ▲ at the mast (▼ climbs down, jump with ◀ ▶ hops off). Jobs:
    - Stand on a leak and hold the action to hammer a plank over it.
    - Tap the action at the pump to bail. Water slows the ship, down to about a fifth of full speed at 100%. The ship never sinks.
    - Haul the glowing rope at its cleat when the wind changes. Until then the sail flaps at 55% speed.
    - Climb to the tangle a seagull makes in every stretch and hold the action on it. Until then the sail is down and the ship drifts to a stop.
    - Climb into the crow's nest in fog. It's the only way the rocks hidden in the fog show on screen and on the sea chart.
    - Walk over coins that land on deck from treasure barrels.
    - Hold ▼ to hold on when a wave is coming. Climbing, sitting in the nest or working a job also counts as holding on.
  - **Why both are needed:**
    - Every stretch has a reef in the starting lane and a tangle. An idle helm or an idle deckhand never gets past them.
    - Fog needs the lookout.
  - **Controls:**
    - Pip: W / S steer and climb, A / D run, A eases off, F is the action.
    - Bo: ↑ / ↓ steer and climb, ← / → run, ← eases off, / is the action.
    - Controller: the stick (or A for ▲) steers, runs and climbs; ◀ eases off; X is the action.
    - Touch: arrow buttons plus the action button.
    - The action word follows the job: "bell" at the helm, "fix" on deck, "anchor" at the end. It shows in the legend, on the touch buttons and on both job cards.
  - **Stretches** (about 54 s each, then a 2.5 s "Buoy! Swap jobs!" hop):
    | # | Stretch | Who steers | What's in it |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | 1 | Harbor Mouth | Pip | Gentle rocks, first reef, leak, wind change, first wave, tangle, coins |
    | 2 | Reef Run | Bo | Reefs, wind change, a wave |
    | 3 | Stormy Patch | Pip | Rain, 3 big waves, a 1,900 px fog bank with hidden rocks placed at random |
    | 4 | Treasure Cove | Bo | Fog, coin barrels, a wave, then the anchor |
    Signs and Polly's sign introduce each new thing just before it matters.
  - **Soft fails** (nothing restarts):
    - Bonk! and Aground!
    - Whoa!
    - "Splash!": Dot the dolphin flips the deckhand back aboard after 2.4 s.
    - "Glug! So much water!"
    - Polly squawks a hint after 7 s stopped (aground or sail down).
  - **Gold help** (announced with a gold message, Polly and a sound; only while both players are pressing things):
    - Gold rudder: after 3 bonks in a stretch. Quicker steering, and a gold arrow on the chart points to the safe lane.
    - Gold bell: after the same helm player misses 2 waves. The window doubles to 0.85 s, there's no early lock, and it stays for that player's later stretches.
    - Gold hammer: after 10 s with the water over 70%. Patches take half the time, and each pump bails twice as much.
    - Gold knife: after 14 s of trying at a tangle. Untangling takes 0.3 s.
    - Gold spyglass: after 2 bonks in fog. Hidden rocks show in gold on the chart.
    - Gold anchor: after 20 s at the cove. The chain never slips back, and a hop at the capstan still counts.
  - **Finish:** at the cove both run to the capstan on the forecastle and hold the action together for 1.3 s. Then "SPLOOSH! Anchor down!", a 5.5 s party with confetti, and the results.
  - **Results:**
    - Tiles: Time, Record, Bonks, Leaks patched (Pip / Bo), Coins "x of 56", Medal.
    - Lines: a bonk summary, waves met "x of 6", who Dot saved, the quickest stretch, and the gold help count.
  - **Medal:**
    - Gold: 4:00 or under with at most 4 bonks.
    - Silver: 5:30 or under with at most 12 bonks.
    - Bronze: otherwise.
  - **Brain:** reads `MODES.runship.needs(m)`: the best lane, judged only from what's in sight (fog rocks only with a lookout or the gold spyglass), whether fog means easing off, the next wave and when it hits, and the deckhand's most useful job (hold on, tangle, nest, trim, leak, pump, coin).
    - At the helm it taps ▲ / ▼ one lane at a time, holds ◀ in blind fog, and rings 0.2 s before the wave hits.
    - On deck it walks and hops up steps, climbs the ladder, holds the action on leaks and knots, taps at the pump and cleat, and holds ▼ until a wave lands.
    - At the end it runs to the capstan and holds.
    - In 1-player mode it says short, rate-limited bubbles: "Steer away from the reef!", "Ring the bell as it hits!", "Climb to the crow's nest, please!", "Patch the leak! Hold /", "Hold the action at the anchor with me!".
    - It never does the person's job.
  - **Bot times:**
    - Computer pair: 221–222 s (Gold, 0 bonks).
    - Noisy Pip: 231–234 s. Noisy Bo: 226–229 s.
    - Scripted person who follows the prompts 0.7 s late: 223–224 s.
    - Kid-like person: 229–231 s (Gold).
    - Waits 25 s at the anchor: about 249 s (Silver).
    - Random masher with the computer: 5:52–7:57 (Bronze, through the gold anchor).
  - **Fairness:**
    - These never clear: an idle partner, a person who quits after 60 s, holding jump or ▲ the whole run, a wrong-way player, or one player alone.
    - A bell masher misses waves until the gold bell.
  - **Tests:** tools/w18/runship-test.js (stats per run), runship-break.js (scripted 1-player people), runship-io.js (keyboard, controller, touch, labels, R, pause, no leaks between levels), runship-shots.js (screenshots).
- **18-2 Grapple & Zip** (team, mode `grapple`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Fleet'`; js/levels/world18/grapple-and-zip.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world18/grapple-and-zip.js; tools in tools/w18/: grapple-shots, grapple-debug, grapple-people, grapple-controls, grapple-windows). Gavyn's pick: cross a whole fleet of friendly pirate ships in side view. One throws a grappling hook to the next mast, the other zips down the rope for coins, then cranks the thrower across. The camera follows one hop at a time.
  - **Moving and controls:** A/D or ←/→ walk, W/S or ↑/↓ climb the rope ladder, aim and lift legs/dip; the action is F or /, X on a controller, the round touch button. The act word changes live with the job: thrower throw → ride, zipper grab → drop → crank. The legend's controller line says "action".
    - **Thrower:** stands on the gold X in the crow's nest. ▲▼ aim (a short dotted guide shows the start of the flight). Hold the action: the power meter behind the thrower goes up and down (one cycle is 2.4 s). Let go to throw. The hook flies with gravity and wind/gusts and must pass through the ring on the next ship's mast; on Breezy Barnacle and Wobbly Whale the ring swings on a boom.
    - **Zipper:** waits on the ladder side of the crow's nest and grabs the handle from anywhere in it. On the rope: ▶ tuck, ◀ brake, ▲ legs up (flags, high coins), ▼ dip (lanterns, low coins). Too fast at the stopper knot is "Bonk! The mast!" and a small bounce back. At the knot, let go (or wait: you drop by yourself after 1.8 s).
    - **Haul:** the thrower hops into the bosun's chair from anywhere in the nest. The zipper cranks the capstan with steady presses (0.3–1.15 s apart is a full heave; faster slips). The rider presses ▶ right after a heave to help and ▲▼ for the chair coins. The end of the line sags uphill, so heaves pull less there.
    - "All aboard!", then the jobs swap (Pip throws hops 1, 3, 5, 7). Nobody can finish a hop alone.
  - **Fleets (4 × 2 hops, 64 coins):**
    - **Dockside Hop:** Pirate Cove dock → Sea Biscuit → Jolly Jelly.
    - **Laundry Day:** socks on the line to throw over or under, lanterns to dip under.
    - **Windy Boom:** tailwind and a swinging boom ring, then a gusty headwind.
    - **Polly's Flagship:** gulls, then The Golden Polly with an uphill last haul, a treasure chest, a cheering crew, and Captain Polly saying "Ahoy, Pip and Bo! Welcome aboard!", then confetti.
    - Each fleet's first hop shows a wooden sign low over the water. A sign written 'text||text' uses the second words in 1-player mode.
  - **Soft fails:**
    - Missed throws ("Clunk!", "Splash!", "Whoosh! Too far!", "Flump! Socks!", "Squawk! A gull!") reel back in 1 s, with a tip on what went wrong.
    - "Splash!" when you let go over the water: Dolly the dolphin carries you back to the start ship.
    - "Bonk!" from a flag, a lantern or the knot just slows you, once per pass.
    - No lives, no game over.
  - **Gold help (always announced):**
    - Gold hook after 3 misses on a hop: ring ×1.9, meter 1.3× slower, a longer guide.
    - Super gold hook after 6: ring ×2.6, meter 1.6× slower, the whole flight shown with a star or a cross where it ends, gold marks on the meter for the powers that catch, Polly by the ring.
    - Gold handle after 2 splashes on a hop (it won't let go over water).
    - Gold capstan after 14 s of hauling (heaves ×1.6).
    - Captain Polly flies over with a hint after 20 s stuck on a job; in 1-player mode she points at the person's job. Her bubble moves beside her if it would cover the HUD.
  - **HUD:** the top-right pill shows time and coins, then a "Ship N of 8 · name · tries" chip (gold border while gold help is on) and a wind pill. Job cards sit in the bottom corners (keys for each player's own device, a meter, "Computer" for the computer). Prompt chips sit over each person's head; two in one spot stack.
  - **Results and medal:** tiles are Coins "x of 64", Hooks / misses, Splashes & bonks, and Medal. One line gives coins per player, first-try catches and good heaves, plus a gold-help line when help was used. Gold is ≤ 3:45 with ≥ 65% of the coins and ≤ 8 misses; Silver is ≤ 5:30 with ≥ 35%; otherwise Bronze.
  - **Brain:** plays the job MODES.grapple.needs gives it.
    - Throwing: plans with the level's own hook physics (`test.hookMove`, `test.powAt`), picks the angle with the widest window of catching powers, and adds an aim error that shrinks after each miss. It holds ▲▼ to the angle, holds the action, lets go on the tick where its pretend throw catches, and replans after 2 meter cycles.
    - Zipping: tucks, then brakes to reach the knot at about 0.55× bonk speed, dodges and reaches for coins, lets go at the knot.
    - Riding: pulls ▶ after each heave and reaches for chair coins. Cranking: a steady 0.40–0.52 s beat once the rider is boarded.
    - With a person it never does their job and nudges with short bubbles after 5 s.
  - **Bot times:**
    - Computer pair: 129–162 s (Gold).
    - Noisy partner: 142–189 s.
    - Scripted 1-player people (following prompts, slow, kid-like, wanderers, ladder-climbers, jumpers, ones who try the computer's job first): 145–387 s, mostly Silver.
    - Idle, masher and quitter people never clear.
- **18-3 Tide Pool Dig** (team, mode `tidedig`, `noFalls`, `ownHUD`).
  - **Pitch (Gavyn's pick):** One player digs for buried chests while the other bails seawater out with a bucket and shoos away crabs that steal coins.
    - **Code:**
      - `js/levels/world18/tide-pool-dig.js`
      - `js/cpu/levels/world18/tide-pool-dig.js`
      - The def is between its BEGIN/END lines in `level-list.js`.
      - The preview is in the 18-3 block of `js/previews/world18.js` (helpers named `pw18t`).
    - **The look:** a side view of a sunny beach, cut away so you can see under the sand. The chests show faintly through the sand, and you watch each hole go down.
  - **Rules:**
    - Each room says who digs (`digger`); the other player is the bailer.
    - The action button reads "dig" or "bail" per player and per room, on the legend and on the touch buttons.
    - **The digger**
    - Stands on the red X and holds (or taps) the action to dig: about 2.6 strokes a second, and mashing is no faster.
    - A sand tile takes 5 + 2 × its depth strokes. The sand flies onto a pile beside the hole.
    - Holding jump (or ▲) in the hole climbs out and pops you out sideways at the top.
    - The last stroke hits the chest: "CLUNK!", and coins shower out onto the sand on both sides. Coins never land in a hole or in the sea.
    - Either player picks up coins by walking over them. Saved coins go to the treasure-blanket stash, which is kept from room to room.
    - **Waves**
    - Each wave goes calm → swell (the HUD meter warns; big waves are warned too) → rush → hold → back.
    - A wave that reaches an open hole pours water into it.
    - With 8 or more px of water the digger can't dig ("Glub!"). Less water than that slows digging.
    - Deeper water floats the digger up. Water never drains by itself.
    - **The bailer**
    - Taps the action at the hole's edge to scoop: 16 px a scoop, 28 px with the gold bucket.
    - A full bucket slows walking.
    - Taps the action at the sea, or at a rock pool, to dump the bucket ("Sploosh!").
    - In rooms 3 and 4, holding the action at the flagged spot pats a sand wall (2.2 s). The wall stops one small wave and is then washed flat. A BIG wave goes over it and still pours in at 0.75×.
    - The hole's mouth is solid for the bailer only, so the bailer can walk over it and the digger drops in.
    - **Crabs (rooms 2-4)**
    - Crabs peek out of burrows by the rocks and go for loose coins first, then the stash.
    - A crab that reaches a coin grabs it and runs home. A coin that reaches the burrow is a crab steal and is lost for good.
    - Either player shoos a crab by tapping the action within 66 px of it, or by landing a jump within 70 px. The crab drops the coin and runs.
    - **The big chest (last room)**
    - It is too heavy to open in the hole. Both players hold the action, one at each rope post, either player at either post, for 2.6 s.
    - During the haul no new crabs come out, and a tap away from the posts still shoos.
    - The chest comes up and sits open on the sand.
    - **Winning**
    - A room ends when every chest is open, no coins are loose, and no crab carries one.
    - It holds for 2 s, then moves on.
    - Waves never hurt anyone. The level can always be finished.
  - **Rooms (96 coins in 6 chests):**
    | Room | Digger | Chests (depth / coins) | Waves | Crabs | Other |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | 1 Sandy Cove | Pip | one: 4 / 12 | every 7 s (first at 4 s), pour 24 | none | teaches dig and bail |
    | 2 Rock Pools | Bo | two: 3 / 12, then 4 / 14 | every 6 s, pour 26 | up to 2 | rock-pool dump spot |
    | 3 Shipwreck Beach | Pip | one: 6 / 20, under the wreck | every 6 s; every 2nd is BIG (pour 40) | up to 2 | sand-wall spot |
    | 4 Skull Rock Lagoon | Bo | 2 / 8, then a big 6-deep chest / 30 hauled on ropes | every 3rd is BIG; the tide rises over 150 s (6.5 s → 4.5 s apart, pour 24 → 32, reaching further) | up to 3, burrows on both sides | rope posts, rock pool, sand wall |
  - **Soft fails:**
    - **Flood:** the digger is blocked by water for 6 s. It is counted, but nothing is lost.
    - **Steal:** a crab gets a coin home. The coin is lost, which lowers the coin score and the medal.
  - **Gold help (announced, once each):**
    - **Gold bucket:** after 2 floods in a room. Scoops take 28 px and the bucket no longer slows the bailer.
    - **Captain Polly the parrot:** after 3 steals in a room. She chases crabs that carry or grab a coin.
    - **Gold shovel:** after 75 s in a room with a chest still shut. Strokes count 1.7×.
  - **Results and medals:**
    - **Tiles:** Coins saved "x of 96", Crab steals, Sand dug (Pip / Bo), Buckets bailed (Pip / Bo).
    - **Lines:** the medal with a cheer, then shoos (Pip, Bo, Polly), waves held by walls, floods and the help used.
    | Medal | Time | Coins saved |
    |---|---|---|
    | Gold | ≤ 240 s | ≥ 85% |
    | Silver | ≤ 400 s | ≥ 60% |
    | Bronze | otherwise | — |
  - **Hints:**
    - Chips read "Name (digger/bailer): …" and use each player's own device keys.
    - They show when a job starts and again when it drags on, and never over the computer's player.
    - They come from `tidedigPlan`, the same plan the brain uses.
  - **The computer partner:**
    - It plays only the job `tidedigPlan` gives it.
    - **As the digger:**
      - Walks to the X, hopping over open holes.
      - Digs while the water is low and waits while the hole is flooded.
      - Climbs out centred in the hole.
      - Picks up coins.
      - Holds a rope post for the haul.
    - **As the bailer, in this order:**
      1. Shoo a crab that carries or grabs a coin.
      2. Dump a full bucket at the nearest sea or rock-pool spot.
      3. Scoop from the hole's edge, ±32 px on its own side.
      4. Pat the wall between waves.
      5. Pick up coins.
      6. Otherwise wait by the hole on the sea side.
    - **Getting around:** it jumps steps, and it jumps over a partner standing in its way, such as a digger floating in a flooded hole or someone standing on the X.
    - **The haul:** it gives up its post to a person who wants it.
    - **Reaction time:** 0.08 s plus up to 0.3 s more at low skill after each job change.
    - **Talking (1-player only):** "Glub! Bail me out, please!", "Dig at the X, Name!", "Dig, dig, Name!", "Shoo that crab!", "Dump the bucket in the sea!", "Hold the rope with me!", "Scoot over, please!".
  - **Bot times:**
    | Run | Total time | Medal / coins | Room times |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | Pair | 149-171 s | Gold, 92-96 coins | about 23, 36-50, 34-44 and 42-52 s |
    | Noisy partner | 190-241 s | — | — |
    | Scripted 1-player people | 160-330 s | — | — |
  - **Preview:**
    - **Loop:** Pip digs at the X and a wave floods the hole ("Glub!"). Bo scoops the water out and dumps it in the sea ("Sploosh!"). "CLUNK!", and coins shower onto the blanket. A crab grabs a stray coin; Pip lands by it ("Shoo!") and it drops the coin. Confetti to finish.
    - **Scene:** a shallow sea over a sandy seabed.
    - **Timing:** the loop is 6.6 s and the still frame is at 3.8 s, at about 1.5 ms a frame.
    - **Blurb:** "One digs for buried treasure, the other bails out the waves and shoos crabs!"
  - **Tests in tools/w18/:**
    - `tidedig-run.js`: a bot run that reports each room, with optional traces and screenshots.
    - `tidedig-input.js`: keyboard, stubbed controller and touch at 844x390 and 390x844.
    - `tidedig-break.js`: scripted people in 1-player mode, as Pip and as Bo.
    - `tidedig-life.js`: pause, R, leaving and coming back, and 1-player as Bo, at 1280x800 and 844x390.
    ---------------------------------------------------------------
- **18-4 Plank Duel** (versus, best of 5, mode `plankduel`, `ownStep`; js/levels/world18/plank-duel.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world18/plank-duel.js). Gavyn's pitch: a foam-sword fight on a plank over a splash pool. You attack high or low and block high or low. Each hit pushes the other player back, and whoever goes off the end gets a splash.
  - **Scene:** side view of a sunny lagoon. One long plank rests on a barrel on a little rock, with posts in the water. Behind it are two friendly pirate ships with star sails (Pip's has the orange flag, Bo's the blue one). A crab, a seagull and a parrot cheer on each rail. Captain Polly the parrot, in a captain's hat, is the referee on a post. She squawks a short word: "En garde!", "Clang! Squawk!", "Thwack!", "Splash! Arr!". The water has fish, bubbles, seaweed and a duck float. Pip and Bo wear tricorn hats with a gold star (Pip's sprout pokes out of the top) and carry foam swords (Pip orange, Bo blue). The platformer bodies are hidden and the mode draws the duellists, like 17-4.
  - **Controls:**
    - Stand = HIGH guard. Hold ▼ = LOW guard (a crouch). A bright bracket in front of you shows your guard.
    - ◀ ▶ take small steps, slower while crouched. Walking always stops at the plank's end, and walking into the other player never pushes them: whoever walks in is stopped. Only swords push.
    - The action (`swing`) swings at your stance height after a 0.42 s wind-up. The sword goes back, an arc fills in at that height, and a ▲/▼ badge shows over your head.
    - Changing stance during the wind-up is a FEINT, once per swing: the height changes, but at least 0.26 s of wind-up stays left so it can still be read. Later stance changes don't change that swing.
    - Jump hops (about 0.5 s). A low swing misses a hopper ("Hop! Missed me!"), but you can't block in the air, so a high swing bonks you further.
    - Hold the action past the wind-up to charge (glowing sword, no guard, can't move). Let go after 0.45 s to LUNGE: a dash plus a big push (100 px).
    - Act labels: `swing` everywhere. The round card shows each person's own keys before rounds 1-2 (touch: "round button swings"). The legend and pad line are set in the def.
  - **Rules:**
    - **Block:** a swing that meets the same guard is "Clack! Block!". Both slide apart 8 px. The attacker wobbles for 0.3 s (no guard, can't swing; 0.7 s after a lunge). The blocker's next swing within 0.45 s is a quick 0.32 s riposte.
    - **Bonk:** a swing that meets the wrong guard, or anyone winding up, charging or wobbling, is a "Bonk!". The defender slides back 50 px, 25% further for each bonk already taken this round (up to 4), and their swing is cancelled.
    - **Clang:** swings that land within 0.07 s of each other at the same height are "Clang!" and both bounce apart. At different heights both land ("Double bonk!").
    - **Edge:** pushed just past the end, you teeter ("Whoa! Step back!" for 0.8 s). Step toward the middle for "Phew!", or fall.
  - **Rounds:** round 1 is Calm Cove (half-plank 300 px, 45 s). Then come 4 shuffled twists:
    - Short Plank (215 px, 40 s).
    - Rocking Plank: tilts ±6° on the barrel every 7 s, and you slide downhill. The HUD shows "downhill ◀/▶".
    - Slippery Plank: wet sheen. Bonks push 1.3x and slide further, and steps slide.
    - Breezy Cove: gusts every 3.5-6.5 s with a 1.2 s flag warning, blowing both players along, less so when crouched.
    - The round time is set per plank by overriding `G.match.timeLeft` in resetRound; def.match.roundTime is 45.
  - **Ending a round:**
    - Off the end is "SPLASH!": a fish jumps and you bob up laughing in the duck float. The round ends 0.8 s later with "Bo took a splash!".
    - At time up, whoever landed more bonks this round wins ("Time's up! More bonks for Pip!"; the ★ count in the HUD). Next, whoever has more plank behind them wins (by 8 px or more). Otherwise it is a tie and replayed.
    - A second tie in a row goes to the most bonks this match. Otherwise the framework's sudden death is "Last Clash" (240 px, 30 s, first bonk wins; a double bonk is a tie).
  - **Soft fails:** foam swords only. "Bonk!", "Swish!" (a whiff), "Whoa!", "Splash!", "Whoopsie!". Nobody is hurt.
  - **Gold help** (announced on the round card, at GO and with a gold HUD edge):
    - 1 round behind: Gold boots (bonks push you 25% less, teeter 1.25 s).
    - 2+ rounds behind: also Polly's call, a gold "Polly: ▲ High!" / "▼ Low!" chip over your head while a swing comes at you.
    - Off in Last Clash.
  - **HUD:**
    - Bottom corners: each player's guard icon and word (High / Low / Open / Hop! / Charging / Whoa! / Splash!), a bar of plank left behind them, and a gold ★ with bonks landed this round.
    - The twist chip sits under the level pill.
    - Hint chips for people only (never the computer) in rounds 1-2 or with gold: "Hold S: block low!", "Let go of S: block high!", "F swing! (S + F = low)", "▶ step closer". In any round, "D step back!" shows when teetering.
  - **Results:** tiles are Bonks landed, Blocks, Hop dodges and Splashes. Lines: clangs · feints · lunges · "Phew!" saves, and "Big lunge bonks: Pip N!". No medal (versus).
  - **Brain** (`plankduel`, `noIdleStop`, `bubbleHidden`):
    - What it reads (all visible on screen), via cpuLag at its reaction time: positions, plank left behind each player, the other's stance, wind-up height and state, charge, lunge, wobble, hop and teeter. Reaction time is Easy 0.5 s (slower than a wind-up, so it mostly guesses), Normal 0.33 s, Hard 0.15 s.
    - It knows at once when a swing has landed (the clack or bonk anyone hears), so it stops guarding against it.
    - Defending: it guards the seen height, misreads sometimes (Easy 30%, Normal 25%, Hard 6%) and hops some low swings. It never starts a swing into a visible wind-up, and it punishes a charger.
    - Attacking: just inside reach it swings at the height the other isn't guarding (Easy 30% of the time, Normal 45%, Hard 88%; otherwise a random height). It punishes wobbles and hops (high), and ripostes after a block once its own reaction time has passed.
    - Normal and Hard feint: start at one height and switch once the other has matched it. They lunge at someone near their end or wobbling.
    - In the last 10 s of a round where it isn't ahead on bonks, it swings more often.
    - Between swings it mirrors the other's stance (Easy holds a guess), steps to just inside reach, backs off only when it has more than 150 px of plank behind it, and leaves a teeter after its reaction time. Easy also dawdles.
    - Bubbles only in 1-player mode ("En garde!", "Arr! That round's mine!", "Nice sword work!", "Whoopsie! Splash!").
  - **Bot results** (tools/w18/plankduel-sim.js, sim seconds):
    - Equal skills, over 60-100 seeds:
      | Match | Pip wins | Bo wins | Rounds (Pip-Bo) | Median match |
      |---|---|---|---|---|
      | Normal vs Normal (100 seeds) | 40 | 60 | 197-228 | 159 s |
      | Hard vs Hard (60 seeds) | 32 | 28 | 130-131 | 187 s |
      | Easy vs Easy (60 seeds) | 29 | 31 | 114-126 | 169 s |
      Per-seed results are noisy: the builder's code gave 59-41 the other way at Normal vs Normal over the same 100 seeds. Taking the round counts with that, there is no clear side bias.
    - Hard beats Easy 20/20 from both sides (about 65 s). Normal beats Easy 20/20 and Hard beats Normal 20/20, from both sides.
    - About half of all rounds end in a splash.
    - A kid-like script (0.35 s reaction, 15% misreads, random-height swings, no feints, lunges or hops) beats Easy 20/20 as Pip and 18/20 as Bo. A slower kid (0.5 s, 25% misreads) beats Easy 8/20. The same kid loses to Normal 0/20, even when it targets the open height or swings more often. It mostly gets bonked during its own wind-up, and it doesn't hop or feint.
  - **Break tests** (tools/w18/plankduel-break.js, as Pip and as Bo vs Easy, Normal and Hard): an idle player, one who walks forward or backward, mashers, hop spam, crouch, holding the action, random keys and a perfect blocker who never swings all lose every match. Every match finishes, with no errors.
    - Lunge spam and feint spam (mashing ▼ while swinging) beat Easy 4/4; feint spam also takes the odd match off Normal or Hard (1/4 each, by more bonks at time up).
  - **Tested:**
    - Real keyboard (both players), a stubbed Xbox controller (stick down = low, X swings, A hops) and touch on a phone at 844x390 (the round button reads "swing", ▲ hops); tools/w18/plankduel-input.js passes.
    - Screenshots at 1280x800 and 844x390 with touch, in 1-player mode as Pip and as Bo (tools/w18/plankduel-shots.js, plankduel-scenes.js).
    - Leaving to another level and coming back starts fresh; R restarts; pause stops the clock (tools/w18/plankduel-leak.js). No console errors.
    - Preview `pw18p` runs at 2.7 ms/frame.

### World 19: Tumbleweed Gulch (mixed)
The Wild West: a sunny desert with red mesas, a train, a gold mine and a main street at high noon. Nothing scary, nobody gets hurt (cork popguns only). World name is Claude's pick. Gavyn picked these four from eight ideas (Nov 2026); he skipped Cattle Roundup, Tightrope Canyon, Bucking Bronco and Barrel Race.

- **19-1 Train Heist Hop** (team, mode `heist`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Stretch'`; files js/levels/world19/train-heist-hop.js, js/cpu/levels/world19/train-heist-hop.js, def in js/levels/level-list.js, preview in js/previews/world19.js): Pip and Bo run along the roofs of the Gulch Line train, from the caboose to the engine. The camera scrolls with the train, and all positions are in train coordinates (`MODES.heist.tx`). Exactly one player carries the gold sack. The carrier runs at 0.8 speed with jumpV 440, which clears narrow gaps (60 px) but never wide ones (124 px). Free players get 0.15 s coyote time and no jump cut, and even a standing jump clears a wide gap. The carrier holds Act to charge a toss (up to 0.55 s, with a dotted aim arc) and releases to throw. The free player catches the sack in the air or picks it up from the roof. The thrower can't take their own sack back across a wide gap they threw it over (a "Let Bo grab it!" pop), so nobody can do both jobs. A sack left lying there for 8 s boings to the partner. Hold down to duck (0.42 speed, no jumping); a ducked player is never bonked. Duck under low bridges, arches, water-tower spouts and the tunnel, which goes dark with lanterns. Each hazard warns 1.5 s ahead with a whistle and a sign ("Duck: hold Pip S · Bo ↓"), and the sign goes once the hazard passes the front player. Tumbleweeds roll down the roof and must be hopped. A hit is a bonk: a short stun and a push toward the middle of the car. There are 4 stretches: Caboose Run, Canyon Bridges, Water Tower Way and Tunnel to the Engine. The sack starts with a different player each stretch, and a stretch ends when both players stand on the flag car with the sack held. In the last stretch both players stand in the engine cab and hold Act (the `brake` label) for 1.1 s. No new hazards come once anyone is in the cab. The train then stops at Tumbleweed Gulch station and the sheriff takes the sack. Soft fails: falling into a gap is a Whoops (Dusty's hay cart boings you back to the last car after 1.6 s, and the sack goes with you), and a sack that falls into a gap bounces back to its thrower. Players are leashed to 420 px apart, on roofs and while hopping above them (not over gaps).
