Gold help, announced in the news line: 2 sack misses at one gap give a Gold sack (catch radius 40), 3 whoopses at one gap give a gold plank across it, and 25 s without progress gives a hint for the stuck player. Medal: Gold at ≤3:45 with ≤4 whoopses, Silver at ≤6:00 with ≤12, otherwise Bronze. The results card shows Whoops, Sacks tossed and Catches per player, plus Bonks and Ducked under. Hint chips avoid the computer's speech bubble. Brain (`cpuBrain('heist')`): the free player crosses the wide gap (its run-up never backs into the leash) and waits facing back. The carrier walks to 13 px before the edge and tosses once the partner is on the far roof, even if the partner is hopping there, waiting a 0.5 s beat when the partner is a person. Other behaviour: catches under the flying sack, fetches a loose sack it is allowed to take (otherwise it walks next to it), ducks about 0.24 s before a beam, hops weeds, and holds the brake when both players are in the cab. Speech bubbles appear only when the partner is a person. Bot times: computer pair about 1:55, noisy pairs 2:35-2:40; one player alone, an idle partner, or a bot that only holds right never clears.
- **19-2 Handcar Hustle** (team, ownStep/ownHUD/noFalls, roomWord 'Stretch'; js/levels/world19/handcar-hustle.js, js/cpu/levels/world19/handcar-hustle.js, preview in js/previews/world19.js): mode 'handcar', all state in G.mst. Pip and Bo stand at opposite ends of a seesaw pump handle (st.back says who has the back end) and each grips their end. A push is a fresh ▼ (input.down edge) or pendingAct (act word 'pump'). It counts only while your end is up, and the handle then flips, so one player alone gets one push and then only "Clunk!". If you push within 0.11 s after your partner, the handle jams for 0.6 s. Evenly spaced turns build a rhythm streak (up to +28% per push), shown on the pump-o-meter. Holding ▲ (jump/up) brakes, and each player's brake is half of the full brake. Presses during the push-back, the swap and the finish are dropped. The course is compiled from tokens: f/u/d/U/D flat and hills, b bridge, t trestle, S: sign, Z: scenery zone, F checkpoint flag, K broken track (sign, flagman, buffer, crew with a plank), C sharp curve, W cows crossing, M mail station, X swap arch, E town station. There are 4 stretches (Dusty Flats, Mesa Hills, Canyon Trestle, Into Town) with 7 mail sacks, and the players hop over the handle to swap ends at each X arch. Soft fails: passing the red flag makes the crew push the handcar back ("Whoops!"). Going over the curve limit (140) makes the handcar start to tip ("Too fast! Brake!"); braking within about half a second saves it, otherwise (or above 1.55× the limit) a "Whoa" wobble drops the speed. Cows give a moo bump. Stalling on a steep hill rolls the handcar back. Stopping just past a mail or town box makes the station hand or station master push the handcar back (there is no reverse); rolling more than 100 px past a mail box misses that sack. Gold help is always announced: a gold push after 2 roll-backs on a hill; gold signs (shown earlier, with a gold brake line) after 2 crashes at a place or of one kind; a speedy crew after 3 crashes at one broken track; a wider gold STOP box after 2 crashes at the town station; and a hay bale that stops the handcar in the box after 3. Hints: "Your turn!" after a long wait, "Let go of the brake" when ▲ is held while it is time to roll, and one-time lessons per stretch. The HUD shows the clock, sacks, a speed dial with a red curve zone, the pump-o-meter, and per-player job panels with PUMP/BRAKE chips. Prompts appear over the heads, merged into one when both players get the same words. Results show mail sacks, pumps per player, rhythm streak and medal: Gold for ≥70% of sacks, ≤260 s, ≤3 bumps and ≤12 jams; Silver for ≥40% and ≤380 s; otherwise Bronze. Brain: it reads MODES.handcar.needs(st, brake*0.5), so it plans to stop using only its own brake. It brakes on brake/hold, and it pushes only when its end is up, the handle is not jammed, and at least 0.2 s have passed since the partner's push. It pumps on a 0.34 s beat (0.3 s on steep hills), or at the person's measured tempo, and creeps forward into stop boxes. It can never pump the handcar alone. In 1-player mode it says short bubbles ("Your turn! Pump!", "Brake! Brake!", "Wait for the cows!", "Got the mail!").
- **19-3 Gold Mine Cart** (team, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, roomWord 'Stretch'; files js/levels/world19/gold-mine-cart.js, js/cpu/levels/world19/gold-mine-cart.js, def in level-list.js, preview in previews/world19.js): mode 'minecart'. Each stretch's `rider` (front seat) jumps (▲ hop, held for a higher one), ducks (▼ held) and grabs lanterns (●). The switcher (back seat) sets the lever with ▲/▼ (it stays put) and brakes with ● or ◀ (the brake works only on the rails, down to 140 px/s). In the air the cart keeps the speed it hopped with: no slope pull and no brake. Courses are token lists built by `minecartCompile`: f, u, d, D steep slope with bend, gN gap, r rock, b beam, c curtain (waterfall), wN ford, n/N gold, aN arc, l lantern, S: wall sign, X station, E exit; fork objects {up, down, dead, sign: plain|xonly|both|count|lantern}. A dead end or missed gap calls `minecartStartOops`: Mabel the mule pulls the cart back with `minecartSafeBack`, to the last spot it rode through before the fork's sign (dead end) or 460 px before the gap (fall), always with a 340 px run-up (`runUp`) before the next gap. Checkpoint flags are only for looks. After 2 dead ends in a row gold signs show; after 2 missed jumps at the same gap a gold spring appears, and it always lands the cart past the gap (it pushes a slow cart forward). `MODES.minecart.needs(st)` is the one place that reads what's ahead, for the prompts and the computer brain. Stretch 4 is dark; the good track's lantern is lit. Gold total counts the main line plus the richer open track at each fork. Medal: Gold needs ≥70% gold and ≤250 s; Silver needs ≥45% and ≤380 s. A perfect computer pair takes about 3:19 and a noisy one about 3:40-4:05.
- **19-4 Quick-Draw Showdown** (versus, best of 7, `ownStep`; mode `quickdraw`; js/levels/world19/quick-draw-showdown.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world19/quick-draw-showdown.js): Stand back to back, pace apart, then wait for the gold DRAW! and fire your cork popgun first (Gavyn's pick). The scene is a high-noon main street: the General Store, the Root Beer Saloon (Mayor Mosey calls the duel from its balcony beside a big brass bell) and Hats & Boots (a crow on its sign, a cowbell on its porch post), with red mesas, a water trough and tumbleweeds. Pip and Bo wear little cowboy hats and carry toy cork popguns (a cork on a string and a POP! flag). The platformer bodies are hidden and the mode draws everything with a gentle camera: it is zoomed in during the countdown, pulls back while they pace and pushes in slowly during the wait.
  - **Moving/controls:** the action (F or /, controller X, the round touch button labelled "fire") fires. In bandit rounds ◀ ▶ (A D, ← →, the stick or d-pad, or the touch arrows) moves your aim marker one spot per press, and holding repeats. Jump does nothing. The framework's Round card and 3-2-1 play while they stand back to back; there is no big GO!. The Mayor counts "1... 2... 3..." while they pace apart by themselves (1.5 s), then they turn. A press while pacing only says "Not yet! Wait for DRAW!". Then comes a random 1.5-5 s wait, and a press during it is a foul. DRAW! is always a huge gold word, a white flash and the bell's ding-ding sound, never just a speech bubble. The first fresh press after it wins and the cork pops the other's hat off. A press on the very tick DRAW! appears still counts as early. Effective times within 15 ms are a tie (replayed). Nobody firing within 6 s of DRAW! is a tie. Reaction times in ms show on the corner panels, over the heads and in the round reason; a late press after a plain round is decided still gets its time on the board.
  - **Rounds:**
    - 1 First Draw (no fakeouts).
    - 2 Tricky Words: the Mayor says DRAWER!, DRAGON!, DRUMSTICK! and so on.
    - 3 Noisy Noon: 1-2 fakeouts of any kind (crow CAW!, a tumbleweed CLONKs the cowbell, the saloon doors go WHAP!, the Mayor sneezes ACHOO!).
    - 4 Bandit Pop-up.
    - 5 Fakeout Frenzy (2-3 fakeouts).
    - 6 Bandit Shuffle.
    - 7 Final Showdown.
    - Sudden death is a Sudden Draw. Replays get fresh fakeouts.
    - In bandit rounds three cardboard cutouts pop up across the street at DRAW!: a silly bandit at a random spot plus two of Granny with a pie, the Sheriff and Daisy the cow. Each player's marker starts in the middle. Hitting the bandit first wins; hitting a friend is a foul ("Oops! Pip popped Granny!").
    - The tiebreak (2nd tie in a row) goes to the fastest best draw of the match.
  - **Soft fails:** an early press is a fizzle and a puff of smoke ("Too early, Pip! (that was the crow)"). Both early at once is a tie. A popped hat tumbles into the dust and its owner laughs. Nobody gets hurt.
  - **Gold help (announced in a gold chip over the helped player's panel during the round card, at the start of the pacing, and on the panel):** 1 round behind gives a 40 ms head start (your time counts 40 ms faster). 2 or more behind gives 70 ms and gold patience: your first early press in the round is forgiven ("Phew! Gold patience!"). It is off in sudden death.
  - **Scoring/medal:** no medal. The results tiles are Best draw (ms), Average draw (ms), Fouls and Bandits hit, plus a "Fastest draw of the match" line (both names on a tie) and the Gulch record: the fastest fair draw in a plain round, kept in the browser under `quickdrawbest:<name>`.
  - **Hints:** the round card (under the match pill, clear of the 3-2-1) shows the round's name and twist. "Three paces... then wait for the gold DRAW!", then "Wait for the gold DRAW!", shows under the match pill until DRAW!. Each person gets their own keys (keyboard, controller glyph or touch) in rounds 1-2, until a bandit round has reached DRAW!, and when they have gold help; on phones the chip sits high over their head. Hints are never shown for the computer.
  - **Brain:** it never presses while pacing. It may fall for each fakeout once it happens (Easy 16%, Normal 6%, Hard 2%) and then presses a reaction time later. After DRAW! it fires after a bell-curve reaction time: Easy 0.55 s ± 0.08 (slowed for young kids, Gavyn, Nov 2026), Normal 0.30 s ± 0.045, Hard 0.21 s ± 0.025. In bandit rounds it adds a moment to spot the bandit, taps ◀ ▶ one spot at a time (Easy 0.19 s a step, Hard 0.10 s) and then fires. Easy aims at a friend 10% of the time, Normal 4%, Hard never. It reads only what is on screen and never the coming DRAW! time. It keeps playing if the person walks away (`noIdleStop`). Bubbles only in 1-player mode.
  - **Bot results:** matches take about 50-110 s of sim time (about 12 s a round including the banners). Hard beats Easy 6/6 from both sides; Normal vs Normal 3-1 over 4 seeds (9-7 over 16 in the build); Normal beats Easy 6/6. Scripted people vs the computer: a masher, a player who mashes every button, one who holds the action and one who only holds right never win a round against Easy (the mashers foul every round; the match ends in about 35 s). A scripted kid (0.35-0.45 s, falls for 10% of fakeouts) beats Easy 14 of 20 and Normal 0 of 8; a slower kid (0.42-0.52 s) beats Easy 4-15 of 20.

### World 20: Dragon Peak (team boss)
The second boss world (Gavyn's rule: a boss world every 10 worlds). A volcanic sunset: dusky red and orange sky, dark basalt stone, glowing orange lava accents. One big boss level; Gavyn picked every phase himself (Nov 2026).
- **20-1 Volcano Dragon** (team, mode `dragon`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Phase'`, every room `ownStep`): Cinder, a chubby, goofy red-orange dragon (stubby wings, big eyes, a curly tail, smoke-puff hiccups), is grumpy and won't let anyone up Dragon Peak. Nobody is ever hurt, Cinder neither: lava is his hot tub. Fire breath and lava are soft fails only, a counted "Toasty!" / "Hot foot!" bonk with a short pop-back. 4 rooms = 4 phases (about 4-5 minutes for a good pair):
  - **Slope Climb** (`climb`): scrolling right and up the volcano in 4 stretches. A shielder holds a big shield over both against telegraphed fire and rolling lava rocks; a climber climbs the grip walls and holds the action at the gold hook to drop a rope ladder. Jobs swap at each flag (in stretch 1 whoever holds the action first takes the shield). Slow lava creeps behind. A shielder pushing into a big step with the shield up is told to let go and jump.
  - **Hopping Pillars** (`levers`): Cinder hops between 4 pillars in his lava tub; pull the lever under the pillar he sits on (hold the action 1.2 s) to dunk him. 4 dunks, jobs swap after each dunk (each player pulls twice and shields twice). He guards his lever with telegraphed flames and rests 0.6 s between them, and the lever won't budge for an unshielded puller during a flame or the rest after it ("hot lever"), so the pull needs the shield. Gold help: "Sleepy Cinder" after 2 misses, "Extra sleepy" after 4 (longer perches and rests, shorter flames).
  - **Gong Golf** (`golf`): World 3 golf with fire boost rings; the partner is the ball. Pip golfs Bo on hole 1, then they swap; ringing his gong ends a hole (2 holes, par 3 each). Pick-up at 10 strokes (the ball is dropped by the gong).
  - **Lava Surf Race** (`surf`): versus inside the boss level, best of 3 split-screen races down the other side (Ember Run, Wave Rapids, Geyser Gulch). Hold ▶ to surf, hop rocks and waves, POP off ramp edges, trick in the air. Whoever trails rides a gold slab; a second tie in a row is settled (tricks, POPs, bumps, then Cinder's lucky coin). Surf hits are not team bonks. In 1-player mode the computer is the opponent and uses the Opponent setting (`vdVersusSkill`).
  - At the end he hugs and waves, the volcano puffs friendly fireworks and he is their friend. R restarts from phase 1.
  - When no person presses anything for 10 s, Cinder waits ("Cinder is waiting...": no attacks, the race freezes) and the clock stops, so the time and the trophy count play time only. In 1-player mode the computer waits too and never clears a team part alone.
  - HUD: a "Dragon Peak" phase strip with pips per phase, a time pill with the phase's own count, news, Cinder's speech and per-player hint bubbles that name each player's own keys, controller buttons or touch buttons. On phones (screen scale below 0.75) the HUD uses the bigger sizes (`vdBig()`).
  - Results card: Time, Record, Bonks [Pip, Bo] and Trophy tiles, then "Surf champ: Bo (2-1). Golf strokes: 4 (par 6)." and the trophy line. Trophy (`VD.medal`, tuned with bots): Gold at 7:00 or less and 16 bonks or fewer, Silver at 11:00 and 40, otherwise Bronze. The final card celebrates beating Cinder (`showFinal` finds him by `mode === 'dragon'`).
- Code: the core `js/levels/world20/volcano-dragon.js` (MODES.dragon, the `VD_PHASES` registry with a working stub per phase, Cinder's drawing and poses, the shield and fire-breath helpers, bonks, badges, bubbles, gold help, the idle wait, the HUD phase strip, the ending, stats, the test hook `MODES.dragon.test`); its header comment is the phase contract. One file per phase (`vd-climb.js`, `vd-levers.js`, `vd-golf.js`, `vd-surf.js`) replaces its stub with `VD_PHASES.<phase> = {...}` and fills in its own room with `vdRoom(phase, fields)`; each file's header explains its rules. Brains: `js/cpu/levels/world20/volcano-dragon.js` (cpuBrain('dragon') dispatching to `VD_BRAINS[phase]`, stub brains, the optional `back()` hook when the person returns) plus one per phase. Preview: `js/previews/world20.js`.
- Status: built, QA'd per phase and as a whole (keyboard, controller and touch, 2-player and 1-player, desktop and phone). Bot pair clears in about 4-5 minutes.

### World 21: Sky Kingdom (mixed)
A bright, breezy sky: fluffy clouds, floating islands, rooftops, a seaside and a cloud castle. Nothing scary, nobody gets hurt (falls are boings, bumps are wobbles). World name from Gavyn's pick of the idea list. Gavyn picked these four from eight ideas (Nov 2026); he skipped Sky Castle Elevator, Glider Relay, Rainbow Slide and Cloud Hop Battle. World index 20 (`world: 20`), right after the World 20 boss Dragon Peak. Each level's full rules are in the header comment of its level file; its brain's plan is in the brain file's header. Test scripts are in `tools/w21/`.

- **21-1 Hot Air Balloon Climb** (team, mode `balloon`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Climb'`, scrolls UP; js/levels/world21/hot-air-balloon-climb.js): both ride one balloon. The burner holds burn (▲, jump or the action) to heat the balloon and rise; letting go cools it and it sinks. Heat over the red line for about 1.1 s makes the burner sputter for 1.8 s, so pulse it. The steerer pulls the vent ropes with ◀ ▶ and the action or ▼ is "steady" (brakes the drift). Hazards: island rows with a gap (gold ▲ marks it), single islands, grumpy storm clouds (zap: drop and lose heat), bird flocks (boing down), wind bands. 64 stars and 8 party balloons. Each climb ends by landing gently on a dock (too fast bounces). Climbs: Meadow Liftoff, Windy Cliffs, Storm Clouds, Cloud Castle Dock; Pip burns 1 and 3, Bo 2 and 4. Gold help (announced): 3 bumps at one hazard (or 25 s stuck under it) turns it gold (a gold breeze to the gap, a smiling cloud, birds dodge); sinking off the bottom brings Gus the goose to tow you back, and 2 tows give a gold burner that can't overheat; 2 hard landings give a gold cushion; 18 s above the dock without landing brings a gold rope. Medals: Gold ≤ 4:30, ≤ 6 bumps, ≥ 50% stars (loosened from 4:00 and 55% for human pairs); Silver ≤ 6:00, ≤ 16 bumps. About 3:15 for a computer pair.
- **21-2 Kite Tandem** (team, mode `kite`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Stretch'`, scrolls right; js/levels/world21/kite-tandem.js): the runner holds ▶ to run with the string, ▲ jumps chimneys, crates, fences and gaps, and ▼ or the action ("reel") pulls the kite lower; letting go lets the string out. The kite only flies while the runner keeps running. The rider leans ▲ ▼ a little and slides ◀ ▶ along the string; the action ("tuck") is a spin that lets birds past. Collect stars and ring gates (3 stars); dodge power lines, washing lines, birds, trees, spires, masts, windmill sails, balloons, and fly under cloud arches. A hit is "Tangled!" (a wobble and a short slowdown); a gap fall is "Whoops!" (an awning or lily pad boings the runner back). Each stretch ends at a tower whose flag only the rider can grab (string all the way out and leaning ▲), then the kite lands on the pad and they swap jobs. Stretches: Rooftop Run, Windy Hill, Seaside Pier, Bridge to the Sky Kingdom; Pip runs 1 and 3. Gold help: a gold gust past an obstacle group after 2 tangles there (all groups after 4 in a stretch), a gold plank after 2 whoopses at a gap, a gold gust on the pad after 12 s without the flag. Medals: Gold ≤ 3:30, ≤ 5 tangles + whoopses, ≥ 40% of the stars (was half; kid pairs get about 44%); Silver ≤ 5:30, ≤ 14. About 2:55 for a good pair.
- **21-3 Cloud Bridge Builder** (team, mode `cloudbridge`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Stretch'`, scrolls right; js/levels/world21/cloud-bridge-builder.js): the caller walks to an island edge, holds the action ("call") to show a ghost flag, nudges it with ▲ ▼ (2 steps) and lets go to plant it (2 flags at most). The builder presses the action ("puff") to send a puff from the bellows to the oldest flag within 300 px; it becomes a cloud you stand on (and can jump up through). Clouds melt after 6 s (1.8x faster in sunshine). Wind gusts (calm 3 s, 1 s warning, 2.2 s wind) blow puffs away. A fall is a "Boing" back to the last island. Also a rainbow spring and a cloud lift (walk into its side to call it down). Stretches: First Puffs, Stairway Up, Windy Gap, Sunny Melt to the Cloud Castle; Pip calls 1 and 3. Gold help: a gap turns gold after 2 Boings, 3 blown puffs or 32 s stuck (gold clouds last 18 s, gold puffs ignore wind). Medals: Gold ≤ 3:30 and ≤ 3 Boings; Silver ≤ 5:30 and ≤ 10. About 2:16 for a computer pair, 3-4 min for kids.
- **21-4 Paper Plane Race** (versus, best of 5, 75 s rounds, mode `paperplane`, scrolls right; js/levels/world21/paper-plane-race.js): each round starts with a throw: hold the action, a power meter swings, let go near the top (gold zone = "Perfect throw!"; auto-throw after 3.5 s). Then ▲ ▼ tilt the nose: diving gains speed, climbing costs it, a slow climb stalls. Gold warm-air columns lift you, gold rings give a burst, and the action is a flap (3 a round). Birds, balloons, kites, trees, roofs, the blimp, masts and hanging clouds are bumps (half speed, a crumple); touching the ground is a skid. First through the finish arch wins; at 75 s the plane farther along wins (a near-tie replays; tiebreak is rings). Courses: Meadow Breeze, Rooftop Run, Seaside Soar, Cloud Castle, Rainbow Final. Catch-up (gold, announced): the trailer is pulled along at the screen's left edge and gets a gold tailwind when 450 px behind; 1 round behind gives an extra flap, 2+ gives 2 flaps and gold wings. Hard beat Easy every match from both sides; rounds take 30-45 s.
### World 22: Coral Deep (mixed)
A bright, friendly underwater world: sunny shallows, coral reefs, kelp, a sandy seabed, a cosy sunken ship and dolphins at the surface. Nothing scary, nobody gets hurt (bumps are "Bonk!"s and wobbles, running out of air is a "Blub!" bubble ride home). World name "Coral Deep" is Claude's suggestion; Gavyn chose the underwater theme. Gavyn picked these four from eight ideas (Nov 2026); he skipped Bubble Buddies, Jellyfish Bounce, Whale Ride and Crab Claw Clash. World index 21 (`world: 21`), right after World 21 Sky Kingdom. Each level's full rules are in the header comment of its level file; its brain's plan is in the brain file's header. All three team levels have a shared `MODES.<mode>.plan(st)` that drives the prompts, the HUD job panels, the gold help and the computer brain. Test scripts are in `tools/w22/`.

- **22-1 Submarine Duo** (team, mode `subduo`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Stretch'`, scrolls right; js/levels/world22/submarine-duo.js): both ride one little yellow-and-teal sub through a reef canyon. The steerer's ◀ ▶ ▲ ▼ push the sub with gentle water physics, and the action ("toot") is a horn that sends sleepy pufferfish floating out of the way and scatters fish. Hitting coral, rock, a turtle or a puffer at speed is a soft "Bonk!" (a wobble and a bounce); slow touches just slide. The grabber's ◀ ▶ ▲ ▼ move the claw on its arm (reach about 165 px), and the action grabs and presses again to let go. Jobs: pearls and shells (62 in all, Clammy's big pearls are worth 3); boulder pinches (lift the boulder out and lower it into the sandy hole, "Plop!", or carry it forward through the pinch and let go on the far side, "Out of the way!"); floaty logs (grab high, pull down while the steerer backs up, let go when nearly flat or it floats back up); Clammy the sleepy giant clam (bring her the conch and she yawns open). Each stretch ends when the claw clips a buoy ring (the last one a treasure chest), then they hop seats and swap jobs. Stretches: Sunny Shallows, Kelp Forest, Coral Canyon, Treasure Grotto; Pip steers 1 and 3. Gold help (announced, only while both are playing): 35 s at a blocker or 3 drops or slips there gives a gold boulder that rolls into its hole, a light gold log that never slips, or a gold conch that floats to the claw; 4 bonks in one spot make bumps there soft; 20 s at the end gives a gold ring or handle. Idle: 12 s with no person pressing anything is "Taking a break" and the clock stops (G.time is wound back, so test loops must count steps, not G.time). Medals: Gold ≤ 4:30, ≤ 6 bonks and ≥ 50% of pearls; Silver ≤ 7:00 and ≤ 16 bonks. Bots: computer pair 3:08 (Gold); a kid with the computer about 3:47; kid pairs about 4:27; slow-kid pairs 6-7.5 min (Bronze). One player alone, idle, mash+mash and a masher with the computer never clear in 10 min. Tests: tools/w22/subduo-sim.js, subduo-break.js, subduo-adversary.js, subduo-input.js, subduo-shots.js, subduo-live.js, subduo-people.js.
- **22-2 Treasure Diver Rope** (team, mode `diverope`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Dive'`, scrolls DOWN; js/levels/world22/treasure-diver-rope.js): a little boat on top, the diver in an old-timey brass helmet on an air hose. The camera follows the diver down and back up when reeled. The boat partner works the winch: ▼ lets out rope, but only while the diver pulls on it (70 px of slack at most). ▲ reels in, twice as fast when the air is red. The action pumps air, but only while the winch is still; each press adds air, with less for very fast presses, so a rhythm and a mash both work. The diver can't swim up (heavy boots). ◀ ▶ swim, ▲ is a little kick off rock or sand, ▼ sinks faster and, held while the hose is tight, tugs for more rope. Gems, starfish and pearls go into a net bag by touch. The action hooks the chest. Treasure counts only once it's back in the boat, and a dive ends when the chest is hauled in. Air drains faster the deeper you go and refills by the boat. Out of air is a soft "Blub!": the diver rides a bubble up, keeps the bag, and the chest sinks back to the rock or sand below. Sleepy eels doze, wake ("!" 0.8 s), then stretch out about 130-190 px. A touch is a soft "Eek!" that drops one item from the bag. Jellyfish drift; a touch is a "Boing!". 4 dives, swapping jobs every dive: Sandy Shelf, Kelp Drop, Coral Cliffs, Deep Trench (Pip dives 1 and 3). 64 treasures. Gold help (announced, only while both are pressing things): 2 frights from one eel give it a gold nightcap; 2 boings from one jelly turn it gold; 2 blubs, or the air running low 3 times before the chest, give a gold air hose (half drain); still running out after that gives a gold pump (air stays full that dive); a boat partner pressing things but not giving the rope the dive needs for 14 s gives a gold winch; no progress for 24 s gives a gold current. Gold sparkles point to the chest after 35 s (not counted as help). The plan waits for awake eels and drifting jellies, and holds the rope for pumping when the air is under half (only while someone pumps). Medals: Gold ≤ 4:00, ≥ 70% treasure, ≤ 5 bumps + blubs, ≤ 1 help; Silver ≤ 5:30, ≤ 14 bumps. About 2:55 for a computer pair; slow or noisy pairs 3:15-3:40; a boat partner who never pumps about 5 min (Silver); a masher with the computer 7.5-9 min (Bronze). One player alone, an idle partner or two mashers can't clear it. Tests: tools/w22/diverope-break.js, diverope-adversarial.js, diverope-shots.js.
- **22-3 Shipwreck Lights** (team, mode `shipwreck`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Section'`, scrolls right; js/levels/world22/shipwreck-lights.js): Pip and Bo swim freely (no gravity) through a dark but cosy sunken ship: sleeping crabs, a hermit crab, glowing plankton, portholes. The darkness is a soft deep-blue overlay (about 0.8) with light circles. Hidden things (levers, wheel valves, keys, treasure, prickly coral, urchins) only show inside the light. The lantern carries Glimmer the lantern fish (light radius 160). Its action is a flash (radius 330, 3 s recharge) that wakes sleepy glowing anemones (switches) and shoos grumpy pufferfish out of hatches ("Eek!"). The grabber's claw works only in the light: tap to pull levers; hold about 1.3 s to turn wheels and to open the final chest; swim into keys and treasure; take a key to its keyhole; push crates out of low tunnels (a cleared crate can't be pushed back in). In the dark: "Too dark! Bring Glimmer here!". The lantern can't open, carry or push anything. Doors open when all their switches are on; both swim out the exit door. Sections: Cargo Hold, Galley, Captain's Cabin, Treasure Room (ends at the big chest); Pip lights 1 and 3. 61 treasures. Soft fails: coral, urchins and pufferfish are a "Bonk!" (wobble, push back), only counted. Leash 600 px. A faint dashed outline stays where a lever, wheel or key was seen. Gold help (announced): 3 bonks on one prickly thing turn it gold (no more bonks), 10 in a section turn all of them gold; 40 s stuck on a step (someone pressing keys) turns the needed thing gold (works in the dark; a gold anemone or pufferfish reacts when Glimmer swims near; a gold crate slides easily); a hint line after 22 s without progress. The clock rests only when every person has been idle 15 s ("RESTING" chip; a lantern keeping still to hold the light counts as playing). Medals: Gold ≤ 3:45, ≤ 5 bonks, ≥ 60% treasure; Silver ≤ 5:30, ≤ 14 bonks. Bot times: perfect computer pair 2:33 (Gold); noisy 3:06-3:26 (Gold); slow person 4:05-4:55 (Silver); a masher with the computer about 9.5 min (Bronze). The brain plays either role with grid paths that steer round urchins; as lantern it knows where the hidden things are (noted in its header). Tests: tools/w22/shipwreck-sim.js, shipwreck-break.js, shipwreck-input.js, shipwreck-shots.js, shipwreck-qa.js. Darkness: Gavyn asked (Nov 2026) for it much darker so the lantern matters: a near-black overlay (about 0.96), only faint glows round the players and portholes.
- **22-4 Dolphin Race** (versus, best of 5, 70 s rounds, mode `dolphinrace`, `ownStep`, scrolls right; js/levels/world22/dolphin-race.js): Pip (lilac dolphin) and Bo (teal dolphin) ride side-on wearing bubble helmets. Dolphins swim right by themselves (cruise 255). ▲ swims up, ▼ dives, and letting go drifts back to your own start lane (one shallow, one deeper; they swap every round). Holding ▲ from below pops out in a little hop. The action is a leap: a tail-flick rush that goes higher the deeper you start (about 80-160 px). It refills in 1.6 s (ring on the panel); in the air it's a flip, just for show. A leap within 0.6 s of GO is a "Great start!". Gold hoops give +145 for 0.85 s: low ones need any hop, high ones need a dive then a leap. Fast currents (mostly deep) add +90. Seaweed ("Tangled!"), kelp gates, buoys, rocks, sleepy turtles, sailboats and gulls are bumps: half speed and a wobble, nobody gets hurt. Big leaps land with a 4% splash, and dolphins touching go "Bonk!" (3%). Courses: Lagoon Splash, Reef Run, Kelp Maze, Sunset Bay, Grand Final, built from tokens with the mode's seeded rand. First through the coral arch wins; at time-up the dolphin farther along wins (within 12 px is a tie and the round replays); tiebreak is more hoops. Catch-up (gold, announced): the trailer is pulled along at the screen's left edge and gets a gold current (+40) when 450 px behind; 1 round behind = gold fins (leap refills in 1.0 s, hoops +25%), 2+ behind adds a gold head start; all help is off in sudden death. Results tiles: Rounds won, Gold hoops, Best jump (m), Bumps, plus fastest-round and hoop-star lines. The brain re-plans by simulating short ▲/none/▼ plans, with or without a leap, using the level's own movement rule. Easy (0.42 s reactions, sees 65% of obstacles, goes for 22% of hoops) is beaten by a kid-like scripted player 14/24, but a random-pressing young kid loses to it. Hard and Normal beat Easy every match. Rounds take about 26 s (Hard), 31 s (Normal), 43 s (Easy). Tests: tools/w22/dolphinrace-{sim,break,solo,input,shots,edge}.js.
- Code: level files in `js/levels/world22/`, brains in `js/cpu/levels/world22/` (each registered with `cpuBrain('<mode>', ...)`), defs in the World 22 block of `js/levels/level-list.js` (one BEGIN/END section per level), previews in `js/previews/world22.js` (helpers `pw22s`, `pw22d`, `pw22w`, `pw22r`). Top-level names: `subduo*`/`SUBD*`, `diverope*`/`DIVEROPE*`/`DROPE`, `SHIPWRECK` (one object), `dlr*`/`DLR*`; sounds under `Sfx.extra.<mode>*`.
- Status: built, reviewed and QA'd per level (keyboard, controller and touch at 844x390 and 390x844, 2-player and 1-player). `node tools/w22/integration.js` checks the world map and World 22 page (all 4 previews animate), plays 22-1 to 22-4 in order to the world-done card, loads every level of every world without errors, and checks `build/pip-and-bo.html` (run `node tools/bundle.js` first) starts 22-1. A computer pair plays the whole world in about 11 minutes.
- Testing tip: phone tests should use `hasTouch` with a phone-sized viewport, not `isMobile: true` (index.html has no viewport meta tag, since the publisher adds it, so `isMobile` lays the page out 980 px wide and never shows the big phone HUD).

### World 23: Barnyard Fair (mixed)
A sunny farm and county fair: red barns, hay fields, pig pens, a hot dog stand and a pie contest on the fair stage, with a Ferris wheel in the distance. Nothing scary, nobody gets hurt (bumps are "Oof!"s, spills are funny, a customer who waits too long just says "Maybe later!"). Gavyn chose the theme from five ideas (Toy Box Town, Hero City, Barnyard Fair, Mega Mall, Robot Factory) and picked these four from ten level ideas (Nov 2026); he skipped Ferris Wheel Fix, Cow Milking Duet, Scarecrow Crow Patrol, Hayride Escape, Chicken Egg Catch and Pig Race. World name is his pick from the list. World index 22 (`world: 22`), in the WORLDS and LEVELS literals right after World 22. Each level's full rules are in the header comment of its level file; its brain's plan is in the brain file's header. Test scripts are in `tools/w23/`. The four levels were built side by side, then each was played by a second agent with scripted "people" (slow kids, mashers, job-stealers, wrong-way walkers, idlers) who tried to break it.

- **23-1 Tractor & Trailer** (team, mode `haytractor`, ownStep, ownHUD, noFalls, roomWord 'Field'; js/levels/world23/tractor-trailer.js): a side view that scrolls right. A little red tractor pulls a hay wagon across 4 fields: Hay Meadow, Pumpkin Patch, Bumpy Hills and Sunset Lane, which ends at the Big Barn at the fair. One player drives: ▶ is the gas, ◀ or ▼ brakes, ◀ from a stop backs up, and the action honks. The other stands on the wagon and catches what farmers, kids, a baler and a pumpkin catapult toss from the fields: ◀ ▶ walk, ▲ jumps for the high baler bales, and holding the action steadies the load. Tossers aim for the field's pace (145 to 160 px/s). A missed item lies on the road for 7 s and can be scooped up: the wagon has to go under 95 px/s and the catcher stands over the item. Bumps come in two sizes, small (rocks, ruts, planks) and big (humps, holes, logs), each with a yellow warning sign. Crossing one under its safe speed (100 or 72) does nothing ("Nice and slow!"). A faster crossing adds to the wobble meter; pumpkins and a tall stack add more, and holding halves it. When the meter is full the top 2 + n/6 items spill off. Sheep and geese block the road until you honk, or leave on their own after 6 s. A crow lands on the load and takes the top item after 5 s unless you honk. At each barn the load is counted in one item at a time, then the two swap jobs with a hop (Pip drives fields 1 and 3, Bo drives 2 and 4). Gold help appears once per field and only while both players are trying: a gold rope after 3 spills (the load can't spill), gold hay after 6 misses in a row (tosses glow, fall slower and aim at the catcher), a gold scarecrow after 2 crow steals, and a gold engine when the driver keeps pressing but the tractor hasn't got 300 px further in 20 s (it putters on at 62 px/s, under every bump's safe speed; ▶ still goes faster, it can't back up, and it only runs while both players are playing). Nothing fails hard. If no person presses anything for 15 s, the game shows "Taking a break..." and the clock stops. There are 84 items, 9 of them golden corn. The results card shows 4 tiles: Delivered, Spills, Gold corn and Medal. Gold needs ≤ 3:45 and ≥ 70% delivered, Silver ≤ 6:30 and ≥ 50%, anything else is Bronze. The computer partner reads the same plan as the prompts and the HUD, and can drive or catch. Typical times: computer pair 201 s with 79/84 delivered and 0 spills (Gold); noisy or clumsy computer pair 242 to 256 s; test "kid" pair 222 to 239 s with 77 to 80 delivered (Gold or Silver); kid with the computer 211 to 217 s (Gold); slow kids 364 to 372 s (Silver); a random masher, a ◀-holder or an only-honker with the computer 350 to 395 s (Silver or Bronze, with the gold engine); two mashers about 405 s (Bronze).
- **23-2 Runaway Pigs** (team, mode `pigherd`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Chase'`, top-down, scrolls right; js/levels/world23/runaway-pigs.js): a farm seen from above, in four chases: Barnyard, Duck Pond, Cornfield Maze and Fairground. Both players walk in four directions (▲ or jump is up) and climb over gates, so gates only stop pigs. Slippery, skidding pigs (4-6 a chase, all named) run from the HERDER, whose action is 'clap': every pig within 200 px dashes away, and a clap close by (120 px) gets a pig out of the mud. The pigs ignore the GATEKEEPER, whose action is 'gate' and works the gate or lever within 54 px. The gatekeeper opens forward gates and shuts side gates. An open side gate lets curious pigs into the corn, the duck pond, the veg patch or the pie tent. That counts as a wrong turn, and after 5.5 s Farmer Fran shoos the pig back ("Shoo, piggy!"). The gatekeeper also flips the fork lever (one way is a dead end), rolls the hay bale into a broken fence (only the gatekeeper can push it) and pulls the barn door rope (the door shuts again after 6.5 s, but never on a pig). Last comes the pen: open it, then shut it once every pig is in ("All in!", confetti). Roles swap each chase (Pip herds 1 and 3). The last pen is at the fair, where the pigs win "Best Pigs". Gold help is always announced and only comes while both are playing. That means a press in the last 10 s; in 2-player a gatekeeper waiting by a gate counts as playing for 90 s. The help: a gold ribbon after 40 s with no headway or 25 s in the mud (the pig trots home along the track and still waits at shut gates; ribbons count as one help per chase); a gold arrow after 2 wrong turns or 22 s on one gate job; a gold gate after 3 wrong turns through the same side gate or down the same wrong fork (the gate shuts or the lever flips by itself, and the hay rolls in by itself); a gold gate when the herd waits 25 s at a shut gate, fork, barn door or pen (it opens and stays open); the pen gate shuts itself 20 s after every pig is in; and gold oats after 85 s in a chase or 30 s with the herder far off (slower pigs). The gatekeeper can't undo a gold gate or lever. The clock rests when every person has pressed nothing for 15 s. Leash: 800 px. A gatekeeper running ahead waits ("Wait for Bo!"), and a herder going back for a straggler pulls the gatekeeper along ("Come on, Bo!"). The computer herder keeps playing for 90 s without presses from a person keeping the gates (brain `idleStop: 90`), but it still can't finish alone. Medal: Gold at ≤ 3:45 with ≤ 3 wrong turns and ≤ 1 gold help; Silver at ≤ 5:45 with ≤ 9 wrong turns; otherwise Bronze. Results tiles: Pigs penned, Wrong turns, Gold help, Medal, plus a line with the quickest chase and the claps. Bot times: computer pair about 2:18 (132-141 s, Gold); noisy partner 2:25-3:40; a slow kid with the computer 5:40-6:15 (Silver/Bronze); a slow-kid pair 8-10 min; a young masher with the computer 8.5-14 min (Bronze); two mashers 13-18 min. An idle person never clears. Tests: tools/w23/pigherd-sim.js, pigherd-break.js, pigherd-people.js, pigherd-edge.js, pigherd-input.js, pigherd-load.js, pigherd-shots.js.
- **23-3 Hot Dog Stand Rush** (team, mode `hotdog`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `noTime`, `roomWord: 'Shift'`, one screen; js/levels/world23/hot-dog-stand-rush.js): a side view inside a striped hot dog stand at the county fair. A divider keeps the cook in the kitchen on the left and the server at the counter on the right; both can reach the pass shelf. The cook's action ('cook') works at the 4-spot grill, the bun basket, the topping bottles and the shelf. Ketchup and mustard come first, then relish, onions and cheese in later shifts, and a sign over each bottle shows a mini hot dog with that topping. A sausage is raw for 4 s, golden for 8.5 s (a red "!" blinks at the end), then burnt. Burnt sausages and wrong hot dogs go to Biscuit the farm dog ("Woof! Yum!"), which costs only time. The arrow asks for at most 2 sausages cooking at once. A wanted hot dog put on a full shelf swaps places with one nobody needs, and that one goes to Biscuit. The server's action ('serve') takes the orders of fairgoers at 3 window spots (a farmer, a cowboy, a granny, a cow in a straw hat, a pig...). Taking an order pins a picture ticket on the kitchen board and gives back 25% patience. The server carries 2 items, picks the needed hot dog off the shelf and hands it over: a right one gives +12% patience and "Yum!" with 1-3 stars, a wrong one gets a kind "Not mine!". With full hands and a full shelf, the server swaps a wrong hot dog for a wanted one. From Lunch Rush on, the server also pours lemonade: hold the action, let go in the 66-90% green band; going over is a funny spill. With full hands, the action at the jug pours a spare lemonade back. Patience rings go green, yellow, red; when one runs out the fairgoer says "Maybe later!" and counts as missed. People still in line don't lose patience. Once 2 or more wait beyond the window, the next order is one hot dog bigger. Shifts: Morning 40 s (goal 4), Lunch Rush 44 s (5), Afternoon 44 s (4), County Fair Finale 50 s (6, with a hay-ride bus of 4 fairgoers). Then up to 10 s of "Last orders"; anyone still waiting goes home and isn't counted. Pip cooks in shifts 1 and 3. Gold help, always announced with a news line and a HUD chip: 2 walk-offs in a row ring the gold bell (full patience, ×0.65 drain for the rest of the shift). 2 burns in a row or 3 in one shift give the gold timer (a countdown ring, golden lasts 12 s). 2 spills in a row give the gold jug (it stops in the green). The timer and the jug stay once earned. A gold hint line appears after 20 s stuck. Rest: 15 s with no person pressing anything is "Taking a break". In 1-player mode, a person who has had a job for 30 s and done none of it gets "The stand waits for Bo: Take the order!" until one useful press. So the computer never runs a shift alone. Medals: Gold needs ≥ 85% happy, 3 of 4 shift goals and 2 stars a customer; Silver needs ≥ 60% happy and at least 10 happy; otherwise Bronze. Bot times: a computer pair takes about 4:00 (237-243 s) and gets Gold with 24-26 happy and 0 missed. Noisy pairs also get Gold. A slow kid with the computer gets Silver or Bronze (9-13 happy), slow-kid pairs get Bronze (5-8 happy), and a masher with the computer gets Bronze. Idle, stand-still and key-leaning people never finish. Tests: tools/w23/hotdog-rules.js, hotdog-break.js, hotdog-sim.js, hotdog-input.js, hotdog-shots.js, hotdog-integration.js, hotdog-trace.js.
- **23-4 Pie-Eating Contest** (versus, mode `pieeat`, best of 5, one screen, `ownStep`; js/levels/world23/pie-eating-contest.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world23/pie-eating-contest.js): Pip and Bo sit at the judges' table on the fair stage with their hands behind their backs. Farmer Fern judges, and the crowd claps a beat that a shrinking circle shows on each player's cheek ring. **Controls:** the action key takes a bite into your cheeks (they hold 6). ▲ (W / Space / ↑ / A / stick up / touch ▲) gulps. **Gulping:** a gulp on the clap (from 0.13 s early to 0.2 s late) is a GULP!: you swallow everything plus 2 bonus bites off the plate, and your tummy calms a little. A gulp off the beat is a "Glug!": you swallow only a third (rounded up), cough for 0.5 s and add 32% air (15% with empty cheeks). **Hiccups:** biting faster than 0.17 / 0.25 / 0.32 s apart adds 32 / 16 / 5% to the hiccup jar, and biting with full cheeks adds 38%. The jar drains 26% a second. A full jar is HIC!: 1.6 s of bouncing with no eating. **Rounds:** the first to swallow every pie wins. At 60 s, whoever ate more wins. The rounds are Cherry (3×26 bites, claps every 1.5 s), Blueberry (1.45 s), Apple (1.3 s), Giant Pumpkin (2×38) and Triple Berry Final (the claps speed up from 1.45 to 1.25 s). Sudden death is a one-pie Sudden Slurp (30 bites), and the tiebreak is more GULP!s. **Gold help (always announced):** one round behind gives gold gulps (a 0.18 / 0.28 s window). Two or more behind also gives a gold tummy (the jar fills 30% slower). There is no help in sudden death. **Results tiles:** Rounds won, Pies eaten, GULP!s, Hiccups, plus best-streak and calmest-tummy lines. **Brain:** steady bites (gap: Hard 0.26 s, Normal 0.31 s, Easy 0.56 s) and a gulp planned for the clap (timing error: Hard ±0.045 s, Normal ±0.08 s, Easy ±0.18 s). Easy also has fast-bite bursts, forgets one clap in four and has short lapses. **Bots (10 seeds):** Hard beats Normal and Easy 30-0 in rounds, no side bias (mirrors over 60 seeds: Normal 137-118, Hard 117-129, Easy 138-141). A calm kid beats Easy 30-3 / 30-5. A slow young kid takes 16-26 and a random presser 8-29 / 13-29 (about a quarter of the rounds, sometimes a match). Mashers lose to Easy (7-30, 12-30) and to Normal (0-30). A steady presser who ignores the claps beats Easy 30-6, splits with a calm kid 21-26 and loses 0-30 to Normal and to a timing grown-up. A good grown-up splits with Normal 22-22, and a pro takes 15 of 43 rounds from Hard. Rounds last about 18 s (Hard), 20 s (Normal), 33 s (a calm kid) and 42-44 s (Easy). Tests: tools/w23/pieeat-{rules,input,break,sim,load,shots,rev}.js.

### World 24: Toy Box Town (mixed)
Pip and Bo are toy-sized in a kid's bedroom: a cosy carpet, a bed, a tall bookshelf, a toy box and a night light. Nothing scary, nobody gets hurt (bumps are "Clunk!"s, "Boing!"s, "Whoa!"s and "Fwump!"s). Gavyn picked the theme and these four levels from ten ideas (Nov 2026); he skipped RC Car Chase, Teddy Zipline, Lights Out Tidy-Up, Marble Run Maker, Toy Soldier March and Slot Car Grand Prix. World index 23 (`world: 23`). Like World 25, the world registers itself from `js/levels/world24/world.js` (`WORLDS[23] = {...}` and `LEVELS.push(...)`, one BEGIN/END section per level def), not from the WORLDS literal and LEVELS list in level-list.js, so it never conflicted with World 23 Barnyard Fair being built in another branch. It loads after the four World 24 level files and before World 25, so its levels sort before 25-1. Each level's full rules are in the header comment of its level file, and its brain's plan is in the brain file's header. Test scripts are in `tools/w24/`.

- **24-1 Wind-Up Train Ride** (team, mode `windtrain`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Stretch'`, scrolls right; js/levels/world24/wind-up-train-ride.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world24/wind-up-train-ride.js): a wind-up toy train (an engine, a wagon and a caboose) chugs right along a wooden toy track. THE WINDER rides on the engine roof. The action (or ▲) winds the brass key. Presses 0.3-0.7 s apart are full clicks, and the beat ring turns gold in that window. Slower presses wind a bit less, and mashing (under 0.22 s apart) winds only a little. Winding a full spring is a harmless "Sproing!" with about 1 s of no winding. ▼ is the brake. Speed follows the spring (vmax·min(1, 0.45+0.9·spring)), and an empty spring stops the train ("Wind me up! Press F on the gold ring"). The prompt over the engine says when to brake, when to hold the brake at a stop, and "Let go of ▼ to roll on" when the brake is held short of a stop. THE SWITCHER runs and jumps on the floor, the decks and the car roofs (▼ drops through a deck). The action ('switch') does these jobs: it flips levers away from dead ends (some switches have a bonus branch, and a lever locks once the engine is on it); kicks toy blocks, domino rows and dust bunnies off the rails; pulls the bookmark that lowers the drawbridge book; helps passengers aboard (Teddy, Duck, Robot and Bunny; the train must be stopped with the brake on and a wagon at the platform, and if not, the pop says "Stop the train first!", "Brake on, please!" or "Roll a bit closer!"); and rings the end bell, again with the train braked in the zone. The switcher's prompt names the job and the way to go ("To the dominoes ▶ D", "To the bell →"). Then Pip and Bo swap jobs. Four stretches: Rug Run; Under the Bed (dim, roaming dust bunnies that cause "Achoo!", a sock tunnel, a yo-yo); Bookshelf Bridge (book ramps, the drawbridge, a metronome); Toy Box Station (the finale: the toy box opens and the passengers hop in). Pip winds stretches 1 and 3, Bo 2 and 4. Each stretch opens (once the intro card has gone, timed on `st.goT`) with a hint for each player. A soft "Clunk!" happens when the train hits a block, a dead end, the raised bridge, the end buffer or a bouncing toy: the train stops and backs up, and the same thing counts at most once per 5 s. The plan holds the brake on whenever the train is stopped or creeping within 70 px of something in the way, so following the prompts never creeps into it. A switcher who falls into the shelf gap or is left behind pops back onto the caboose ("Boing!"). Gold help is announced and fires only while both players are pressing things: a block hit twice, or after 18 s waiting, turns gold and rolls away; a dead end, or 18 s at a wrong lever, gives gold lever arrows and longer reach; 2 falls, or 25 s, lay a gold ruler over the gap; a bouncing toy takes a gold nap after 2 clunks, or after 15 s of the train waiting just short of it; 3 sneezes put the dust bunnies to sleep in gold nightcaps; a winder who keeps winding but crawls for 20 s gets a gold key; hitting the end buffer twice gives a gold brake, and 15 s stopped at the end gives a gold bell. Idle: after 12 s with no person pressing anything, "Taking a break" and the clock stops; a rolling train coasts to a stop but never through an obstacle. Medals (`WTR_MEDAL`): Gold ≤ 240 s, ≤ 4 clunks and ≥ 50% of the 92 stars; Silver ≤ 390 s and ≤ 12 clunks. Results tiles: Time, Stars (of 92), Clunks, Passengers, Medal, plus an on-beat % line and a gold-help line. Sign text is 20 px on phones (hudBig). Bot times: computer pair 196 s (Gold, 0 clunks, 86/92 stars); noisy 205-212 s; idle never clears. Scripted kid pairs 213-222 s (Gold, 3-4 clunks), slow kid + kid about 4:35 (Silver), slow-kid pairs 5:10-5:30 (Silver), a kid with the computer 201-211 s (Gold). Full play-throughs on the real input paths: kid pairs 3:33-3:39 (Gold), 1-player with a kid 3:26-3:28 (Gold). A winder who never brakes, brakes too early or winds in bursts still clears (Silver or Gold); a switcher who waits 20 s at every job about 5-6 min (Silver). Never clear: idle, one side idle, holding, act only, wrong job, holding ◀, running to the right edge, riding the caboose; two mashers don't clear in 600 s (a masher with the computer reaches stretch 4). Tests: tools/w24/windtrain-sim.js, windtrain-break.js (with windtrain-people.js), windtrain-adversary.js, windtrain-input.js, windtrain-qa.js (plays the whole level through real keyboard, controller and touch input in 7 setups), windtrain-shots.js, windtrain-debug.js. Preview helpers `pw24w`.
- **24-2 Jack-in-the-Box Launch** (team, mode `jackbox`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Shelf'`, scrolls UP; js/levels/world24/jack-in-the-box-launch.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world24/jack-in-the-box-launch.js): 4 shelves: Bottom Shelf, Toy Shelf, Night Light Shelf and Top of the Bookshelf. Each shelf has 2 launches and 10 stars, and the tunes have 8, 10, 10 and 12 notes. In the way: a ribbon; then a toy car and a desk fan; then a mobile and a cat's tail; then a fan, ribbon, car and mobile, with a trophy at the top. On each launch one player is the CRANKER and the other is the JUMPER inside the box; they swap after every launch, and Pip jumps first on every shelf. CRANKER: presses the action on the beat (a 0.55 s beat ball plus a tick). One note counts per beat: Perfect (within 0.1 s) adds 1, Good (within 0.2 s) 0.62, Off-beat 0.25. Extra presses only rattle the box, so mashing is no faster and gives weak pops. The last note pops the lid. Power = 0.42 + 0.58 × how good the tune was. ◀ ▶ tilt the box up to 3 steps each way, and a dotted arc shows the pop with no steering. JUMPER: holds ▼ to squish the spring (+0.06 power). In the air ◀ ▶ steer, and ▲ gives one hop per launch that sets the climb speed to 420 (best at the top; only a fresh press in the air counts, and an early press says "Not yet!" without using it up). Shelves are one-way platforms. Landing back on the start shelf, or falling below it, is a "Boing!" pillow, and the tune starts again. On the new shelf the jumper walks to the gold hook and drops the yo-yo; the cranker presses the action to hook the box and hops in, then the jumper hauls with about 10 presses (presses closer than 0.11 s count half). The car and the mobile bump you, the cat's tail swishes you, the fan pushes, and the ribbon boosts you up. Gold help is announced and only comes while both players pressed something in the last 6 s; a skipped help comes at the next boing, always in this order: gold spring (+0.24) after 2 boings on a launch, gold landing pad (+50 px, plus a pull toward the landing spot) after 3, gold calm after 4. A gold yo-yo hook rolls to the jumper after 14 s in the yo-yo phase while the jumper is pressing. Idle: 12 s with no person pressing is "Taking a break" and the clock stops; the clock also stops at the top, so the results time matches the HUD clock and medal. Medals: Gold ≤ 225 s, ≤ 3 boings, ≥ 55% of stars and no help; Silver ≤ 360 s and ≤ 10 boings. Results tiles: Time, Record, Stars, Boings, Best launch (cm), Medal, plus lines on perfect notes, bumps and help. Phone HUD at 1.3× via hudBig (tune bar, power bar with a shelf mark, job panels with key chips, climb gauge); per-role act labels (crank/hook for the cranker, haul/yo-yo for the jumper). About 27 `Sfx.extra.jackbox*` sounds. Bot times: computer pair 112 s; noisy 124-137 s; idle never clears. QA play-throughs on real input: 2 players on keyboard 1:55 (Gold), controllers and touch about 2:00, 1-player 1:49-1:52 (Gold). Scripted people: slow Pip/Bo 147/176 s, offbeat cranker 124 s, masher with the computer 140-154 s (Silver), a wrong-aiming cranker with a non-steering jumper 215 s (Bronze), two mashers 355 s (Bronze). One side idle, holding, wrong job, never hauling or never hooking never clear. Tests: tools/w24/jackbox-break.js (16 scenarios), jackbox-adversary.js (15 people), jackbox-shots.js, jackbox-solo.js, jackbox-qa.js. Preview helpers `pw24j`.
- **24-3 Slinky Stairs** (team, mode `slinky`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Flight'`, scrolls DOWN; js/levels/world24/slinky-stairs.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world24/slinky-stairs.js). Gavyn's pick: "ride slinkies down a tall shelf. One sets the flip timing, the other steers to land on the next step and not off the edge." A rainbow slinky flops end over end down zig-zag toy steps inside a tall bedroom shelf. The flipper holds the back end and the steerer rides the front end. FLIPPER: hold the action (▲ also works) to stretch. The flop meter (bottom middle) swings from short to far and back (1.2 s each way), and letting go flops. A green band shows where letting go lands on the next step, allowing for where the slinky stands and where a moving step will be. A stretch must start with a press made since the last flop: pressing early is fine, but holding forever never flops. STEERER: on a step, ◀ ▶ point the way; a flop always goes the way the slinky points, so the flipper can't go anywhere alone. In the air ◀ ▶ lean the flying end about ±40 px over a flop (fine-tunes the landing and catches stars, can't save a badly timed flop). ▼ (or the steerer's action, "stick") from 0.2 s before to 0.12 s after touchdown is a "Perfect!"; only a flop with ONE press in the air can be Perfect, and holding ▼ as it lands sticks it without a Perfect. Soft trouble, counted as Whoas: a flop that lands nowhere boings back up to the step it left (a flop only lands on that step or the next). Landing near an edge, or anywhere on a tiny dice step, wobbles for 0.95 s; ▼ saves it ("Phew! Stuck it!"), otherwise it topples back. Shiny tin lids slide (hold ▼ to stop). A marble rolls along some steps (landing on it is "Bonk!"; it stands still while the slinky stands on its step). Squishy pillows sink in 7 s (a ring shows the time left), then "Squish!" back to the step before. The shelf sides bounce the flying end back. Other steps: springy (a bigger next flop), a toy truck moving sideways, a little lift moving up and down, and branches where the steerer points to pick "★ more stars" or "safer". Flights: Bookshelf Steps, Toy Block Tower, Pillow Pile, Down to the Rug (ends in the open toy box). Pip flips 1 and 3; they swap jobs every flight. Room signs are `[x, y, text, role 'F'|'R', alt]`; in 1-player mode a sign for the computer's job shows its alt text ("Bo (computer) points the way"). 164 stars (rainbow bonus stars are worth 3). Gold help (announced, only while both are pressing things and neither is mashing, about 6+ fresh presses a second): 2 whoas at one step (or 4 in a flight) give a gold guide for that flight; 3 whoas at one step make it a gold sticky step (catches 40 px further out plus 20 px per later whoa, up to 160; never wobbles or slides, its marble naps, its pillow stops sinking); a pillow squished twice turns gold; 20 s on one step while both play and the steerer presses ◀ ▶ gives a gold compass plus the guide. Idle: 12 s with no person pressing on a step is "Taking a break" and the clock stops (G.time is wound back). Medals (`slinkyMedal`): Gold ≤ 3:30, ≤ 3 whoas, ≥ 70% stars, ≥ 30 perfects, ≤ 1 help; Silver ≤ 5:30, ≤ 12 whoas. Results tiles: Stars, Perfect landings, Whoas, Medal (the engine shows Time and Record). Bot times: computer pair 2:23-2:26 (Gold, 64 perfects); noisy 166-174 s; idle never clears; a kid with the computer 2:49-2:55 (Gold); a slow kid with the computer 3:45-3:51 (Silver); kid pairs 3:15-3:24 (Gold); a slow kid with a kid 4:16-4:52; slow-kid pairs about 6 min (Bronze); a masher with the computer 3:20-4:28 (Silver/Bronze). Never clear in 10 min: one player alone, an idle partner, mash+mash, hold+hold, act+act, wrong+wrong, a steerer who only holds ◀ or only presses the action, a flipper doing the steerer's job. The brain plays either role: it lets go at the middle of the band (waiting for marbles and moving steps), takes the starry branch unless the flight has had 4 whoas, leans toward bonus stars and sticks about 0.07 s before touchdown. Tests: tools/w24/slinky-check.js (course reach), slinky-sim.js, slinky-break.js + slinky-people.js, slinky-adversary.js, slinky-gold.js, slinky-input.js (keyboard, controller or touch, any viewport, 1-player, intro, pause, until-clear, Next, world page). Preview helpers `pw24s`; globals `slk*` / `SLK*`.
- **24-4 Pillow Fort Battle** (versus, best of 5, 75 s rounds, mode `pillowfort`, `act: {label: 'toss'}`, `stomp: false`; js/levels/world24/pillow-fort-battle.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world24/pillow-fort-battle.js). Gavyn's pick: "build up your fort and toss plushies to knock the other one down". A bedroom floor with a peach rug (Pip) and a blue rug (Bo) that meet at a stitched line you can't cross. Each player has a fort of 7 soft-block columns at their own end (pillows and cushions take 1 knock, folded blankets and gold pillows 2; the blocks are real 'P' tiles you can climb). A teddy holding a flag in the player's colour sits on column 3, behind a shield column 2 blocks taller, with 5 gold stars over him (bigger on phones). The toy box in the middle pops the same toy to both sides every 3.0 s (2.5 s after 40 s); at most 16 toys lie about, then the oldest still one is tidied away. Walk over a toy on your side to pick it up (or press the action next to it); ▼ drops it. Hold the action to aim: a dotted arc shows where the toss lands, with a ring and a "Teddy!" label when it's on the teddy; longer holds go farther, it fires on its own after 2 s, and you walk slowly while aiming. A quick tap lobs at one of the other fort's front columns. A press made while dizzy, or in the 0.22 s after a toss, still counts once you can act again if still held. A hit knocks one block out and the column settles; a block knocked from under the teddy, or a direct hit, wobbles him; at 5 wobbles, or with his column gone, he topples. He gets a star back ("Steady!") after 12 s of live play without a hit. A hit on a player is a "Fwump!": dizzy 0.75 s, then safe 1.4 s (tosses fly through). Specials: the big teddy is heavy and also knocks the block behind; the bouncy ball bounces once and hits again; the feather pillow bursts into feathers. Building: press the action at your own fort while holding a pillow; it goes on the column you face, else your own column (lifting you), else the one behind you, with a see-through ghost showing the spot. It's refused when too far, when the column is over 4 blocks above your feet or 8 high, or with no room. A pillow on the teddy column takes away one wobble ("Comfy!", not in sudden death). In a pit between two walls 4+ high, a jump becomes a "Boing!" super jump. A round ends when a teddy topples; at the 75 s bell the bigger fort wins by any margin, same-size forts go to the steadier teddy (more stars), and a full match is a tie only then (the round replays; a second tie in a row goes to more blocks knocked this match, then fewer wobbles taken). Rounds: Cosy Castle first; Bouncy Ball Day, Tall Towers and Feather Frenzy shuffled; then Bedtime Battle (night light on); sudden death is Wobbly Teddies (both teddies start on 4 wobbles). Catch-up (gold, announced on the round card, outlined on the HUD panel and popped at GO; off in sudden death): 1 round behind, every building toy comes as a gold 2-hit pillow; 2+ behind, also a gold pillow on your 3 front columns. Idle: 40 s of live play with no person pressing pauses the game. First-time hint bubbles show over people for their first pickups, and key chips for the first 8 s of rounds 1-2. Results tiles: Rounds won, Hits, Blocks knocked, Blocks built, plus a toppler or master-builder star line and a Fwumps line. The brain (Easy / Normal / Hard = 0.35 / 0.7 / 1) picks a toss by sweeping charges through the real flight path (teddy 100, teddy column 70+, shield 45, other block 25), builds the shield first, then the teddy column when he's wobbly, then steps, and drops a pillow it can't place after 6 s; Easy taps, aims wobbly, has lapses, dawdles and admires its tosses. Bot times (whole match): Normal pair about 220-260 s (rounds about 49 s), Hard pair about 175-225 s (about 33 s), Easy pair about 325-405 s (almost every round to the bell); Hard beats Easy 3-0 both ways. A kid script beats Easy about 2 times in 3, never beats Normal; kid vs kid on real input about 6.5-7 min. Tests: tools/w24/pillowfort-qa.js (full play-throughs on real keyboard, controller and touch input to the World 24 done card, 9 setups), pillowfort-sim.js, pillowfort-break.js, pillowfort-input.js, pillowfort-shots.js, pillowfort-adversary.js. Preview helpers `pw24p`.
- Code: level files in `js/levels/world24/`, brains in `js/cpu/levels/world24/` (each registered with `cpuBrain('<mode>', ...)`), the world entry and the four defs in `js/levels/world24/world.js` (one BEGIN/END section per level), previews in `js/previews/world24.js` (helpers `pw24w`, `pw24j`, `pw24s`, `pw24p`). Top-level names: `wtr*`/`WTR*` (24-1), `jack*`/`JBOX*` (24-2), `slk*`/`SLK*`/`slinkyMedal` (24-3), `pfb*`/`PFB*` (24-4), `W24_STUB_MAP` (world.js); sounds under `Sfx.extra.<mode>*` (`windtrain`, `jackbox`, `slinky`, `pillowfort`). No name clashes with any other file (checked by grep at integration).
- Status: built, break-tested by a separate reviewer and QA'd per level (keyboard, controller and touch at 844x390 and 390x844, 2-player and 1-player, through the real input paths to the results card and Next level). `node tools/w24/integration.js` checks the world map (World 24 sits between World 22 and World 25), the World 24 page (eyebrow, 4 cards in order, flips to World 22 and World 25 and back, all 4 previews animate), plays 24-1 to 24-4 in order with a computer pair to the World 24 complete card, loads every level of every world without errors, and checks `build/pip-and-bo.html` (run `node tools/bundle.js` first) starts 24-1. A computer pair plays the whole world in about 11.5 minutes. When World 23 Barnyard Fair lands, its flips should land between World 22 and World 24 with no change here.

### World 25: Zombie Acres (team, one endless level)
Gavyn asked for World 25 early (Nov 2026), before Worlds 22-24 existed (they are all built now; World 24 adds itself the same way as World 25): one zombie-survival level from his own idea. A moonlit night on an old farm. Not overly cartoony (Gavyn: "a game for everyone; some animated violence is fine, silly guns can be fun"): grey-green zombies in torn clothes fall over and fade, no gore. World index 24. It adds itself from its own file (`WORLDS[24] = {...}` and `LEVELS.push(...)` at the bottom of the level file), so worlds 22-24 can go into the WORLDS literal and LEVELS list later and still sort before it; the world map simply skips the empty numbers until then.
- **25-1 Defend the Heart** (team, mode `zombiefort`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `noTime`; js/levels/world25/defend-the-heart.js, whose header has every rule): protect the glowing heart in the middle from endless waves of zombies coming from both sides.
  - Gavyn's rules: 5-minute build time before wave 1; waves get harder; upgrade weapons and the base; lose when the heart is destroyed; incentives to build wide and tall; zombies jump walls that are too short, path through as few blocks as possible and dig up, down, left and right. His answers to our questions: 15-20 minutes on average; endless, so you always lose in the end; not overly cartoony; building should feel natural; Terraria-like trees to chop (they don't block your path) and stone below the dirt; players must know every block tier and its durability; zombies knock you back and damage you, with a short respawn near the centre. He kept the names.
  - Controls: one action button. Tap shoots (hold ▼ to aim 45° down); hold works: chop a tree you stand at or face, dig the dirt or stone at your feet, place a block in front (stacks up to head height + 1; in the air it goes under you, so jump-and-hold builds a tower), and on a full wall fix the lowest cracked block or upgrade. With ▼ held: dig below-front (one hold digs down 3 deep), take back your own block below-front (refund; a turret goes back in your hands), fill a hole, or BUY on a pad (▼ + hold, so walking over a pad never buys by accident). One hold keeps doing the job it started with; an upgrade paid in coins ends the hold. You can't build on a standing tree's trunk, in the zombie lanes, or in the 3 cells above a pad.
  - Blocks (shown on screen in build time and on the build cursor): Dirt 2 HP, Wood 5, Stone 10, Metal 20, Turret 10. New blocks use the team's strongest material (stone, wood, dirt). Upgrades: dirt to wood (1 wood, or 2 coins when out of wood), wood to stone (1 stone, or 3 coins), stone to metal (6 coins). The coin prices give late-game coins a use. Shared dirt, wood, stone and coins.
  - Pads by the heart: Guns (each player's own: Pistol, SMG 12, Shotgun 25, Laser rifle 45, Rocket launcher 70 coins), Turret (15 coins, placed like a block, max 6), and the heart itself heals it (+20 HP for 8 coins).
  - Zombies: walker, digger (likes digging, also comes up through the ground at the edges), leaper (jumps 4 high, so build taller than 4), brute, and the Behemoth every 10th wave. They follow a cost field from the heart where digging a block costs its HP plus a fixed amount, so they take the way with the fewest and weakest blocks. Digging down costs extra (except diggers), and natural ground just under a built block counts as that block, so going under a wall is no cheaper than going through it. Turrets can't shoot through natural ground (a zombie tunnelling underground is safe from them) but shoot over your own blocks. Each wave has more and tougher zombies (`zfWaveSpec`).
  - Incentives: walls under 3 high get jumped (4+ for leapers); after every wave a fort bonus pays coins for width and height; thicker and stronger walls take longer to dig through.
  - Players have 100 HP: a zombie touch hurts and knocks you back, you heal after 4 s without a hit, and at 0 you're back on your pad after 4 s.
  - Both players standing still by the heart holding ▼ (not the action) for 1.6 s start the next wave early, with a countdown. No person input for 15 s pauses the zombies.
  - Game over is not a "Level clear!": no confetti, and the results card says "You held N waves!" (engine hooks `quietClear` and `resultTitle`, read in levelClear and showResult).
  - Results: waves survived (best kept per browser, `zfwave:<name>`), zombies stopped and blocks built per player, medal (Gold 12+ waves, Silver 8+), time, biggest fort.
- Code: the level file above (MODES.zombiefort), the brain `js/cpu/levels/world25/defend-the-heart.js`, the preview `js/previews/world25.js`.
- Status: built and reviewed (23 review findings fixed). Computer tests (`sh tools/w25/zf-runs.sh`): a computer pair survives 18 waves (about 14-15 minutes; computers start waves early), a pair with a clumsy partner 15-16, a computer with the simple follow-the-leader test person 7-9; the idle check never ends (the zombies wait). People who use the full 5-minute build time should land around 15-20 minutes. Open for Gavyn: are 12 / 8 waves right for Gold / Silver once he has played it?

### World 26: Circus Big Top (mixed)
Inside a big striped circus tent: red and cream canvas, bunting, a cheering crowd in the stands, spotlights and sawdust. Nothing scary, nobody gets hurt (a miss lands in the safety net with a "Boing!", a bonk is a squeaky "Honk!", a topple is a soft roll on the sawdust). Gavyn picked the theme and these four levels from ten ideas (Nov 2026). World index 25 (`world: 25`). Like Worlds 24 and 25, it registers itself from `js/levels/world26/world.js` (`WORLDS[25] = {...}` and `LEVELS.push(...)`, one BEGIN/END section per level def), so it doesn't conflict with other worlds built in parallel (World 27 Robot Factory). Shared helpers (text, effects, drawing Pip and Bo, tent scenery) are in `js/levels/world26/circus-kit.js` (global `CIRCUS`). Each level's full rules are in the header comment of its level file, and its brain's plan is in the brain file's header.

- **26-1 Trapeze Catch** (team, mode `trapeze`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Act'`): one player is the FLYER on the left trapeze, the other the CATCHER hanging by the knees on the right one; they swap every act. Both swings keep the same 2.6 s beat. The catcher pumps with ◀ ▶ and calls "Hep!" with the action while the flyer is on the board; holding the action reaches out. The flyer lets go with the action (best at the top of the swing), holds ▲ to tuck and flip, and steers a little with ◀ ▶. A catch needs the hands close together (wider with gold help). A miss drops into the net ("Boing!") and the flyer climbs back. 4 acts of 4 catches. Cheers: height, flips, hoops and perfect catches. Medal: Gold ≤ 3:15, ≤ 3 boings and 2200 cheers; Silver ≤ 5:30 and ≤ 10 boings.
- **26-2 Clown Car Getaway** (scrolling team, mode `clowncar`, `roomWord: 'Stretch'`): the DRIVER steers a tiny clown car (▶ gas, ◀ brake/back up, ▲ or the action hops). The POPPER rides in the sunroof: ◀ ▶ lean to catch clowns leaping from the stands, ▼ ducks under banners (a "Duck! ▼" sign warns), the action honks to shoo animals off the road (an elephant needs 2 honks). Pies and banana peels to dodge, a pit-stop tent at the end of each stretch. Gold bumpers after 3 bonks, gold clowns after 5 misses in a row. Medal: Gold ≤ 3:30, 75% clowns, ≤ 4 bonks; Silver ≤ 5:30 and 50%.
- **26-3 Human Cannonball Tour** (scrolling team, mode `cannonball`, `roomWord: 'Tour'`): the CANNONBALL aims with ▲ ▼ and fires with the action; in flight ▲ or the action flaps (2 a shot), ▼ dives, ◀ ▶ drift, to fly through rings and pop balloons. The TRUCK drives a net on wheels with ◀ ▶ to catch them, then stops at the next cannon to reload (the action beeps the horn). 4 tours of 4 cannons. A gold, wider net after 3 misses. Medal: Gold ≤ 3:15, ≤ 2 misses, 60% stars; Silver ≤ 5:00, ≤ 8 misses.
- **26-4 Stilt Race** (scrolling versus, best of 5, 60 s rounds, mode `stiltrace`, `act: {label: 'steady'}`): each player races on tall stilts in their own lane. You lean with ◀ ▶ like balancing a broom: more lean is faster, too much and you topple (a soft 1.4 s roll). The action is "steady" (0.6 s of calm, 1.6 s cooldown). ▲ hops popcorn boxes and ▼ ducks bunting; spills are slippery. 5 courses; the lanes swap each round. Catch-up: a gold tailwind pulls a player who falls far behind along, and a player 1 round behind gets gold balance (2 behind: gold stilts too). Time up gives the round to whoever is farther; a tie goes to fewer topples.
- Code: level files in `js/levels/world26/`, brains in `js/cpu/levels/world26/`, previews in `js/previews/world26.js` (helpers `pw26*`). Top-level names: `CIRCUS` (kit), `STR*`/`str*` (26-4), `W26_STUB_MAP`, `K26tent`; 26-1 to 26-3 keep everything inside their MODES entry. Sounds under `Sfx.extra.<mode>*`.
- Status: built. Computer tests (`node tools/cpu-test.js 26-1,26-2,26-3`): a computer pair clears 26-1 in about 1:55, 26-2 in 2:45 and 26-3 in 1:35 (noisy test people 3:45, 3:05 and 1:50); the idle check never clears. In 26-4 Hard beats Easy every match. `node tools/w26/shots.js <code> <times>` takes screenshots into build/w26/.

### World 27: Robot Factory (mixed)
A bright, busy robot factory: steel halls, conveyor belts, cranes, warning stripes and friendly helper robots. Nothing scary, nobody gets hurt (bumps are "Boing!"s, "Clonk!"s and "Bonk!"s, and falls land in foam). Gavyn picked the theme and these four levels from ten ideas (Nov 2026); he skipped Battery Relay, Rocket Boot Test, Circuit Board Maze, Scrap Chute Sort, Drone Race and Spare Parts Grab. World index 26 (`world: 26`). Like Worlds 24 and 25 it registers itself from `js/levels/world27/world.js` (`WORLDS[26]` and `LEVELS.push(...)`, one BEGIN/END section per level def), so it never conflicts with World 26 Circus Big Top, built at the same time in another branch. It loads after World 25 and 26. Each level's full rules are in the header comment of its level file, and each brain's plan is in its brain file's header.

- **27-1 Mech Suit Duo** (team, mode `mechduo`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Zone'`, scrolls right; js/levels/world27/mech-suit-duo.js). Both pilot one big mech through four zones (Bolt Alley, Crate Drop, Steam Pipes, Big Bay Door). LEGS: walk, jump (▲ or the action), duck under low beams (▼). ARMS: ▲ ▼ set the arm low / middle / high, the action punches. A wall breaks after 3 punches at its glowing spot (the arm must be at that height); a tall ledge needs the arm high and a punch to grab and pull up; holding the arm high blocks falling crates. A fall into a gap is a "Boing!" back to the edge. They swap every zone; Pip works the legs first. Gold help (only while both play): a gold fist after 18 s stuck at a wall, a plank, an umbrella and a lift after 15 s at a ledge. Medal: Gold under 3:30 with at most 3 boings and half the bolts; Silver under 5:30 with at most 10 boings.
- **27-2 Runaway Assembly Line** (team, mode `runbelt`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noTime`, scrolls right with the belt; js/levels/world27/runaway-assembly-line.js). The screen moves with a conveyor belt that speeds up through each line (Bolt Line, Paint Shop, Spring Press, Final Inspection). The RUNNER rides the belt, jumps small gaps and dodges stampers (they glow before they slam). The FIXER runs the catwalk above and hits switch boxes to slide missing belt pieces into wide gaps ahead. An unfixed wide gap jams the line until fixed. Gold medal: at most 3 bumps and 55% of the gears; Silver: at most 10 bumps.
- **27-3 Magnet Crane Crew** (team, mode `magcrane`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Bay'`; js/levels/world27/magnet-crane-crew.js). One screen per bay (Pit Stop, Stair Climb, Double Pit, Tower Top). The OPERATOR drives the crane (◀ ▶, cable ▲ ▼; the action drops a new crate down the chute). The RIDER stands on the magnet and grabs and drops metal crates (the action) to fill pits and build steps to the high exit. The exit only counts when the rider walks there from the START pad (no riding the magnet to the door). Gold help: a plank after 2 pit falls, a gold guide after 30 s with no crate placed. Medal: Gold under 3:30 with at most 2 falls; Silver under 6:00.
- **27-4 Robot Rumble** (versus, best of 5, mode `robotrumble`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `stomp: false`; js/levels/world27/robot-rumble.js). Each round has a BUILD and a RUMBLE. Build (up to 22 s): a hopper drops the same part twice onto a belt every 1.4 s, one riding to each side. Walk into a part to pick it up, carry it to your own pad. Four slots with two kinds each: legs Treads (heavy) or Springs (big jump), body Tank (heavy) or Turbo (fast), arms Boxers (strong shove) or Bumpers (soft to hit), head Antenna (fast charge) or Visor (long reach). ▼ + the action drops a part. The build ends 4 s after the first robot is done ("Hurry!"); empty slots get Rusty spares; the first done gets a 3 s Turbo Start. Rumble: hold the action to charge a shove and let go to lunge; hold ▼ to brace (max 1.5 s). Pushed into the foam pit is a ring-out. After 25 s the platform edges fold in; at 45 s the robot nearer the middle wins. Catch-up: whoever is behind finds a gold Mega Fist on their pad. Two shoves landing at the same moment hit both robots.
- Brains: `js/cpu/levels/world27/` (one per level). Team brains play at full skill. Robot Rumble: Hard waits for a full charge but shoves first when the other robot is about to; it beats Normal and Easy every time in tests. Normal beats Easy most of the time.
- Code: level files and the shared kit `js/levels/world27/factory-kit.js` (`RF27`, `rf27*` helpers: backdrops, panels, pops, sounds `Sfx.extra.rf27*`); top-level names `MECH`, `BELT`, `CRANE`, `RUMBLE`, `W27_MAP`; previews in `js/previews/world27.js` (`pw27*`). Test scripts: `node tools/w27/sim.js 27-N [--who=pair|noisy|idle] [--skills=hard,easy]` and `node tools/w27/shots.js 27-N 5,10` (screenshots to build/w27/).
- Status (2026-11-09): built and tested with the computer pair, a clumsy partner and an idle partner (team levels never clear alone). Computer pair times: 27-1 about 2:08 (Gold), 27-2 about 2:57 (Gold), 27-3 about 2:40 (Gold).
### World 28: Pyramid Sands (mixed)
A sunny desert of dunes and pyramids, and the tombs inside them. Gavyn picked the theme and four of ten level ideas (Nov 2026: 1, 4, 5, 9). For the boulder run he asked for "good effects, this should feel like Indiana Jones": so it has big adventure-film effects (screen shake, dust, falling rocks, crumbling floors, close-call slow motion) but an original look, with no characters or likenesses from any film. Gavyn (Nov 2026): the boulder is not too scary, this is not a game for very young kids, so keep the thrills. Nobody gets hurt: a squished player is a pancake for a moment, a trap is an "Ouch!". World index 27 (`world: 27`). Like Worlds 24 and 25 it registers itself from `js/levels/world28/world.js` (`WORLDS[27]` and `LEVELS.push(...)`, one BEGIN/END section per level), loaded after the four level files. Shared drawing, effects and sounds are in `js/levels/world28/pyramid-kit.js` (prefix `pyr`). Each level's full rules are in its level file's header and its brain's plan in the brain file's header. Screenshot helper: `node tools/w28/shot.js <code> <seconds> [out.png] [--hold=right|cpu] [--room=N]`.
