- **28-1 Rolling Boulder Run** (team, scrolls right, mode `boulderrun`, 3 runs: The Long Hall, Crumbling Bridge, The Great Escape). A giant boulder drops ("RUN!") and chases the pair down a tomb corridor. One player pulls levers (hold the action) to raise stone doors, the other smashes rubble (3 presses); the jobs swap every run. Gaps, crumbling floor tiles, steps and shaking ceiling rocks on the way. The boulder rolls a little faster each run but never gets more than 520 px behind; it pancakes a player it catches and bounces back, and after 3 squishes it gets tired (gold help). At the end it falls into a chasm. Medal: Gold within 200 s and at most 1 squish, Silver within 330 s and 5. Computer pair about 108 s, with a clumsy partner about 144 s; the computer can't finish alone.
- **28-2 Tomb Torch Explorers** (team, mode `tombtorch`, 3 chambers). The tomb is dark. One player carries the torch (it lights the way and the braziers), the other is the seeker: only the seeker presses switches (seen in the torch light) to open gates and disarms traps that have been seen; an unseen trap gives an "Ouch!". The jobs swap between chambers. Gems and a treasure chest to collect. Medal: Gold within 240 s, at most 2 ouches and 60% of the gems; Silver within 400 s and 8 ouches. Computer pair about 50 s, clumsy partner about 87 s.
- **28-3 Sliding Stone Puzzle** (team, top-down grid, mode `stonepuzzle`, 3 puzzles: The Eye Door, The Four Seals, The Pharaoh's Seal). Push glyph stones onto the sockets that match the picture tablet. Heavy (big) stones need both players in a line pushing the same way; hold the action and step back to pull a small stone; stand on the reset tile for 1 s to start the puzzle again. When solved the stones sink and the secret door opens. Every puzzle was checked with the computer's planner. Computer pair about 50 s, clumsy partner about 57 s.
- **28-4 Camel Race** (versus, best of 5, 75 s rounds, scrolls right, mode `camelrace`, `act: {label: 'dash'}`). Each player rides a camel in their own lane over the same dunes (lanes swap every round). The camel runs by itself; ▲ jumps, ▼ in the air drops faster. Landing on the far side of a dune is a "Perfect landing!" (+60 speed), landing on the next dune's face a "Thud!". The action eats a date for a dash (3 dates, a new one every 4 s; a dash right at GO is a "Great start!"). Cacti and rocks halve your speed, pots break, quicksand slows you right down, oasis puddles splash; gold scarabs in the air give a boost. Courses: Dune Dash, Cactus Canyon, Quicksand Flats, Oasis Run, Pyramid Final (rounds about 35-45 s). Catch-up like Dolphin Race: the camera never leaves the trailer behind and a far trailer gets a gold tailwind; 1 round behind gives a 4th date that grows faster, 2+ behind also a gold head start. Tiebreak: more perfect landings. The brain tries "jump now", "jump a bit later" and "don't jump" with the level's own movement (and the best next jump after landing) and scores each; Easy notices things late and presses a bit early or late. Hard beats Easy every time in our runs, Hard beats Normal about 2 in 3, and Easy beats a player who does nothing.
- Previews: `js/previews/world28.js` (prefix `pw28`).

### World 29: Ninja Dojo (mixed)
A moonlit old town: tiled rooftops with curled eaves, paper lanterns, a guarded fortress, a downhill market street and a training dojo. Cartoon ninjas: foam-soft throwing stars that go "bonk", guards who get dizzy, nobody gets hurt. Gavyn asked for thrills ("not a toddler game"), so these levels have chases, close calls and real timing. Gavyn picked the theme and these four levels from ten ideas (Nov 2026); he skipped Bamboo Forest Dash, Castle Wall Climb, Kite Glider Escape, Dojo Trap Hall, Smoke Bomb Heist and Rooftop Race. World index 28.

- **29-1 Rooftop Moonlight Run** (team, mode `roofrun`, ownStep + ownHUD + noFalls, scrolls right, roomWord 'Stretch'): moonlit rooftops under a big moon. Both run ◀ ▶ and jump ▲ over small gaps (up to about 110 px) and steps. They hop white roof cats: a bump is a 0.7 s dizzy, then that cat dozes for 1.8 s so nobody gets stuck behind it. Crumbly roofs fall away 0.9 s after the first foot and come back after 2.6 s. They collect moon stars (79 in all). On the ground you stop and teeter at the edge of a wide gap ("Too far to jump!"). A jump or rope leap that just misses catches the edge ("Phew! Caught the edge!": feet up to 46 px below the top, hands within 28 px, heading for that edge; running off a roof is never caught). Each WIDE GAP takes the grappling hook in three beats. (1) The THROWER stands on the strip in their colour at the roof's edge; the aim line sweeps by itself and the action throws. The hook must pass through the ring around the hook point (lantern post, swaying lantern or kite); the ring turns green while a throw would catch, and a miss reels back. (2) The SWINGER grabs the rope at the peg (action), leans ◀ ▶ the way they are swinging to pump, and leaps with the action while "Leap!" is lit. The thrower can give one "Tug!" at the back of each swing. (3) The PULL: the thrower HOLDS the action while the swinger taps to heave in rhythm (mashing slips; letting go half way drops the thrower). A fall is soft: hay cart, bounce back, counted "Whoops" (a fall from the rope adds the hint "Pump higher, then leap when it says Leap!"). Rooms (jobs swap each stretch, each ends at a dojo gate): Lantern Lane (Pip throws, 4 post gaps, sweep 3.4 s), Breezy Ridge (Bo throws, wind gusts, swaying lanterns, 3.1 s), Kite Alley (Pip, kites and crumbly roofs, 2.9 s), The Moon Tower (Bo, wind, 5 long gaps up to 460 px, 2.7 s). Act labels per stretch 'throw' / 'grab' (pad label 'hook'). Results card: Moon stars, Hook throws (hits of throws), Whoops, Medal, a line with gaps, tugs, edge catches and cat bumps, and the gold-help line. **Medal:** Gold ≤ 3:50 with ≤ 3 whoops and ≥ 75% of the moon stars; Silver ≤ 6:00 with ≤ 10 whoops; else Bronze. **Gold help** (through `K.goldHelp`, only while both are playing, each at most once a stretch, counted): 3 hook misses in a row → gold hook (catch radius 80); 2 falls from the rope → gold rope (pumps higher) plus a gold ledge to land on at every far roof (`wallX(R)`: a plate, not a wall); 2 drops in the pull → gold loop (the thrower no longer has to hold). Idle 12 s: "Taking a break" and the clock stops. **Brain:** both jobs read `MODES.roofrun.plan(st)` (phase run/throw/swing/pull, throwNow, canGrab, pump, letGo, tug, holding, heave, x and act per job) and run with `MODES.roofrun.nav(st, id, x)`. Always full skill; waits for a person more than 380 px behind ("This way!"); short cpuSay tips; cannot finish alone. **Key constants (S):** runV 265, jumpV 575, g 1500, maxJump 130, zone 150, throwV 1050, range 430, catchR 44 (gold 80), aim 0.45-1.35 rad, green 0.8, pump 1.15 (gold 1.6), tugE 0.75, heave 0.125 with heaveCd 0.32, dropT 0.3, mantle 46, reach 28, crumbT 0.9, catCalm 1.8, goldT 230, silverT 360. Course tokens: `r420[+30|-25]`, `k150`, `g90`, `G360:post|sway|kite[:h220][:o0.38]`, and after a roof `/c200,p420,s300` (cat, prowling cat, star). **Times:** computer pair 2:35 Gold; scripted person (0.18 s reactions) with the computer 2:45 Gold; two scripted people 2:53 Gold; a slow kid about 4:05 Silver; masher 7-9 min Bronze. Tools: `tools/w29/roofrun-people.js` (scripted people with the computer or each other), `roofrun-input.js` (controller, keyboard, touch), `roofrun-restart.js` (restart, next stretch, pause mid-beat), `roofrun-check.js`, `roofrun-gold.js`, `roofrun-human.js`, `roofrun-shots.js`.
- **29-2 Shadow Sneak** (scrolling team, mode `shadowsneak`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Stretch'`, act labels `dash` / `toss` swapping per room): side view of a fortress wall at night. Each stretch is 3 guard posts, each ending at a gatehouse. The SNEAKER (courtyard) creeps with ◀ ▶ (100 px/s, silent). Holding the action dashes (250 px/s), but every step makes a noise ring that pulls guards over (no noise while pushing at a wall). ▲ hops crates and creaky boards. The sneaker is hidden automatically in any spot with a closed-eye badge (barrel, doorway, hay cart, bamboo, awning shade, gatehouse arch) while not dashing. The action at the lever in the arch opens the gate and the tower door. The PEBBLE THROWER (wall walk, never seen) walks with ◀ ▶, slides the aim ring with ▲ ▼ and tosses with the action. "Tik!" makes the nearest free guard walk over and look for a few seconds, and dogs run to it. The pouch holds 3 and refills one every 2.2 s. Guards: patrols, sentries, sleepers (a close noise wakes them for 4.5 s), dogs (sniff circle, run to every sound) and searchlights (can't be distracted). Lantern cones on the cobbles show exactly how far each guard sees (190 px). Standing in a cone fills the eye over the sneaker (0.5 s, or 0.16 s up close). SPOTTED: gong, "!" and a startled jump (0.5 s, up to +0.7 s when very close, +0.3 s for a sleeper), then the post's guards chase at 228 px/s. The sneaker sprints at 250 px/s: hops crates (which slow guards) or a guard's head, and ▼ slides (0.42 s at 400 px/s under a guard's arms, 1.1 s cooldown, chase only). Hiding spots within 130 px of where you were seen get a red open eye and don't work in that chase; gatehouses always do. Reaching cover with the guards 80 px behind (30 px at a gatehouse) starts "Shh! Stay still": the guards run up and look around for 1.5 s. Stepping out means "There!" and the chase resumes; staying means "Phew!". The thrower's pebbles bonk chasing guards dizzy for 1.5 s. Caught = "Gotcha!", carried back to the post's gatehouse (soft fail). Stretches: The Outer Yard (Pip sneaks), The Sleepy Barracks (Bo; sleepers, creaky boards), The Kennel Court (Pip; dogs, two sentries needing two pebbles), The Keep (Bo; searchlights plus everything). Gold help (only while both are playing, announced and counted): spotted 2 times in one stretch gives a gold moon cloud (guards see 105 px, gold cones) for the rest of the stretch; stuck 45 s at one post gives a gold owl that hoots at the plan's pebble spot while the sneaker waits hidden (at most every 6 s), for the rest of that post. Idle 12 s: "Taking a break", clock stops. Results: Spotted, Caught, Unseen gates, Medal, plus a pebbles / bonks / escapes / slides line and the gold-help line. Medal: Gold ≤ 4:30, spotted ≤ 2 and never caught; Silver ≤ 7:00 and caught ≤ 3; else Bronze. Room def `{ title, sneak, look, posts: [{ w, items }] }` with tokens B D H M A (hiding spots), P a-b (patrol), S xL/R (sentry), Z (sleeper), G (dog), C (crate), K (creaky boards), L a-b:secs (searchlight). `MODES.shadowsneak.plan(st)` runs the level's own guard rules a few seconds ahead (`stepGuard`, `forecast`, including pebbles and the owl in flight) and feeds the prompts, the "toss here" ring, the HUD chips and the brain. Brain: the sneaker moves only when the look-ahead says it's safe, turns back if the way closes, and dashes only out of earshot. In a chase it runs for the spot (not a red-eyed one) or gatehouse with the guards farthest behind, hops guards, slides when one is close behind, then stays still. The thrower puts the ring on the pebble that opens the way soonest (a pair of pebbles for two guards) and leads chasing guards for bonks. Neither can finish alone. Bot times: computer pair 3:06 (Gold, 0 spotted); prompt-following person + computer 3:20-3:22; 1 s-slow person 4:11-4:13 (Gold); two people 0.35 s late 3:49; two people 1 s late 5:41 (Silver); noisy partners 3:42-4:36; idle never clears. Tools: `tools/w29/shadowsneak-{shots,trace,card,perf,people,input,edge,chase,owl,lose}.js`.
- **29-3 Runaway Rickshaw** (scrolling team, mode `rickshaw`, `ownStep`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Stretch'`, per-room `act.labels` hop/swing that swap with the jobs): a rickshaw with no brakes rolls by itself down a sloping, bumpy two-lane market street at night, faster on the steep parts; the camera follows it right and downhill. The STEERER (in the seat): ▲ ▼ swerve between the far and near lanes, the action hops (0.74 s in the air), ◀ held drags a foot as a soft brake (only about a third slower). They swerve round melon carts and noodle stalls, hop or swerve crates, barrels and hens, take the ramp lane to fly over canals, hop ditches, keep to the open lane on narrow bridges, and drive through dumplings (hopping for ones in the air). The SWORD RIDER (standing on the canopy): ◀ ▲ ▶ point the sword behind, up or ahead, and the action swings (0.3 s; a swing that hits nothing has a 0.26 s cooldown, so mashing fails). ▼ ducks under lantern gates (a "Duck! ▼" sign stands before each). Rival ninjas on rooftops (ahead or behind) and on kites (above) pop up with "!", a shrinking ring and flashing chevrons by the rider, then throw a foam star. Warning goes from 1.05 s down to 0.85 s and flight from 0.95 s down to 0.78 s over the 4 stretches; volleys of 2-3 stars come 0.6 s apart. A swing in the last 0.14 s before a star arrives is GREAT: the star flies back and bonks the thrower dizzy. A swing up to 0.08 s late still deflects it, but not as a Great. Spills (a star that gets through, a crash with a 0.7 s stop, a splash that bobs across the canal for 1 s, or a gate bonk that leaves the rider dizzy for 0.9 s) wobble the rickshaw and knock one of 5 steamer baskets off the back; when all 5 are gone there is a 2.2 s restock stop. No game over. Each stretch ends with a soft crash into a hay cart at a tea stop (the last one is the dojo). 4 stretches: Market Hill (Pip steers, pace 280), Lantern Lane (Bo, 305, gates and ramps), Kite Bridge (Pip, 330, kites and a 1500 px narrow bridge), The Big Plunge (Bo, 355, 17-19% slopes, volleys of 3, a bridge). Gold help (gold banner, counted on the results card, only while both are playing): 3 stars through in one stretch gives a gold sword for 15 s (it deflects stars from every side, swings longer and swishes past gates); 3 crashes, splashes or bridge bonks give gold wheels for 15 s (they bump things aside and bounce over canals). The corner panel counts down the seconds, a message says when it runs out, and 3 more misses in the same stretch earn it again. Idle: after 25 s with no person pressing anything, "Taking a break... press anything to go on": the rickshaw is held still and the clock stops. Results: Time, Dumplings, Spills, Stars bonked back, Medal. Medal: Gold <= 3:40, >= 75% of the 125 dumplings and <= 6 spills; Silver <= 5:00, >= 50% and <= 20 spills; otherwise Bronze. Brain (reads `MODES.rickshaw.plan(st)`, the same plan the prompts and HUD chips read): as steerer it scores both lanes over a lookahead that grows with speed, holds ▲ or ▼ until the rickshaw is heading for the lane it picked, hops at the last moment for low things and ditches, and drags a foot only while a person rider is dizzy. As rider it points at the soonest star (or the next warning) and swings 0.09 s before it arrives (always a Great); it ducks for gates unless a star comes first. It talks in short bubbles. Key constants are in `MODES.rickshaw.S` (slopeK 2, hopV 480, rampV 520, swingT 0.3, great 0.14, grace 0.08, laneRate 5.5, roadY 362, goldDur 15). Bot times: computer pair 3:20 (Gold); noisy partner 3:27-3:30. Tools: `node tools/w29/rickshaw-run.js [--skip=steer|ride]`, `rickshaw-shots.js <times> [--solo=0|1] [--sloppy=P] [--pause] [--results] [--w= --h=]`, `rickshaw-human.js [--react= --sd= --mash=1]`, `rickshaw-people.js [--who=good,kid,masher,holdup,holddown,holdright,wrongjob,away,idle,duo] [--as=0,1]`, `rickshaw-edge.js`, `rickshaw-input.js`, `rickshaw-frame.js`.
- **29-4 Throwing Star Duel** (versus, best of 5, 45 s rounds, mode `starduel`, `ownStep`, one screen, `act: {label: 'throw'}`, `tagLift: 30`): Pip duels from the left half of a moonlit dojo (tatami, paper screens, gong, lanterns) and Bo from the right; the halves are exact mirrors. The ninjas are drawn at twice engine size (S = 2, heights scaled by Z = 2/1.5 so timings did not change). Foam stars fly in two lanes: the action throws HIGH when standing and LOW when ducking, from a pouch of 3 that grows one back every 1.0 s, but only while you are standing (never while ducking, so camping in a duck runs dry; the panel says "Stand up to refill!"). ▼ ducks high stars, ▲ hops low ones, ◀ ▶ step along your own half (closer means less time to react for both). When a star is under 0.42 s away the action swings the sword instead. A swing at the right height (standing for high, ducking for low) in the last 0.16 s is a "Clang!" (the star goes back 18% faster and is now yours); in the last 0.065 s it is a "Perfect!" (36% faster). Each bounce shortens the window by 0.012 s (never under 0.1 s), and stars top out at 880 px/s. A star rings white while it is in your window. Too early or the wrong height is a whiff (no throw or swing for 0.55 s). A bonk is 0.8 s dizzy plus 1.5 s safe; 3 bonks (your three wall lanterns go out) loses the round. Rounds: First Bow, Lantern Bob, Swift Stars (stars 18% faster), Quick Lantern, Grand Duel; in rounds 2, 4 and 5 a jade lantern bobs in the middle and swallows any star in its lane ("Paf!"). Time up: fewer bonks taken wins, then more deflects that round, else a tie. `tiebreak()`: more bonks landed in the match, then more deflects. Sudden death: the first bonk wins. Gold help (announced at GO, counted on the card, off in sudden death, given to whichever side is behind, the computer too): 1 round behind gives a gold sword (0.25 s window), 2 behind also a gold pouch (4 stars, one per 0.75 s). Prompts over the head and key lines show in rounds 1-2 and for anyone with gold help. Results: rounds won, bonks landed, deflects, perfect deflects, plus a longest-rally line and the gold-help line. `MODES.starduel.plan(st, id)` (nearest star, lane, seconds away, in window, lanes the lantern blocks) feeds the prompts, panels and brain. Brain: sees a star after a lag (Easy 0.42 s, Normal 0.26 s, Hard 0.13 s) and decides once per star to deflect, dodge or blunder. It parries Easy 6%, Normal 42%, Hard 72% of the stars it reads right, and blunders Easy 34%, Normal 12%, Hard 5% a star (more in long rallies). It throws every Easy 1.6-3.2 s, Normal 1.1-2.1 s, Hard 0.9-1.7 s, minds the lantern, throws low at a ducker and high at a hopper, and sends two low stars at a duck camper. Pairs of stars in different lanes and a duck feint before a high throw fool it. Bot results: computer vs computer even skills, rounds average about 25 s and no side bias over 400 Normal matches (208-192); Hard beats Easy every match, in about 12 s rounds. Scripted people: a kid script beats Easy 20/20, an everyday dodger goes about 50/50 with Normal, a sharp feinting player about 55% vs Hard, a parrying player about 15% vs Hard, mashers lose every match to Normal and Hard. Constants in `SD` and `ROUNDS` at the top of the level file; sounds `Sfx.extra.starduel*`; preview helpers `pw29sd*`. Tests in tools/w29/: `starduel-stats.js --skills=a,b --n=N [--from=K]`, `starduel-people.js --who=kid|avg|sharp|swinger|perfect|masher|thrower|ducker|holder|idle --skill=...`, `starduel-input.js` (controller, keyboard, touch with no computer), `starduel-edge.js` (restart, pause, NaN, idle), `starduel-look.js [--phone] [--solo]`.
- Code: level files in `js/levels/world29/`, brains in `js/cpu/levels/world29/`, previews in `js/previews/world29.js` (helpers `pw29*`), screenshot and test scripts in `tools/w29/`. The shared kit `js/levels/world29/ninja-kit.js` (`NINJA`, the same helper names as World 26's `CIRCUS` plus night scenery, ninja poses, guards, stars, hooks and `ninja*` sounds; its header is the manual; `node tools/w29/kit-sheet.js` draws all of it). Unlike Worlds 24-28, each level's def sits at the bottom of its own level file as `NINJA.defs.<mode>`; `js/levels/world29/world.js` sets the world entry and pushes the four defs in order.
- `node tools/cpu-test.js` also takes exact level names (`node tools/cpu-test.js "Shadow Sneak" --who=noisy`), and `node tools/w29/shots.js <mode> <times>` takes a mode or name, so tests still pick the right level while other worlds' codes shift.
- Status: built and reviewed (Nov 2026). Each level was built, then a second agent tried to break it with scripted people (mashers, slow kids, wrong-job, idle, two people with no computer, controller and touch through the real input paths) and fixed what it found. A computer pair clears 29-1 in 2:35, 29-2 in 3:06 and 29-3 in 3:20; the idle test never clears. 29-4: Hard beats Easy every match from both sides, no side bias over 400 Normal matches. Nobody has played it by hand yet.
- Draw cost: the outdoor levels take about 4-6 ms a frame in headless software rendering (mostly filling the full-screen night sky); fine on real hardware so far, but caching the sky to an offscreen canvas would help if phones feel slow.

### World 30: Time Chase (team boss)
The third boss world (Gavyn's rule: a boss world every 10 worlds). The whole idea is Gavyn's own (Nov 2026): a time-travel chase, one level, following the boss through important moments in history. He picked the eight moments and asked for 2-3 minutes each. The world name "Time Chase" is Claude's pick. Thrills are fine (Gavyn: not a game for very young kids); nobody is ever hurt, fails are soft bumps with a short pop-back.
- **30-1 The Time Thief** (team, mode `timethief`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Era'`, every room `ownStep`): the Time Thief (a coat covered in clocks, a top hat, a brass jet time machine with a grabbing claw) steals one famous thing from each moment in history. Pip and Bo chase him through a portal at the end of every era and win each thing back. 10 rooms = 10 eras in `TTH_ORDER`: 8 history eras, the finale and a versus race. A good pair takes about 2 minutes an era and about 24 minutes in all (computer pair 23:37, clumsy pair 28:14); people will take 30 to 45 minutes, which is why there are checkpoints.
  - Checkpoints: the start of every era after the first is saved in the browser (store key `tth:cp`: era, time, stats, loot). Coming back to the level resumes there with the clock and bumps carried over. R or "Restart this era" on the pause card restarts the era; "Start over from Era 1" on the pause card clears the checkpoint (generic `pauseExtra()` / `pauseAct(arg)` mode hooks in `js/ui.js`). Finishing the level clears it. Bot tests never use checkpoints.
  - Every era has two jobs that swap, soft fails only, hazards telegraphed, announced gold help after a few misses, and cannot be cleared by one player alone. When no person presses anything for 10 s the Thief waits ("The Time Thief is waiting...") and the clock stops. In 1-player mode the computer takes the job the person leaves and never clears an era for an idle person.
  - HUD: a 10-slot era strip (top centre) that fills with the things won back, a time pill with the era's own count, a news line, the Thief's speech bubble, per-player hint bubbles that name each player's own keys, controller buttons or touch buttons. Bigger sizes on phones (`tthBig()`).
  - Results card: Time, Record, Bumps and Trophy tiles, then the saved-treasures and race-champ lines. Trophy (`TTH.medal`, set from bot runs): Gold at 30:00 or less and 30 bumps or fewer, Silver at 45:00 and 80, otherwise Bronze. The final card counts the Time Thief among the bosses beaten.
  - **Era 1: Dinosaur Asteroid, 66 million years ago** (`dino`). Era 1, Dinosaur Asteroid, 66 million years ago (js/levels/world30/tth-dino.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world30/tth-dino.js). Gavyn's pick: the Thief is stealing a T. rex egg; run from the fire and meteors while one carries the egg and the other clears the path. It is a side-scrolling run to the right through a fern jungle, a tar swamp and a volcano slope, with the asteroid growing in the sky all era. A meteor startles the Thief and he drops the egg ("YIKES! My egg!"); Pip and Bo carry it home to the mother T. rex's nest. The ground is a height map with its own collision (slopes, 64 px ledges, pits); Pip and Bo pass through each other. The egg decides the jobs: whoever holds it is the CARRIER (slower, 150 px/s; small 40 px hops; action = toss; hold down = duck and hug the egg), the other is the CLEARER (bone club; the action does what fits and the touch button's word follows: smash / shoo / pull / whack). The egg is tossed to the partner within 240 px sideways and 175 px up or down when the carrier looks at them, and always lands in their hands. It is tossed across at each of the 2 checkpoint flags ("Swap jobs!"), so both do both jobs; the room's 'first' says who catches it at the start. A dropped egg rolls back a little and glows; whoever walks into it picks it up; it never breaks, never falls into a pit and hops ahead by itself if the fire reaches it. Things in the way: boulder (nobody passes; 4 smashes), log (3), meteor rock (2), napping baby Triceratops (1 "Shoo!") which a free jump clears but an egg hop does not; ledge (too high with the egg: the clearer jumps up, the egg is tossed up); pit of tar or lava, 112 px (a free jump clears it, the egg is tossed across; a fall is "Splat!" / "Hot foot!" and a pop back to the rim); vine bridge over a 232 px pit (the clearer HOLDS the action at the glowing post while the carrier walks across, then it ties itself; let go early and it creaks about 0.9 s, "Hold on!", before it drops the walker). So an idle partner stalls everything and one player can never clear it. Three stretches (Fern Jungle, Rumbling Swamp, Volcano Slope; 12 / 10 / 11 things in the way). The Thief hovers top right all era with one trick at a time, every 6.4 / 5.4 / 4.8 s: a meteor at a player (glowing ring for 1.15 s, then BOOM: "Bonk!"); from stretch 2 a meteor that stays as a rock to smash; in stretch 3 a shower of 3 rings in a row (1.35 / 1.6 / 1.85 s); and his DIVE for the egg (1.3 s of aim, 1.8 s the first time, then a 0.55 s dive): the carrier ducks, or the clearer whacks the claw (he spins off dizzy), otherwise "Yoink!" knocks the egg loose (he never gets away with it). A bonk makes the carrier drop the egg. Never a ring over someone holding a vine post or on a bridge, never a dive by a pit, on a bridge or near the nest, nothing during cards, celebrations or when nobody plays. The fire wall is the pacer (64 / 76 / 88 px/s, catches up to 380 px behind the slower player, never closer than 210 px to the next thing in the way, so waiting at a boulder is safe); a touch is "Hot foot!", a pop forward, and the fire falls back; it falls back 240 px at each flag. "Wait for Bo!": nobody gets more than 470 px ahead (the leash never stops a jump over a pit or the one walking back to a dropped egg). The end: the carrier steps up to the nest with the partner up there too; the egg hops in, the Thief grabs for it ("NO! That egg is MINE!"), the mother T. rex ROARS him away ("EEK! OK, OK! Keep your egg!"), and the asteroid's flash lights the horizon as the portal opens. Gold help, always announced: 3 hot feet in a stretch slow the fire (x 0.6); 3 egg-dropping bonks in a stretch make the Thief sleepy (rings 1.6 s, aim 1.8 s, tricks 1.4 x rarer); 2 falls into the same pit put a gold stepping stone in it / tie the vine bridge (after two pits needed that, after 1 fall); 20 s without progress gives a gold hint and a gold arrow. Hints name each player's own keys (on touch "the smash button"), including "Come back! Bo can't get over it with the egg." and "Too far for a toss! Go back to the edge."; the computer's player never gets key hints. True facts in the taunts and news: the asteroid was about 10 km wide, it hit near today's Mexico 66 million years ago, T. rex means "tyrant lizard king". HUD: "Stretch n/3" in the time pill, 3 pips on the era strip, a track along the bottom (fire, both players, egg, flags, nest), hits-left pips over boulders. The computer partner plays whichever job the egg gives it, goes back to smash a rock that lands between the two, waits for a slow person, never clears alone, and in 1-player mode takes over the clearing after 7 s if the person leaves it (it tosses them the egg: "Catch! I'll do it."). Stats: st.stats.dino (stretchT, smashed, shoos, bridges, tosses, drops, whacks, ducks, snatched, meteors, rocks, hot, pits). Bot times: computer pair 2:12-2:17, clumsy partner 2:53-3:13, young kid 3:32-4:31, an idle partner never ends. Tests: tools/w30/dino-keys.js (keyboard, controller, touch), dino-solo.js (scripted people with the computer), dino-dbg.js and dino-play.js (tracers), dino-perf.js (draw time).
  - **Era 2: Building Stonehenge, 2500 BC** (`henge`). Era 2, Building Stonehenge, 2500 BC (mode 'timethief', phase 'henge'; js/levels/world30/tth-henge.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world30/tth-henge.js). The Time Thief dragged the last giant stone (25 tons) away from the half-built circle. Pip and Bo roll it home on log rollers, about 3.7 screens to the right, in 3 legs with a rest post between them where the jobs swap (Bo pushes first). The stone lies on 4 logs; a log under it moves at half its speed, so logs travel to the back and roll out behind. The stone only rolls while a log lies under its front half: a "!" blinks at the nose when that log is nearly spent, and with no log there it stops with a "Thud!" and waits. Jobs: the PUSHER holds toward the stone at its back end (41 px/s on the flat, 32 uphill); the ROLLER grabs a free log with the action button, jumps on the stone, runs to its front and presses the action again, and the log drops just ahead of the nose (a dashed ring shows the spot). Each log buys about 200 px, so a leg needs 5-7 trips. Only the pusher can move the stone and only the roller can carry logs, so one player alone never gets anywhere. Either player's action also shoos sheep and whacks the Thief's claw. Controls: left / right walk, push, brake and haul; up jumps; the action grabs, drops, shoos, whacks and pulls (button words grab / drop / shoo / pull). Hills: uphill (legs 1 and 3) the stone rolls back to the last wooden chock if the pusher lets go for more than 0.35 s ("Rolled back!" after 50 px, a team bump). Downhill (leg 3) the stone runs away, so the pusher hops over it and brakes from the front (hold left, 52 px/s); a runaway that outruns its logs gives "Runaway! Thud!". On the way: mud (legs 2 and 3) swallows one log, with a pile of spares just past it; sheep graze on the track and stop the stone until shooed; the stream (leg 2) needs 3 logs laid in the water as a log road. At every rest post and when the log road is done the rotation is topped up to 4 logs ("Fresh logs!"). The Thief (on screen all era, empty claw, because the stolen thing is the stone): he tugs at the stone at the start ("25 tons?!"). Twice he pinches a free log with his claw (1.0 s aim with a red ring, then 0.75 s reach): grab the log first or whack the claw, otherwise "Yoink!" and he dangles it for 6.5 s until whacked or dropped. Twice he lassoes the stone (1.4 s wind-up) and tows it backward: both hold right to haul, the rope snaps after about 2.4 s; towed 170 px or 10 s gives "Towed back!". He starts nothing during cards, celebrations or while nobody plays. Winning beat: at the circle the stone tips into its hole by itself (THUNK), then each player stands at a gold rope ring and holds the action; the lintel rises only while both hold (5.4 s, props click in at each third), CLONK, the trilithon is complete, the Thief tugs at it in vain ("Three stones?! WAY too heavy! Bah!") and the portal opens. Gold help (always announced, stays for the era): a miss at the logs is the pushed stone standing 3 s without a log. 2 misses give a gold 5th log; 4 misses give gold rollers (each log carries the stone twice as far). 2 roll-backs on a hill give gold chocks (no more rolling back). 2 runaway thuds give a gold brake. 2 yoinks make his claw slower. A lost lasso makes his next rope frayed. 13 s with no progress (9 s at the ropes) gives gold hints and a gold arrow. All fails are soft team bumps; nobody is hit. History facts on the news line: each great stone weighs 25 tons, like 4 elephants; the stones travelled 25 km without wheels. Stats (st.stats.henge): legT, thuds, waited, rollbacks, skids, yoinks, whacks, shoos, lasso [won, lost], carried [pip, bo]. Bot times: computer pair 2:05-2:15 with no gold help and usually no bumps; clumsy partner 2:31-3:16; young kid 2:57-3:55; an idle partner never ends it. Tests: tools/w30/henge-keys.js (keyboard, controller, touch, 1-player), henge-dbg.js, henge-perf.js.
  - **Era 3: The Fall of Troy, 1200 BC** (`troy`). Era 3, The Fall of Troy (1200 BC), Gavyn's pick: roll the giant wooden horse through the gates with both of you hiding inside. Files js/levels/world30/tth-troy.js and js/cpu/levels/world30/tth-troy.js; names start with tthTroy / TTHTROY. It is a sneaky game of red light, green light at dusk. Pip and Bo sit inside the horse, which is drawn as a cutaway, and roll it to the right across the plain, through the Great Gate, along the streets and up onto the city square; the camera scrolls with it. The roller holds right to roll (up to 135 px/s), down to tiptoe (62 px/s), and left or the action button ("brake") to brake; the horse is heavy and only coasts when let go. The lookout holds the action button ("peek"; up works too) to open the hatch in the neck. The twist: with the hatch shut the screen goes dark behind a plank curtain, and the wheels only turn while there is light, so the roller can do nothing without the lookout and the lookout cannot move the horse. Guards sweep lantern beams over marked strips of ground. Each raises his lantern with a "?" for 0.8 s first, and beams move at most 210 px/s. If the horse moves while a beam is on it ("Hey! That horse moved!"), or the hatch is open while a beam is on the head ("Hey! Who is peeking?"), it is caught: a soft fail, where a guard wheels it back 130 px (never past the last safe spot), shrugs "Just a horse..." and leaves, about 2.5 s plus a short grace. In the dark, gold light leaks through the planks where a beam is on the horse and "FREEZE!" shows. Three stretches, with a safe breather and a job swap after the first two. The Plain: a slow watchtower guard, a hill up and down ("Wheee!"), a second watchtower with a back-and-forth beam, a safe spot, the wall guard, then the narrow Great Gate (tiptoe through or "Scrape!"). The Streets: the Thief's first claw grab, two overlapping guards, a loose plank (fast = "CLONK!" and a guard looks over, tiptoe = silent), a patrol that walks up and knocks on the horse, and a guard the Thief wakes with a bell. To the Square: a fast guard with a plank in his beam, a second patrol, two overlapping guards, a narrow alley arch, the bell again, the stronger claw, the ramp up to the square (stalling rolls the horse back to the foot), a last guard, then park on the gold star. The claw (telegraphed 1.3 s) hooks the ring on the neck and drags the horse back, at most 170 px; the roller holds right to pull against it while the lookout holds the action button to unhook (1.5 s, the second time 2.2 s), and it only works with both at once. Gold help, always announced: 2 catches by the same guard make him sleepy (4: extra sleepy), 2 patrol catches make the next patrol hurry past, 2 scrapes widen the gate, 2 roll-backs make the ramp gentler, 14 s of tugging makes the claw rusty, and 22 s without progress gives gold hints saying whose turn it is. Nothing can catch the horse during cards, cutscenes, the swap, the claw, a catch or for 1.1 s after them, or while nobody plays. The Thief hovers top right all era; he wants the whole horse, so he carries nothing. He taunts, tips off the patrol, rings the bells and hooks the horse. The horse is won back by parking it on the gold star: every guard's lantern swings onto it ("What a fine horse! A gift for Troy!"), and the Thief sulks "Bah! It's FAR too watched to steal!". History: the story of the wooden horse comes from Greek poems about 3,200 years old, and nobody knows if it really happened. The computer partner plays either job, waits for the lookout's first peek before rolling, never rolls in the dark, and never clears the era alone. Stats in st.stats.troy: stretchT, caught, moved, seen, scrapes, clonks, quiet, rollbacks, unhooks, peeks, patrols. Bot times: computer pair 2:10 (stretches 33 / 37 / 57 s, 0 bumps), clumsy partner 2:26 to 3:05, young kid 2:48 to 4:24 (15 to 20 bumps, 4 to 11 gold helps), one player idle never ends. Drawing about 3.4 ms a frame. Tests: tools/w30/troy-keys.js (keyboard, controller, touch, 1-player both ways), troy-perf.js, troy-shots.js, troy-dbg.js.
  - **Era 4: The First Olympics, 776 BC** (`olympics`). Era 4, The First Olympics, 776 BC (mode 'timethief', phase 'olympics'; js/levels/world30/tth-olympics.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world30/tth-olympics.js). A scrolling chariot chase at Olympia over 3 lanes and 3 laps. Pip and Bo ride one chariot behind two horses; the Time Thief flies ahead with the champion's wreath on his claw. Two jobs that swap at every lap line (room field 'first' says who drives lap 1). The DRIVER changes lanes with up / down (one lane per press, a held button repeats every 0.32 s), holds right to gallop (or taps the action button "gallop": a spur that gallops for 1.2 s, so a touch thumb is free for lanes) and holds left to rein in; with nothing pressed the horses ease to a stop. The GRABBER holds the action button ("grab") to swing a long hook out (0.2 s) and lets go to snatch an olive branch as its pole passes the hook (a window of about 0.4 s; the branch glows), only if the chariot is in that branch's lane; a tap does nothing and a snatch at nothing rests the arm 0.55 s. Each branch is one leaf on the "Catch up!" gauge (bottom-left) and a speed boost. Laps need 10 / 11 / 12 branches. When the gauge is full the Thief comes down to tease: the wreath swings on a rope and the grabber lets go when the hook ring turns gold ("NOW!"), while the driver keeps galloping under him (below 380 px/s he drifts away) and, from lap 2, follows him when he weaves to another lane (an arrow 0.8 s before). Laps 1 and 2: the snatch tears off a ribbon ("RRRIP!"), he jets off dropping clock gears (4, then 6; a shadow and "!" 1.1 s before each lands), then the lap line: "Swap jobs!". Lap 3: the snatch takes the whole wreath, the horses pull up, the crowd goes wild, he sulks "Bah! Real winners got OLIVE leaves anyway!" and the portal opens. Each lap adds something: lap 1 amphorae, mud and turning posts; lap 2 fallen hurdles (two lanes wide), rolling discuses, the Thief's old socks on some poles and his weaving; lap 3 rival chariots that weave (driver points and chevrons blink 0.75 s first), more socks, faster horses (440 / 470 / 500 px/s) and a faster swing. Hazards are all soft: amphora / hurdle / gear / discus = "Crash!" (the team's bump, down to 40% speed); mud = the horses plod; a rival = "Bump!"; a turning post has a 440 px "rein in" zone and crossing its line above 330 px/s is a "Skid!" (below: "Clean turn!"); grabbing a sock = "Yuck!" (the arm rests 1 s). Everything is telegraphed at least 0.6 s (things scroll in at least 0.9 s away; one-way rows blink "!" 0.55 s before they scroll in; a discus "!" 0.8 s); nothing moves or drops during cards, the cutscene or idle. A lane change that clears a thing by under 130 px is a "Close one!". Gold help (announced, per lap): 3 branches missed in the right lane = a golden hook (just hold; it never takes a sock); 3 missed in the wrong lane = a long golden hook that reaches the next lane; 3 crashes = a clearer track; 2 skids by the same driver = the horses slow for turns by themselves; 3 whiffs at the wreath (or 16 s) = a slower swing and bigger ring, 6 whiffs (or 30 s) = the golden hook (6 too-quick taps count as 3 whiffs); 60 s in one lap's chase = the crowd throws a branch every 8 s while the chariot moves. The era can never be cleared alone: an idle driver stops the chariot, an idle grabber never takes the wreath, and in 1-player mode the computer takes the job the person leaves and waits. History in the news line and taunts: the first Olympics in 776 BC had one event, a foot race of about 192 m; chariot races joined in 680 BC; winners got a wreath of olive leaves, not gold. Stats (st.stats.olympics): laps [s], branches, missed, crashes, skids, whiffs, socks, mud, close. Bot times: computer pair 2:05-2:14, clumsy partner 2:37-2:50, young kid 2:59-3:21; nobody playing never ends. Test scripts: tools/w30/olympics-keys.js (real keyboard, controllers, touch, 1-player hints), olympics-solo.js (scripted people in 1-player mode), olympics-perf.js (frame time).
  - **Era 5: Viking Voyage, 1000 AD** (`vikings`). Era 5, Viking Voyage, 1000 AD (js/levels/world30/tth-vikings.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world30/tth-vikings.js; Gavyn's pick: row a longship through stormy waves, one rows to the beat, the other bails and watches for icebergs). The Time Thief stole the Vikings' sunstone, so thick fog lies on the sea. Leif Erikson's longship stays at the left and the sea scrolls in three lanes; Pip and Bo ride the ship (the era draws them). Two jobs that swap at two marker buoys (a gust carries the ship 2.6 s while they change places). The ROWER presses the action on the drum beat (a drum sound, the drum on deck and a beat track bottom left: "Perfect!" within 0.085 s, "Good!" within 0.19 s; off the beat is a weak splash, and only one stroke counts per beat, so mashing crawls) and steers one lane up or down with up / down. The LOOKOUT walks the deck with left / right, bails with the action at the bucket near the bow (holding works), and climbs the mast with up (down slides): from the mast top the fog opens 900 px ahead instead of 240 and everything seen stays "Spotted!" for both; nobody bails from up the mast, so it is a juggle. The ship's raven caws when something unseen is close. The hull fills by itself (faster in rain and after a hit); above 30% the ship slows, at 100% it is "Swamped!" and will not move until bailed down to 55%. So an idle rower and an idle lookout each stall the era: nobody clears it alone. Three stretches with a faster beat each (0.80, 0.72, 0.66 s): 1 Storm Swell (the Thief tips rain clouds over the ship and sends jet waves down the ship's lane after a blinking "!" of 1.15 s: steer out or "Splash!"); 2 Iceberg Field (rows of icebergs in the fog, every row leaves a lane free, "Crunch!" bounces the ship back); 3 Serpent Strait (a friendly sea serpent: thread the tall hoop, the coils go "Boing!", plus jet waves and rain). Then THE GRAB: the Thief dangles the sunstone over the mast just out of reach while big waves roll in; the rower keeps the beat so the ship climbs the wave, and on the crest the lookout at the mast top presses the action ("Yoink!"; swiping too soon makes him pull it away for that wave). Three grabs win the sunstone: the fog lifts, the sun comes out, "Land ho! Vinland!", and the Thief sulks "Bah! Keep your sparkly rock!". All fails are soft team bumps (Splash, Crunch, Boing, Swamped) that cost a few seconds. Gold help, always announced: Golden drum (6 weak strokes in 8: a wider beat window, then any stroke counts, per player), Gold lantern (3 crashes in a stretch: the deck sees 380 px), Gold bucket (swamped twice: scoops bail double), Golden waves (2 big waves missed: slower waves, they lift a slow ship, no more water comes in and the Thief cannot pull the stone away). Fairness: every hazard shows at least 0.7 s ahead (usually far more), nothing starts during cards, cutscenes, celebrations or the idle wait, and jet waves never come with a row close ahead. History in the news line and taunts: Leif Erikson's crew reached North America about 500 years before Columbus; longships had oars and a sail; Vikings told of a giant sea serpent and took ravens to sea. Bot times: computer pair 2:09-2:10 (stretches about 29 / 35 / 44 s, the grab 20 s), clumsy partner 2:24-2:55, young kid 2:27-4:11, an idle player never finishes. Tests: tools/w30/vikings-input.js (real keyboard, controller, touch, 1-player hints), vikings-trace.js (timeline), vikings-perf.js (draw time).
  - **Era 6: Leonardo's Wings, the 1490s** (`davinci`). Era 6, Leonardo's Wings (the 1490s, 'davinci', js/levels/world30/tth-davinci.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world30/tth-davinci.js). The Time Thief snatches Leonardo da Vinci's plans in Florence and jets off, so Pip and Bo fly Leonardo's wood-and-canvas flying machine after him; the era scrolls right and both ride the machine. Two jobs that swap twice. The FLAPPER (action word "flap") taps the action button: every press is one wingbeat that pushes the machine forward and holds it up for 0.8 s. A steady beat of about 2 a second flies best; every beat costs wing power (the wing meter under the machine), and mashing empties it: "Wings tired!", no beats for 1.2 s. With no beats the machine glides down and lands softly; a beat takes off again. The STEERER (action word "shoo") holds up to climb (slower, and only while the wings beat) or down to dive (faster), nothing held is level; the action toots the horn, which scatters a flock of pigeons in front of the machine. Neither can fly alone: without beats the machine sits on the ground, without steering it flies level into the first tower and pops back at the same height. The course is hand-made with no randomness, 30400 px in three stretches, and the 111 loose pages of the plans mark the way through every gap ("Pages 12/111" in the time pill). Stretch 1, the Tuscan hills: hills, windmills and castle towers to climb over, storm clouds to dive under, warm updrafts (rings of rising air that lift for free), a storm gust, two flocks of pigeons. Stretch 2, the rooftops of Florence: tower houses, washing lines (over), banners (under), tower-plus-banner gates, the updraft over the great dome, two flocks. Stretch 3, the river and the piazza: the Thief flies just ahead and weaves; stay close behind his jet in his slipstream (gold streaks when you are in it) until the slipstream meter at the bottom is full (7 s in it). Over the open piazza his jet runs out of puff and he sinks low: dive under his machine and fly through the plans case in his claw to snatch it ("Got the plans!"), and the era is won; his sulky line is "Bah! Flying machines will NEVER catch on!". Between stretches the machine lands by itself on a tower with a gold flag (the checkpoint and breather), the seats swap, and the new flapper's first beat takes off. Hazards are all soft: a tower, cloud, banner or the dome from the side is a "Bonk!" and a pop-back of about 230 px at the same height; the ground, a roof or the river at speed is a "Bump!" or "Splash!" and a bounce up; pigeons are announced 1 s ahead (the Thief points and a red "!" blinks at the right edge at their height) and a hit halves the speed; a near miss pops "Close!". While nobody plays, the machine hangs still and nothing moves. Gold help, always announced: 2 bumps on the same obstacle (and again at 4) give "Gold wind!" (that gap 45 px wider, gold arrows); 3 ground bumps in a stretch give "Gold feathers!" (each beat lifts longer); 3 times tired gives "Gold beat!" (a gold dot shows when to flap, quicker refill); 7 times tired gives "Gold wings!" (they never tire, so a kid who mashes can fly); 2 pigeon bumps give smaller flocks; 24 s of chase with the meter under half gives a wider gold slipstream that never drains. In the dive a miss is flying past him or a bump: he laughs, zips ahead and it starts again; 2 misses give "Gold claw!", 4 a bigger gold claw, 6 a "Gold magnet!" (flying past him at any height takes the plans). The longer a dive takes, the slower he flies (150 down to 70 px/s after 4 s), and he hovers while the machine is on the ground. History, in the news line and his taunts: Leonardo drew flying machines with flapping wings around 1490, about 400 years before the first aeroplane, and his never really flew (until today). Nothing needs more than about 2 presses a second or two directions at once. Stats in st.stats.davinci: pages, of, flaps, tired, close, shoos, stretch (seconds per stretch), diveMiss; the result line reads "Caught every page of Leonardo's plans" when all 111 are collected. Bot times: computer pair 2:04 to 2:06 with 0 bumps, clumsy partner 2:37 to 2:55, young kid 3:04 to 3:29, an idle partner never clears it. Tests: tools/w30/davinci-keys.js (keyboard, controller, touch, 1-player), davinci-course.js (trail clearance), davinci-dive.js (the dive done wrong, gold help), davinci-dbg.js (rare moments and draw time, 1.5 to 2.9 ms a frame), davinci-trace.js (the bot runner with machine and chase state).
  - **Era 7: The First Flight, 1903** (`wright`). Era 7, "The First Flight, 1903" (phase 'wright', js/levels/world30/tth-wright.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world30/tth-wright.js). Gavyn's pick: keep the wobbly first plane in the air; one holds the wings level, the other pedals the engine. Kitty Hawk, 17 December 1903: the Time Thief has stolen the Flyer's spare propeller. Pip and Bo both ride the Flyer over the dunes, scrolling right. Like the real day there are four flights (37, 53, 61 and 260 m; about 12, 20, 29 and 40 s), each longer and windier, and the two swap seats after every landing like Orville and Wilbur did. Every flight starts on the wooden launch rail: the Flyer only rolls while BOTH hold right. WINGS (action word "bell"): hold left / right to lean and bring the tilting plane level (lean toward the high end); tilted wings lose lift and speed, and 0.85 s past the red mark the low wing scrapes the sand ("Scrape!", a team bump) and the wind blows the Flyer back to the last flag (flags every 6-7 s). The action rings the bicycle bell, which scatters a seagull flock 100-430 px ahead (then it rests 3 s). ENGINE (action word "pedal", "grab" at the end): tap the action in a steady beat, about 1.5 taps a second, when the shrinking ring is home (0.4-1.05 s after the last tap); taps inside 0.28 s make it cough and three in a row flood it for 1.2 s. Power runs down by itself: under 60 % the plane slows and sinks, and a weak engine sets it down softly on the sand ("Engine out!", no bump) until it is pedalled up again. Up climbs, down dives; climbing heats the engine and at 100 % it steams for 2.6 s and cannot climb. Hazards, all announced at least 0.9 s ahead (the second half of a double gust 0.6 s): gusts from the Thief's jet with a big arrow (from flight 1; double gusts from flight 3), tall dunes to climb over (from flight 2; flying into one is a "Bump!", a plain dive onto flat sand only skims), seagull flocks high or low with a "!" sign (from flight 2; dive under, climb over, or ring the bell; a hit is a "Squawk!"), parachute sandbags aimed at the Flyer's height with a red target ring (from flight 3; "Bonk!", tips the plane) and downdrafts (from flight 3). A photographer stands in every flight (John T. Daniels took the famous photo): passing him with level wings is a "Snap! Perfect photo!" bonus (four of them give the stat line "Four perfect Flyer photos"). History in the news line: flight 1 was 37 m in 12 seconds, shorter than a jumbo jet's wing span; the brothers took turns; they were bicycle makers. Winning it back: at the end of flight 4 the Thief's jet runs out of puff and he hangs low; fly right under his claw with level wings for 2.2 s (a ring fills), then the engineer's next action press grabs the propeller ("Got it!"), the Flyer lands and he sulks ("Bah! Who needs a propeller? I have a JET!"). Gold help (always announced, kept for the era): 2 scrapes since the last flag or 3 in the era give steadier wings (4 / 5: steadier still); 2 sand bumps or engine-outs in a flight (3 in the era) or 2 floodings give gold pedals (power lasts longer, too-fast taps ignored, 30 % faster climb, less heat); 2 gull or sandbag hits in a flight (3 in the era) give gold arrows, smaller hit zones and a longer bell reach; 14 s into the catch the Thief holds still and the catch zone grows. Fairness: 1.6 s of grace after every take-off and pop-back, nothing hits during cards, the cutscene or while nobody plays. Never alone: take-off needs both; with no pedalling the plane sets down and waits; with nobody at the wings the tilt runs away after 3 s without a lean and the plane scrapes before the next flag (never eased by gold help); the catch needs level wings, the right height and the grab. Bot times: computer pair 2:19 (138.8-139.0 s, 0-1 bump), clumsy partner 2:38-2:52 (1-7 bumps), young kid 2:45-3:12 (6-9 bumps), one side idle never ends. Tests: node tools/w30/wright-bots.js (all bot runs), node tools/w30/wright-keys.js (keyboard, controller, touch, 1-player; 113 checks), tools/w30/wright-dbg.js (what led to each bump), tools/w30/wright-perf.js (draw time, about 2.7 ms for the era's drawing).
  - **Era 8: Moon Landing, 1969** (`moon`). Era 8, Moon Landing, 1969 (js/levels/world30/tth-moon.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world30/tth-moon.js). Gavyn's pick: land the moon lander before the Thief takes the flag; one works the thrusters, the other watches the fuel. Pip and Bo ride the lander Eagle down from orbit while the screen scrolls down; the Time Thief flies just ahead in his jet machine holding the Moon flag, with his own clock flag on the back. The course is built fresh each start, about 8300 px. The PILOT steers with left / right (side jets, free) and burns the main engine with up or the action button to slow the fall; the engine only burns from a small ready tank (3.2 s). The ENGINEER pumps with the action button: one stroke moves 1 s of fuel from the two side pods into the ready tank (a stroke takes 0.3 s; pumping when the tube is in its red top spills 0.6 s). The pods are not enough, so the Thief fumbles spare fuel cells (one about every 4.6 s, 3 s of fuel each) that float 100-185 px beside the way down; the engineer swings the grabber arm with left / right and the action grabs when a cell is at the claw ("GRAB!"). The arm only reaches about 120 px, so the pilot must fly close and slow, and the pilot cannot slow at all without the engineer's fuel. A cell goes into the pod on the arm's side; a lopsided lander tilts and drifts, and more than 1.3 cells of difference is too lopsided to land. Three stretches: High orbit (7 rows of drifting rocks and old satellites with one wide gap each; the Thief nudges a rock into a gap now and then: it blinks red with a "!" and an arrow for 0.9 s, then slides in over 1.2 s), Crater canyon (7 narrow passes; dust geysers blow across 3 of them: 1 s of warning puffs, 1.1 s of blast, 3 s of rest; a blast tosses the lander back up), Boulder field (6 rows of bigger boulders, more nudges, then the landing pad: touch down on the pad at 75 px/s or slower, not sliding, level). Between the stretches a hover beacon is the checkpoint and breather: fly through its ring under 60 px/s for 0.8 s; the lander docks, the pods are topped up to half and the jobs swap, so each player flies and fuels. Soft fails only, all team bumps: hitting a rock, satellite or wall faster than 105 px/s is a "Bonk!" and a bounce (slower touches are free, the lander can rest on things); falling past a beacon is "Too fast!" and a pop-back 240 px above it; a bad landing is a floaty bounce to try again. Free fall is capped at 215 px/s, with speed streaks and "Close!" pops for near misses. Gold help, always announced: 3 spills = gold safety valve (no more spills); 4 cells missed while low on fuel = gold magnet claw; both tanks empty for 2.2 s = the Thief tosses a gold fuel cell that floats beside the arm until the engineer grabs it; 4 bonks in a stretch = gold bumpers; 3 geyser blasts = sleepy geysers (5 s rest); 2 missed beacons = a bigger, more forgiving gold beam, 4 = a gold tractor beam that holds the lander at the ring, which fills while the pilot burns; 2 bad landings = a wider gold pad and 100 px/s allowed, 4 = gold landing legs (125 px/s, sliding and lopsided forgiven). Winning the flag back: the touchdown thump bounces the Thief's machine in the low gravity, he drops the Moon flag and his clock flag drifts off into space; Pip and Bo hop out with slow moon hops and plant the flag together by both standing at it and holding the action for 1.3 s; he sulks: "Bah! One giant SULK for me. Keep your flag!". An idle partner stalls everything: no engineer means no fuel to slow down, no pilot means nothing slows or steers, both beacons need a burning engine, and the flag needs both buttons; while nobody plays an autopilot holds the lander still. Facts in the news line and taunts: 20 July 1969, the lander was called Eagle and landed with about 25 s of fuel left, the Moon's gravity is one sixth of Earth's, twelve people have walked on the Moon. HUD: fuel gauges on the left (ready tube, two pods, seconds in all), the race-down bar and falling speed on the right; the time pill shows the fuel in seconds. Stats: stretch times, cells grabbed and missed, pumps, spills, landing tries, fuel left at touchdown, close calls, gold help used. Bot times: computer pair 2:12-2:21 with no gold help; a clumsy partner 2:25-2:50; a young kid 2:50-4:15 with gold help; an idle partner never ends it. Tests: tools/w30/moon-all.js (bot matrix), moon-keys.js (real keyboard, controllers, touch), moon-alone.js (nobody clears alone), moon-perf.js (draw time, about 3.8 ms a frame), moon-trace.js, moon-foot.js, moon-dbg.js.
  - **Era 9: The End of Time** (`finale`). Era 9, The End of Time (finale). A brass clockwork tower in a swirling clock sky, climbed upward in 6 ledges, then the Time Machine is smashed together at the top. Two jobs that swap at every ledge, so each player does each job three times; Pip brakes first. The HOLDER stands at the brake lever and holds the action: while it is held the huge clock hand stops, the gears slow to a ridable speed (green lamps) and the folding teeth lock out. A grip meter drains in 7 s and refills in 5 s; when it runs out the lever slips and must be let go and rested. The CLIMBER climbs the clockwork: a step, gear A, the safe shelf, gear B, the hatch, then presses the action at the button on the next deck. That stops the clock and sends the lift down for the holder; both on the new deck is the checkpoint and "Swap jobs!". Each climb has a new step: a shelf, a pendulum, two teeth that click in and out, a piston, a faster pendulum, teeth again. Gears cannot be skipped and are too fast to ride without the brake ("Too fast!", "Whoa!"), so the climber always needs the holder. The huge hand sweeps round in 22 to 13 s (unbraked); at 12 the Thief rewinds whoever is up in the clockwork back along their path. The safe shelf is a checkpoint: a rewind or a fall after it only goes back to the shelf. From climb 2 the Thief throws tick-tock bubbles (a ring shows the spot for the 1.5 s flight; inside it you are frozen for 1 s). On climbs 5 and 6 the hand fights back: while the brake is held the lever shakes, flashes red and a ring closes on it for 0.9 s, then it kicks. Let go before the kick and grab again ("Dodged!"); hold on and you are "Kicked!" (grip gone, a counted soft fail). The clockwork holds still from the warning until 0.45 s after a dodged kick, so letting go early costs nothing. At the top there are no jobs: each player stands at a plunger and both press the action together when the lamps turn green (within 0.55 s). 4 hits; a lone ram just gets "Together!". Between hits the Thief throws bubbles and rolls cogs to jump (telegraphed 0.9 s). Every hit shakes two of the 8 stolen things out of the machine and they fly home one by one into little portals named after their eras, each with its line ("The T. rex egg is home, 66 million years ago!"). After hit 4 the machine bursts, the Thief plops out dizzy, the last two things fly home, "Everything is home. History is safe!", and he opens one last portal: "race you home!". Gold help, always announced: 2 rewinds slow the hand, 4 stick it; 3 shake-offs or 24 s on one climb give gold grip (the brake holds twice as long, gears speed up slowly); 40 s gives sleepy gears and a stuck hand; 3 freezes give sleepy bubbles; 3 kicks and the hand is too tired to kick; 26 s with no new height slows the clockwork; at the top 3 lone rams widen the window (0.9 s, then 1.5 s) and 3 trick hits make the tricks sleepy. Nothing can be cleared alone: gears need the brake, the lift needs the button, a hit needs both rams. History facts in the news line: the first clocks had only an hour hand; the first pendulum clock was built in 1656; tick-tock is the escapement's teeth. Controls: move, jump, and one action (hold "brake", press "press", press "smash"). Bot times: computer pair 2:03; clumsy partner 2:18 to 2:43; young kid 4:10 to 4:52; an idle player never gets through. Tests: tools/w30/finale-keys.js (real keys, controller, touch, 1-player), finale-dbg.js, finale-perf.js.
  - **Era 10: Portal Race Home** (`race`). Era 10, Portal Race Home (js/levels/world30/tth-race.js, brain js/cpu/levels/world30/tth-race.js; names tthRace* / TTHRACE*). History is safe and the Time Thief is a friend now, so the way home is a versus race inside the team level. Pip and Bo run on foot in a split screen (Pip's lane on top, Bo's below, each with its own camera); the other racer shows as a faded ghost in your lane and the bar between the lanes shows both faces, the lap marks and the home portal. Best of 3 races, first to 2 wins is the Race champ. A race is 3 laps and every lap is a different time period with its own look and trick; between laps you run through a portal ring (through it at speed = "Time boost!"). Race 1 Ancient Dash: dinosaur jungle (hop logs, a shadow means a meteor lands there 0.8 s later: hop the hot rock), Stonehenge (slide under the lintels), Olympia (hurdles in a rhythm). Race 2 Heroes' Run: walls of Troy (jump the gaps), Viking ice (slippery, a long slide is the fast way), the Moon (low gravity, huge floaty jumps over craters). Race 3 Sky Sprint: Florence rooftops (jump in an updraft to float over wide alleys), Kitty Hawk dunes (duck through headwind gusts), the Time Tunnel (a bit of everything). Controls, the same for both players: hold right to run (speed is kept in the air, in a slide and for 0.35 s after letting go, so a touch player can lift the thumb to tap up or down; nothing needs two directions at once), left brakes, up (the jump button) hops, down slides or ducks (down in the air drops fast), the action is "dash" (a burst of speed, then a 3.6 s cooldown shown as a little bar under your feet). First-time hints over each runner name that player's own keys; the computer's runner gets none. Soft fails only, and they are race slowdowns, never team bumps: running into a thing is "Bonk!" (a short stumble), a gap is "Whoops!" (you pop back out on the far side, slow), standing up in a gust is "Whoa!". Fairness: both lanes get the same hand-made track; everything is seen at least 1.5 s ahead; a dead heat (within 0.002 s) replays the race, a second tie in a row is settled by fewer stumbles, then more time boosts, then the Thief's lucky watch; a safety bell ends a race nobody finishes. Gold help, always announced: whoever trails in races gets Gold shoes (a quicker dash, softer stumbles, longer time boosts; shown on the race card, in the news and as a chip in the lane), and a racer more than 380 px behind at a portal ring gets a longer "Gold boost!". After the same kind of miss twice, a hint says how ("Hop a little sooner"). The Thief is the starter and commentator at a little desk between the lanes for the whole era: he counts down, calls lead changes and cheers; each lap's name, date and a true fact go in the news line (for example "The first Olympic Games had just one event: a sprint!"). The race and its countdown freeze while nobody plays (the Thief waits), and if the people were away at GO the race is started again when they are back, so the computer never wins alone. After the deciding race comes a short crowning scene at the gold HOME portal: the Thief trots in and hands his Golden Hourglass to the champ on the winner's step ("Race champ: Pip!"), then the core's ending starts. Results line: "Race champ: Bo (2-1)"; stats in st.stats.race (wins, champ, races, stumbles, boosts, best). In 1-player mode the computer is the opponent at the saved Opponent setting (Easy / Normal / Hard): Hard beats Easy nearly always from either side, a kid-like player beats Easy most of the time. Numbers: laps 5,800-6,000 px, top speed 300 px/s (+45 flow, +160 dash); a race is a 5.5 s card and countdown, about 43 s of running for a Normal computer pair (Hard 41, Easy 52) and 3.4 s of result; the crowning is 6 s. Bot times: computer pair 1:49 when it goes 2-0 and 2:45 when it goes 2-1; clumsy and kid bots 1:48-1:55; idle never ends. Tests: tools/w30/play.js race, tools/w30/race-keys.js (real keyboard, controller, touch, 1-player), tools/w30/race-balance.js (skills against each other), tools/w30/race-perf.js and race-prof.js (draw time).
- Code: the core `js/levels/world30/time-thief.js` (MODES.timethief, the `TTH_PHASES` registry with a working stub per era, the Thief's drawing, portals, loot, bumps, badges, bubbles, gold help, the idle wait, the portal cutscene, checkpoints, the era strip, the ending, stats, the test hook `MODES.timethief.test`; its header comment is the contract every era follows) and one file per era `js/levels/world30/tth-<era>.js`. Names are prefixed `tth` / `TTH` (`tt` belongs to Tomb Torch). Brains: `js/cpu/levels/world30/time-thief.js` (core, `TTH_BRAINS`) plus `tth-<era>.js`. Preview: `js/previews/world30.js` (prefix `pw30`). Tests: `tools/w30/play.js <era|all> --who=pair|noisy|kid|idle` (one era with bots), `tools/w30/shot.js` (screenshots) and per-era scripts (`<era>-keys.js` for keyboard, controller and touch).
- World 30 adds itself from its own file (`WORLDS[29]`, `LEVELS.push`). World 29 must load before it in `index.html` so the boss stays the last level.
- Status: built; each era tested alone (computer pair, clumsy partner, young-kid bot, idle, real keyboard, controller and touch, 1-player both ways, desktop and phone) and the whole level end to end with the computer.

### World 31: Shrunk in the Backyard (mixed)
Pip and Bo are the size of beetles: a grass blade is a tree, a pebble is a boulder, an ant is as big as a pony and a robin is a monster. World 6 Backyard Bash is the backyard at normal size; this world is seen from down among the grass roots, and the big things are meant to be a bit scary (Gavyn: not a toddler game). Gavyn picked the theme and these four levels from ten ideas (Nov 2026); he skipped Sprinkler Surf, Spider Web Rescue, Bumblebee Ride, Garden Hose Rapids, Bottle Cap Boat and Beetle Wrestling. World index 30, added after the World 30 boss.

- **31-1 Lawnmower Escape** (scrolling team, mode `mowrun`, ownStep + ownHUD + noFalls, roomWord 'Stretch', act labels `chop` / `bend` swapping per stretch): a giant red mower roars along behind. Both run ◀ ▶ and jump ▲ (a plain jump clears acorn caps, bottle caps, little stones). The CUTTER taps the action at a clump of tall grass to chop through it (3-6 chops, notches show what's left). The SPRINGER holds the action at the foot of a pebble or fallen stick to bend a springy grass blade down (Gavyn, 2026-11-15; it replaced cupped hands and a pull-up); the cutter runs onto its tip and jumps ▲, and the blade flings them about 200 px up on top (SPROING!); then the cutter, on top at the near edge, holds the action to drop a loop of old kite string, and the springer jumps ▲ to grab it and climbs (hold ▲, 210 px/s; ▼ slides down). Once someone is on the string it stays down. (The QA fixes below are from before the change: "boost" there means the fling, "booster's hands" the bent blade, "pull up" the string.) The mower has a speed per stretch (80, 88, 96, 104 px/s), hurries when far behind (x1.35 past 420 px, x2.4 past 640 px) and eases (x0.7) under 150 px; a red edge and the Mower meter show how close it is. Caught: "WHOOSH!", both tumble back to the last safe spot (just past the last clump, pebble or stick both got over) and the mower backs off 600 px (Close calls). Dewdrops: 55, some only reachable from a boost. Stretches: Front Lawn (Pip cuts), Dandelion Patch (Bo), Pebble Path (Pip), The Big Finish (Bo; ends on the patio). Gold help: caught twice in one stretch = gold sprinkler, the mower sputters at 60% for 20 s. Idle 12 s: "Taking a break", the mower waits. Medal: Gold <= 2:40 with <= 1 close call and >= 75% dew; Silver <= 4:30 with <= 5. Brain reads `MODES.mowrun.plan(st)` (per job a target x, hold/tap action, jump). Bot times: computer pair 1:52 (0 close calls); noisy partner 2:44-2:50; idle never clears. Polish after Gavyn's first play (2026-11-11, "the AI gets stuck"): a person could leave the computer stranded on the wrong side of a pebble or stick (the cutter walking off the top, or sailing over it from a boost), and the computer then walked back to the wrong side. Now a boosted jump always lands on the top it was aimed at, the one up top can't walk off the far side while the partner is still in front of it ("Pull Bo up first"), dropping onto the booster's hands counts as a boost (so an early or late jump still works), the boost range is 36 px (was 28), and the plan treats anyone already down the far side as over. The mower is drawn as a whole machine: engine dome, handle, headlight eyes, a toothed bumper, the blade spinning under the deck and a rolling front wheel.
- **31-2 Ant Hill Heist** (scrolling team, mode `antheist`, ownStep + ownHUD + noFalls, roomWord 'Tunnel', no action button): side view of the tunnels inside an ant hill. Pip holds the left end of a giant cookie crumb, Bo the right. Both pushing ◀ ▶ the same way moves it (130 px/s, quiet); one alone drags it (35 px/s) and after 0.5 s the scrape makes a noise ring (320 px) that wakes sleeping soldiers; opposite ways is a tug. Hold ▼ to freeze under the crumb. At a step up both press ▲ within 0.4 s to lift it. Soldier ants walk upside down on the ceiling and feel 150 px ahead of the head, 60 px behind (a glow shows the zone when near); in a zone the "?" fills in 0.5 s if the crumb moves, 1.6 s if someone is standing up, and empties if both are frozen. Full: "GOTCHA!", the crumb is hauled back to the last checkpoint flag (Caught), then 2 s of calm. Patrol ants (speed x1.3 of the course number, 0.8 s turn), sleeping soldiers (wake for 7 s on a scrape, zone 170 px), march shafts (a column every 6-7 s for 2.4-3 s, drum warning 2 s before; within 70 px you must be frozen). Fooled ants (review, Nov 2026): a patrol ant whose zone has been over the crumb for 0.4 s or more with both frozen, and then leaves it without a "?", is fooled ("Just a crumb...", a green tick): it ignores the crumb for 6 s (`S.foolT`), and the plan skips it. So one good freeze always gets you past a patrol ant; before, an ant that turned round near the crumb passed over it two or three times and the crumb sat still for up to 35 s. Sugar grains: 43. Tunnels: The Way In (2 ants), Nursery Hall (2 ants, 3 sleepers), Marching Shafts (3 shafts, 1 ant), The Queen's Gate (2 ants, 2 sleepers, 2 shafts). Course rule: keep 360 px or more between a shaft and the nearest end of an ant's patrol (their zones must not touch, or the crumb has nowhere to wait between them), and put checkpoint flags outside the patrols. Gold help: caught twice in a tunnel = gold sugar dust, zones shrink to 60%. Medal: Gold <= 4:15, caught <= 1, >= 75% sugar; Silver <= 7:30, caught <= 5. `MODES.antheist.plan(st)` runs the ants' own movement ahead (go only if 0.4 s of pushing stays clear; freeze if a zone reaches the crumb within 0.6 s standing still). Brain: with a person it never pushes alone (it pushes while the person pushes, or tapped ▶ in the last 0.35 s, so a person who taps ▶ instead of holding it still moves the crumb), presses ▲ right after the person at steps, and after a "wait" holds off 0.3 s before pushing again (following an ant used to be a push on every other step). The crumb is drawn as a chocolate chip cookie wedge; the tunnel has a soft dark ceiling, warm floor light and green glow pools under the mushrooms. Bot times after the review (shorter tunnels, fooled ants): computer pair 2:55 (0 caught; it was 4:21), a careful person-like partner 3:03, a noisy partner about 7-8 min (9 of 10 clear inside 10 min; before, 1 of 3); idle never clears. Still the longest of the four.
- **31-3 Bird Attack Dash** (scrolling team, mode `birddash`, ownStep + ownHUD + noFalls, roomWord 'Stretch', act labels `warn` / `dive` swapping per stretch): a robin as big as a house circles off the top of the screen. Cycle: circle (2.3-4.8 s by stretch) -> eye 1.0 s (stares at whoever is farthest from cover; if nobody is in the open it climbs away) -> tuck 0.6 s -> dive 0.85-1.0 s (steers after its target for the first 60%, so you can't outrun it) -> strike -> rise 1.3 s. Under a hiding spot (leaf, flowerpot, toadstool, cup, clover; closed-eye badge) you are safe ("THUNK!"); caught in the open = "SNATCHED!", dropped back at the last hiding spot both reached. The WATCHER lies down and holds ▼ to look up: the spyglass circle at the top shows the robin; the action during the stare = "LOOK OUT!" and the strike spot shows at once (about 2 s warning). A warn while it circles is a False alarm (1.2 s lockout). No warn: the strike spot shows only when the dive starts. The DASHER's action is a dive-roll (0.32 s at 1.7x, 1 s rest). Seeds: 49. Stretches: Open Lawn (Pip watches), Clover Patch (Bo), The Flower Bed (Pip), The Bird Bath (Bo; the robin sometimes stares and looks away). Gold help: snatched twice = gold pinwheel, strike spots show as soon as it stares. Medal: Gold <= 3:00, snatched <= 1, >= 70% seeds; Silver <= 6:00, snatched <= 5. Brain: it knows only what the screen shows (the spyglass while the watcher looks, the robin when low, the strike spot). What the spyglass showed stays known for 1.5 s after the watcher gets up (`st.seenSky`, `knows` in the plan). Without that the plan flipped between "clear, go" and "nobody is looking, wait" on every step, so the computer watcher twitched between lying down and standing and crawled at about 9 px/s (found in the review; this was the biggest "computer gets stuck" in the world). Bot times after the fix: computer pair 1:34 (0 snatches; it was 2:39 with 1), a careful person-like partner 1:50, noisy partner about 1:57; idle never clears.
- **31-4 Snail Race** (versus, best of 5, 60 s rounds, mode `snailrace`, ownStep, `act: {label: 'mash'}`): split screen, Pip's lane on top, Bo's below, the same course in each, a progress strip between. Each action press is +22 px/s; it slows by itself. Over the line (110 px/s) the nerve ring fills (faster the further over) and when full: "EEK!", the snail hides 1.3 s. ▲ ▼ steer between 3 paths (a jump press also steers up). Pebbles and wandering ladybugs: bump = 0.8 s hide. Puddles: +70 px/s at once (can tip you over the line). Lettuce: 0.6 s munch. Hills: uphill slows, downhill pushes. Rounds: Garden Path, Mossy Hill, Puddle Run, Ladybug Crossing, Grand Garden Prix. First over the line wins; time up: further along by 20 px wins. tiebreak: fewer shell scares. Gold help (behind by a round): gold shell, the line is 128 and nerves fill slower. Brain: targets Easy 80% / Normal 88% / Hard 94% of the line, taps no faster than every 0.2 / 0.14 / 0.1 s, eases off before downhills and puddles (not Easy), Easy mashes excited bursts; steering lookahead 160/240/320 px, misses 35% / 12% / 3% of pebbles and bugs. Bot results: rounds about 36 s; Normal vs Normal even; Hard beats Easy every match from both sides.
- Code: `js/levels/world31/` (the shared kit `shrunk-kit.js`, `SHRUNK`, is World 29's `NINJA` kit with daylight garden scenery: sky with the giant fence, far grass, blades, soil, pebbles, twigs, clover, dandelions, daisies, toadstools, leaves, dewdrops, seeds, and `shrunk*` sounds; its header is the manual), brains in `js/cpu/levels/world31/`, previews in `js/previews/world31.js` (helpers `pw31*`), tools in `tools/w31/` (`trace.js <mode> --expr=...` prints level state as a computer pair plays, `shots.js <mode> <times>`, `input.js` real keyboard check, `stuck.js [modes] --human=lag,eager,slow` plays the team levels with a person-like partner (late reactions, running ahead, slow with pauses) on both characters and lists every spot where the computer didn't move for 8 s, with the plan; use it for every new team level). The kit's far fence is now solid boards behind a haze, and the far grass is opaque, so the scenery no longer looks washed out. Like World 29, each def sits at the bottom of its own level file as `SHRUNK.defs.<mode>`; `world.js` adds the world and pushes the four in order.
- Status: built and bot-tested (Nov 2026), then reviewed by a second agent (see the review entry under World 32); not yet played by hand since the review. Keyboard checked through the real key path; controller and touch use the same p.input fields as every other level.

### World 32: Superhero City (mixed)
Pip and Bo are superheroes for a day in a big city at night. In every team level each of them has a different power and the two powers only work together; the powers swap between stretches so both players try both. Gavyn picked the theme and these four levels (Nov 2026): Runaway Subway, Bridge Collapse, Rooftop Chase, Power Punch Showdown. Pip wears a red cape, Bo a gold one.

- **32-1 Runaway Subway** (scrolling team, mode `subway`, ownStep + ownHUD + noFalls, roomWord 'Stretch'): a train with no brakes races through the tunnel. `G.mst.zoom` is the SPEEDSTER this stretch, the other is the STRONG HERO. Each car has a glowing brake lever; the speedster holds the action at it (1.1 s to pull, the pull drains if let go). The pull shakes loose a steel girder from the roof (red warning 0.85 s, falls 0.32 s); the lever stops at 92% until the girder is held, so the strong hero must stand under it and hold the action to catch it (within 34 px) and keep holding until the lever clunks. A miss or an early let-go is a crash (a dent, the lever resets, the speedster is stunned). Hazards by tunnel distance: crates on the walkway (the speedster jumps them) and low pipes on the roof (the strong hero ducks ▼); no new pipes while a girder is up, and pipes ding off a held girder. A pipe that reaches the strong hero while a girder is coming down near them clangs off it too (review fix: the hero can't duck and catch at once, and a pipe already on its way when the lever was pulled cost a bonk and a dent even for a computer pair). Each lever slows the train (`v0*(1-0.72*done/n)`); after the last one it stops at a station. Stretches: Downtown Line (4 cars), Under the River (5), Express Uptown (6), The Last Stop (6). Gold help (3 bad events in a stretch): the next 2 levers pull in 0.45 s with no girder. Medal: gold 170 s, silver 300 s. The strong hero stands with elbows out and gloved hands up (K.suit `arms: 'ready'`, `hands: true`), braced to catch, and straight up while holding a girder (Gavyn, Nov 2026). Tokens: 3 on each car roof and 2 on each walkway, past that car's lever; pulling the last lever stops the train and ends the stretch, so the last car's tokens must be fetched before then. The computer fetches every token on its own track (`coinFor` in the brain: missed ones behind the next lever, the whole last car before its lever, and it stands under a high one and jumps straight up); a computer pair gets 105/105 in about 109 s (was 75/105 in 97 s).
- **32-2 Bridge Collapse** (scrolling team, mode `bridge`, ownStep + ownHUD + noFalls, roomWord 'Stretch'): a storm is breaking a long bridge. `G.mst.str` is the STRETCHY hero, `G.mst.lift` the STRONG hero. The strong hero taps the action to lift or set down a bus (carried at 145 px/s, no jumping while carrying).
