The stretchy hero holds the action at a gap's edge (within 32 px, gaps up to 300 px) to stretch across it as a rubber bridge; it sags and strains (7 s, twice as fast with the bus on it); letting go with someone on gives 0.5 s grace, then it snaps. Let go with nobody on and the stretchy hero boings across to the strong hero's side (that is also how they cross a slab that crumbled behind the bus). Cracked slabs crumble 2.2 s after first touched; live wires lash down (idle 2.3 s, red warning 1.05 s, zap 0.4 s; a zap drops the bus). A fall is a rescue back to the last safe spot. Stretches: Rush Hour, The Middle Span, Live Wires, The Last Tower (storm rising). Hero badges: 26. One over a gap is collected by anyone walking over the rubber bridge, however far it sags; a high one is at most 100 px up, which a plain jump reaches (review fix: before, 20 of the 26 could not be reached at all, so the 70% for Gold was impossible). Gold help: a gold cable, no strain for the rest of the stretch. Medal: gold 180 s, silver 320 s.
- **32-3 Rooftop Chase** (scrolling team, mode `roofchase`, ownStep + ownHUD + noFalls, roomWord 'Stretch'): chase a jewel thief over the roofs. `G.mst.web` is the WEB-SLINGER, `G.mst.gho` the GHOST. Small gaps, AC boxes, vents and steps are jumped. At a wide gap the web-slinger taps the action at the edge (within 76 px) for a web line to the hook above; the ghost taps next to them (46 px) to hold on and they swing together (a pendulum with a push big enough for the far roof); the web-slinger taps again to let go ("Let go!" shows when a let-go would land). Presses before the swing passes its lowest point are ignored (so button-mashing can't drop them), and a second press within 0.5 s of the web line doesn't cancel it. Too early or too late: the web pulls both back to the start of the swing (a slip). Guard drones sweep beams over the roofs: the ghost holds the action to vanish (the meter drains 0.12/s alone, 0.19/s with a partner within 52 px who vanishes too, refills after 0.5 s, locks when empty until 30%); seen for 0.25 s = netted, both go back before the drone. Stretches: Moonlit Roofs, Neon Alley, The Building Site, Clock Tower (the thief is webbed up at the end). Gold help (3 slips or spots): gold goggles, narrower beams and a slower drain. Medal: gold 200 s, silver 360 s.
- **32-4 Power Punch Showdown** (versus, best of 5, 45 s rounds, mode `punchout`, ownStep, `act: {label: 'punch'}`): a rooftop brawl. One roof (x 120-840, y 440) with two steel catwalks (y 330) and a neon sign (y 240) to jump up through. Move, jump (double jump), tap the action to punch (wind 0.07 s, hits 0.07-0.17 s, ready again at 0.32 s), hold ▼ on the ground to block (a fifth of the damage, little push). A punch adds 9% (giant fists 14%) and knocks back 120 + 3.4 px/s per %; a hit gives the double jump back and 0.3 s of can't-be-hit after the stagger (the hero flickers), so nobody gets punched again and again. Both punches are checked before either lands (a trade hits both). Falling just past an end of the roof (not while staggered) grabs the edge and climbs up. Fall off = RING OUT, the other wins the round; time up: less damage wins by more than 5%. Power-ups drop on parachutes every 7-10 s (one at a time, gone after 9 s): SPEED, FLIGHT (hold jump), SHIELD (stops one punch) and GIANT FISTS, 7 s each (shield 10 s). tiebreak: more punches landed. Gold help (a round behind): a gold shield bubble at the start of the round. Brain: punch reaction Easy 0.4 / Normal 0.22 / Hard 0.12 s, blocks 12% / 35% / 60% of punches, goes for power-ups when closer (Easy notices half), double-jumps back to the roof; Easy wanders and swings at the air now and then. Bot results: rounds about 25-35 s; Normal vs Normal even; Hard beats Normal and Normal beats Easy every match.
- Code: `js/levels/world32/` (the shared kit `hero-kit.js`, `HEROCITY`, reuses World 31's `SHRUNK` helpers and adds the superhero look `K.suit` (cape, mask, chest star, power glow), `K.comic` bursts, `K.speedLines`, the night/dusk city sky and skyline, building facades, rooftop props, and `city*` sounds; its header is the manual), brains in `js/cpu/levels/world32/`, previews in `js/previews/world32.js` (helpers `pw32*`), tools in `tools/w32/` (`stuck.js` person-like partners, `trace.js`, `shots.js`). Each def sits at the bottom of its level file as `HEROCITY.defs.<mode>`; `world.js` adds the world (`WORLDS[31]`) and pushes the four in order. Each team level has a `plan(st)` that the prompts, the HUD chips and the brain all read.
- Testing: `node tools/w32/stuck.js subway,bridge,roofchase` plays each team level with the computer partnered with a late, an excited and a slow person-like player; all clear. Fixed along the way: a stretchy hero stranded behind a slab that crumbled (now stretches and boings across), the ghost and web-slinger flicking between "wait" and "go" at a drone when the meter was right at the line (now needs 8% to spare to start), the ghost setting off before the web-slinger held hands, and a lagging web-slinger's extra presses dropping the swing.
- Status: built and bot-tested (Nov 2026), then reviewed by a second agent; not yet played by hand.
- **The comic-book look** (Gavyn, Nov 2026: "something that looks straight out of a comic book"; he approved the third round of mockups, then it was built from `pip-and-bo/notes/world32-comic-book-restyle-spec.md` in the project files). Art only: no rule, timing, layout, medal or computer-partner change. World 32 only; World 31's `SHRUNK` look and the shared engine are untouched.
  - `js/levels/world32/comic-kit.js` (`COMIC`, loaded before `hero-kit.js`): black ink outlines (`#14121c`), flat bold colours, halftone dot fades and dot screens (pre-rendered tiles, cached per colour and canvas resolution), Bangers and Comic Neue lettering (`index.html` loads both), caption boxes, speech balloons with tails, jagged bursts, big hand-lettered sound words, focus lines, and the printed finish (`COMIC.finish`: a faint dot screen, a cream multiply, then a white gutter and a wobbly ink panel border; the off-register copy of the whole canvas is still in the kit but `K.page` leaves it off, because it cost about 50x the rest of a frame on a slowed CPU). Its header is the manual; `HEROCITY` copies its helpers onto `K`.
  - Engine hook: `render.js` calls the mode's `printPage()` (via `drawHook('printPage')`) after the scene and before the HUD, so the print finish sits under the HUD. World 32's four modes use it (`printPage() { K.page(); }`).
  - HUD: the four defs set `ownPills` and draw `K.pills(st)`: a yellow caption box "32-3  ROOFTOP CHASE" and, for team levels, a pale-yellow italic narration line "PART 1 OF 4:  AFTER THAT THIEF!" from each stretch's `narr`. `K.counter` (time and pickups, yellow box), `K.panel` (cream player cards with a colour header and a tilted portrait panel in the hero's costume), `K.chip` (white, blinking yellow when it's time, green while held), `K.meter`, `K.card` (cream boxes for the speed dial, strain bar and the 32-4 damage cards), `K.drawMsg` (a caption box), `K.prompter` (prompts as white speech balloons with a tail to the hero). `K.pop(st, x, y, str, col, size, { from })` makes a speech balloon that follows someone (the 32-3 thief). The 32-4 match pill and clock are the engine's and are unchanged.
  - Sound words on real events (`K.sound(st, x, y, word, o)`: at most 2 at once, kept off the heroes, skipped off screen and when the same word is already up; `K.drawWords` draws them in `drawTop`): 32-1 CRRACK! (a girder shakes loose), CLANG! (caught), KRUNCH! (crash), SCREEEECH! (lever home), BONK! (pipe); 32-2 CREEEAK! (crack), CRASH! (slab falls), ZZZAP! (wire), BOING! (boing over or snap), RRRUMBLE (thunder in a big storm); 32-3 THWIP! (web line at the hook), WHOOSH! (bottom of the swing), ZZT! (spotted), SNAG! (net closes), GOTCHA! (the thief webbed); 32-4 the punch bursts (POW!/BAM!/WHAM!/BIFF!/KA-POW!, bigger, behind the fighters) and RING OUT!. Focus lines (`K.focusAt`) for about 0.25 s on a caught girder, a landed punch and the thief's capture. None of these touch `Math.random`, so the sim stays the same.
  - Heroes: `K.suit` draws the body with an ink outline (it swaps `PDARK[id]` for ink while drawing) and a costume per job (`kit`: speed goggles and bolt, strong belt and gauntlets, stretch stripes, web lines, ghost hood with the dashed vanished look, champ belt and gloves); the power glow is comic spikes. Costume colours follow the outfit rule (Pip no blue, Bo no orange or red); Bo's chest star is white now (it was red).
  - Backgrounds: `K.sky` (flat night or dusk bands with dot fades, an inked moon, clouds with the hero signal, the blimp, a helicopter, birds, a shooting star, searchlights; `storm`, `dawn`, `deep` options), `K.skyline` (far towers, landmarks, mid towers, the elevated train, near towers with neon, tanks and billboards; `neon`, `site` (cranes and scaffolds), `clock` + `clockA` options). 32-3 varies each stretch: Moonlit Roofs, Neon Alley (more neon), Building Site (cranes, an orange dawn band), Clock Tower (deeper navy; the clock tower in the distance comes closer, then fades out as the real one at the finish comes on screen). 32-2's storm grows each stretch. Playable surfaces get the brightest edges (roof cornices, the 32-4 roof edge and catwalks: thick outline and a cream top edge).
  - 32-4 keeps a still copy of its sky and skyline (taken straight off the canvas the first frame, retaken when the canvas changes size) and draws the blimp, birds and searchlights over it each frame.
  - Some colours were picked so a hero never stands on its own colour: the 32-1 tunnel bricks per stretch, the cream train skirt, the darker 32-2 river.
  - Frame time: `node tools/w32/perf.js [codes] [--throttle=4] [--frames=600]` (median and 95th percentile of `render()` with the CPU slowed down). Previews: `node tools/preview-shots.js 32`.
  - Review of the comic look (Nov 2026, a second agent). Gameplay is untouched: cpu-test pair / noisy / idle and physics-check give the same results as main, and `tools/w32/stuck.js` clears all 54 runs. Fixed:
    - **Speed.** `tools/w32/perf.js` only times the drawing calls, not the work of putting the pixels on the canvas, so it missed the real cost: played live in a browser that draws the canvas with the CPU, the levels ran at 23 / 15 / 16 / 32 frames a second where main ran at 50 / 39 / 50 / 52. Nearly all of it was pattern fills: every dot fade, and the fine dot screen over the whole page. Dot fades are now strips drawn once at the screen's own pixel size and copied on with `drawImage`, pixel for pixel (`fadeFast` in `comic-kit.js`; a fade under a rotation or another scale still uses the pattern), and the page's dot screen is one picture the size of the canvas. Same look; now 47 / 28 / 31 / 50. **Measure a drawing change by frames a second with the game running live** (count `requestAnimationFrame` calls for a few seconds), not only by timing `render()`.
    - Focus lines were thick black wedges across the whole screen on every punch and catch; they are thin now (about 10 px at most mid-screen) and lighter.
    - Costumes: Bo's stretchy suit had wide white stripes and read as a white hero (now thin light-teal stripes, yellow on Pip); the ghost's hood was the same white for both (now tinted peach for Pip, pale blue for Bo).
    - 32-1: the printed shade on the train windows was drawn over the passengers and faded their shirts; it goes on the glass first now.
    - Left as built: the 32-4 match pill and clock are the engine's; the off-register print copy stays off.
- Review of Worlds 31 and 32 (Nov 2026, a second agent, after Gavyn found World 31's computer partner getting stuck). Method: screenshots of all eight levels, `tools/w31/stuck.js` and `tools/w32/stuck.js`, cpu-test pair / noisy / idle, physics-check, a read of the level code, and two new checks worth repeating on every new team level: (1) count how often a computer player's held keys flip per second (12 or more is a twitch: the plan depends on something the computer's own move changes, so it flips every step); (2) print the level's end stats for a computer pair and check that Gold is possible (collectables in reach, no bumps or dents for a pair that plays perfectly). Fixed: 31-3 watcher twitch (above), 31-2 length and repeated ant passes (above), 32-1 pipe during a girder and the speedster landing on a slow crate (it now carries on past a crate while in the air), 32-1 duck flicker, 32-2 badges out of reach, 32-4 key hints (run and jump were missing from the top bar). Left alone: 31-1 and 31-4 and 32-3 and 32-4 played well in every check; comic words ("Nice swing!", "Stretch!") can sit behind a prompt bubble for a moment.

### World 33: Storm Chasers (mixed)
Pip and Bo chase wild weather in a storm van: outrun a tornado, climb out of a flooding canyon, cross a blizzard roped together, and in the versus level they ARE the tornadoes. Gavyn picked the theme and levels 1, 2, 7 and 9 from the ideas list (Nov 2026; spec in the project files, `pip-and-bo/notes/world33-storm-chasers-spec.md`). World index 32. The first world built on Gavyn's three-step workflow (ideas thread, build thread, then a separate review thread before merging). Three of the four levels scroll.

- **33-1 Tornado Alley Run** (scrolling team, mode `tornadorun`, ownStep + ownHUD + noFalls, roomWord 'Stretch', act labels `horn` / `swing` swapping per stretch): the van races right along a farm road with a huge tornado on the left edge. The DRIVER (`G.mst.drv`) changes between 3 lanes with ▲ ▼, eases ◀ ▶, and taps the action for a horn boost (needed to jump broken bridges, 6 s cooldown). The SPOTTER (`G.mst.spt`) stands in the roof hatch, aims a bat ◀ ▶ (7 aims), taps to swing at the junk the tornado throws (a red ring 0.9 s ahead), holds to brace, and ducks ▼ under low bridges. Cows bounce off the roof (never bat a cow). A loose dish has to be swung back on within 2 s. Each hit costs gap; at zero the van is spun out back to the stretch start. Stretches: Farm Road, Cornfield Shortcut, Trailer Park, Storm Cellar Dash. Gold help: gold bull-bar (next 3 road hits shrugged off). Medal: Gold <= 175 s, Silver <= 300 s.
- **33-2 Flash Flood Climb** (vertical scrolling team, mode `floodclimb`, act labels `rope` / `winch`): the water never stops rising. Every climb has an ORE BUCKET on a timber boom (Gavyn, 2026-11-15; it replaced a crouch springboard): the rope hero stands in it and the WINCH hero (`st.boost`, the old name in the code) holds the action at the crank 100 px along the ledge to wind it up to the next ledge; the winch hero also taps the action to shove wedged boulders. Results count bucket rides. The ROPE hero holds the action at an anchor ring to drop a rope; the partner jumps to grab it, climbs with ▲ (or jump) and slides with ▼; jump plus ◀ ▶ or ▼ lets go (a jump alone keeps climbing, so a mashing player doesn't drop off). Logs roll over 2.5 s after you step on; crumbling shelves fall 1.8 s after touched; waterfall spouts gush on a rhythm; a flash surge from stretch 2. A swept hero is roped up after 1.5 s; both in the water restarts the stretch. Stretches: The Dry Wash, Root Wall, The Narrows, Mine Scaffold (two climbs use old mine lift cages instead of buckets). Rain gauges: 20. The flood is never more than 250 px below the lower hero. Gold help: gold life ring, the water rises 30% slower. Medal: Gold <= 190 s, 16+ gauges, <= 2 sweeps; Silver <= 330 s.
- **33-3 Blizzard Whiteout** (scrolling team, mode `whiteout`, act labels `flare` / `pick`): a whiteout where you only see inside the flare's glow (a fog canvas with holes for the flare, lit cairns and the beacon; lightning thins it for a moment). Roped together (170 px). The FLARE hero (`G.mst.flare`) holds the action to raise the flare (light 150 -> 240 px, can't jump while raised) and fetches a new one when it burns low. The ICE hero (`G.mst.ice`) taps to swing the pick (ice walls 3 hits, icy slopes 2 chips, drifts 2) and holds next to a buried crate to dig it out. Crevasses: jump them one at a time; a hero who falls dangles on the rope and the partner has 1 s to brace (hold the action), or both slip back to the last cairn. Gusts (crouch ▼), icicles that shake then drop, a frozen lake whose tiles crack and break 2.8 s after first touched, yetis that block the road until the flare is raised near them and throw snowballs at unlit heroes. The cold meter refills at cairns (also checkpoints). Snow probes: 16, some on rocks. Stretches: Trailhead, Frozen Lake, The Pass, Summit Station (switch on the hut's beacon). Gold help: a gold lantern after 3 bad events. Medal: Gold <= 200 s with 12+ probes, Silver <= 350 s. `plan(st)` only uses what has been lit or shown by lightning (it remembers), so the computer plays fair.
- **33-4 Twister Tag** (versus, best of 5, 45 s rounds, mode `twistertag`, ownStep, `act: {label: 'dash'}`): top-down farm field, each player is a twister. Move with the stick or keys (eight ways, it drifts). Things inside your pull ring get sucked up if you are big enough: hay, scarecrows, mailboxes and fences +1, cows +2, tractors and the shed +3 and +4 (need size 4). The action is a gust dash (3 s cooldown) that spits your biggest thing as a missile: a hit costs the other twister 2 sizes. The pond kills your spin and soaks things away; power lines zap (stunned 0.8 s, 2 sizes lost). Be 3 sizes bigger and touch the other twister to swallow it (not in the first 6 s). From 25 s a wall cloud closes in and nothing new spawns. Time up: bigger twister wins, then more things picked up. tiebreak: total things over the match. Supply is limited on purpose (7 on the field at most) so rounds don't end with both at the size cap. Brain: reaction Easy 0.45 / Normal 0.25 / Hard 0.12 s; flees a twister 3 bigger (escape dash only at the last moment, since a dash costs its biggest thing), hunts when 3 bigger (Hard leads and cuts off from the middle), otherwise grows by value over distance (Hard prizes the shed and avoids races it would lose; Easy takes the nearest thing, even one too big, and wanders off every 4-8 s; Easy never dashes); shoots only when it's worth the size. Bot results: rounds about 38 s, about 1 swallow in 4 rounds; Normal vs Normal even (7-9, 8-8); Normal beats Easy 13-3 and 16-0; Hard beats Normal about 2 to 1.
- Code: `js/levels/world33/` (the shared kit `storm-kit.js`, `STORMCHASE`, reuses World 31's `SHRUNK` helpers and adds the storm look: `K.sky`, `K.rain`, `K.snow`, `K.windStreaks`, `K.funnel` tornadoes, `K.bolts` / `K.flashA` lightning, `K.debris`, `K.farm`, `K.van`, `K.chaser` (Pip and Bo in rain gear); its header is the manual), brains in `js/cpu/levels/world33/`, previews in `js/previews/world33.js` (helpers `pw33*`), tools in `tools/w33/` (`stuck.js` person-like partners, `trace.js`, `shots.js`, and `check.js`, which prints the end stats and the most held-key flips in any one second, with the time). Each def sits at the bottom of its level file as `STORMCHASE.defs.<mode>`; `world.js` adds the world (`WORLDS[32]`) and pushes the four in order. Each team level has a `plan(st)` that the prompts, the HUD chips and the brain all read.
- Testing (Nov 2026): computer pairs clear 33-1 in about 161 s, 33-2 in 147 s (20/20 gauges) and 33-3 in about 138 s (16/16 probes, no slips), all with Gold; key flips at most about 10 a second. Noisy partners clear all three; idle partners never do. `node tools/w33/stuck.js` (late, eager and slow person-like partners on both characters) clears every run; the remaining long waits are real waits (the partner hasn't come yet). Fixed along the way: 33-2 a re-pressed jump dropping a lagging climber off the rope; 33-3 a 108-flips-a-second twitch (targets that moved with the hero, and following a moving partner; now stable targets and pace-matching), the rope going taut while one went for a probe, falls into crevasses (a hero stopped mid-jump, the first jumper yanked back, the ice hero walked into a gap by the yeti job), a hero pushed out of a crevasse wall falling forever (now clamped to the gap walls), loops at gap edges for slow partners (a run-up rule and a safety jump), and digging blocked by swings. 33-4: both twisters reached the size cap every round (less supply), Easy as strong as Normal (Normal wasted size on dashes; Easy now wanders), and some swallows at 4-7 s (6 s grace).
- Review (Nov 2026, a second agent, before merging). Method: the spec, a read of the level and brain code, screenshots of all four levels and the previews, `tools/w33/check.js` and `stuck.js`, cpu-test pair / noisy / idle, versus skills against each other, physics-check, and a few one-off scripts (round-by-round sizes and zap logs in 33-4, sweep counts with a very slow partner in 33-2, a burnt-out flare in 33-3). 33-1 was left alone: it played well in every check. Fixed:
  - 33-2: the flood was out of sight for anyone who climbed well (755 px below a computer pair at the top), so the level had no chase. It now keeps up: never more than 250 px below the lower hero (`S.chase`), always on screen, and from there it only closes in at its steady speed. After a rescue it holds still for 4 s (`S.calmT`; a very slow partner was swept again straight away, up to 22 sweeps a run). The gold life ring counted sweeps from zero after every stretch restart and was skipped on a double sweep, so a pair that kept being washed back never got it; it now counts over the whole stretch. A rescued hero could land two ledges above a partner in mid-jump (now the partner's ledge). The computer boost hero walked off its ledge into the flood, again and again, to reach a rope hero stuck in the lift cage below (it now waits when that ledge is about to flood). The last stretch ends in the open: sky above the rim instead of more canyon wall.
  - 33-3: a flare that burnt out with no crate left stayed out for the whole stretch, and a yeti can only be scared off by a raised flare, so the pair took 3 snowballs until the gold lantern came. A burnt-out flare is now relit (half full) when both warm up at a cairn.
  - 33-4: Normal and Hard zapped themselves on the power lines about 16 times a match (things that blew in behind a line, escape dashes into a line, fleeing into a corner) and often ended a round at size 1-3. They now leave a thing alone while a line is in the way, look a third of a second ahead, and never dash into a line: about 1 zap a match. Balance after the change (8 matches each): Normal vs Normal 4-4, Hard beats Normal 12 of 16, Normal beats Easy 15 of 16.
  - Still worth a look when played by hand: 33-2 is the same boost-then-rope climb about 36 times (the spec's split routes, wall-kick chimney and log stepping stones were not built), so it may feel samey; 33-4's last 20 s squeeze both twisters round the pond, so rounds often end with small twisters.
### World 34: Viking Voyage (mixed)
Pip and Bo are Vikings on a voyage north: a longship with a dragon prow and a striped sail, grey seas, fjords, an ice fortress and a roaring mead hall at the end. Not a toddler world (Gavyn): the serpent is huge and has teeth, the fortress rains arrows, the hall is a riot; nobody gets hurt on screen (a failure is a knock-back, a mess or a restart of the stretch). Gavyn picked the theme and these four levels on 2026-11-11, from ten ideas written by a planning thread (spec: project file `pip-and-bo/notes/world34-viking-voyage-spec.md`); he skipped Longship Storm Row, Burning Village Dash, Frost Giant's Footsteps, Rainbow Bridge Run, Shield Wall and Axe Throw Duel. World index 33. Pip wears a horned helmet and an orange braided beard, Bo a winged helmet and a blue cloak.

- **34-1 Sea Serpent Chase** (scrolling team, mode `serpentchase`, ownStep + ownHUD + noFalls, roomWord 'Stretch'): a sea serpent hunts the longship. `G.mst.helm` steers (▲ ▼ three wave rows, ◀ ▶ inside a window, action = "Row!" a 1.2 s burst on a 6 s cooldown), the other is the HARPOONER (◀ ▶ swing the stern ballista through 7 aims, hold the action to crank 0.5 s, let go to fire; a dotted line shows the flight). Threats with tells: lunge (jaws open behind the stern: a bolt in the mouth makes it sink, else it snaps along its row), coils across one or two rows, arches (Row! under them), the tail (a shadow marks the row; shoot the tip), spit globs (shoot them out of the air), dives (bubbles; the throat glows for a double shot), rocks. 20 glowing scales to shoot between attacks. A gap meter instead of lives; at 0 the serpent swallows the ship and the stretch restarts. Stretches: Open Water, The Skerries, Fog Bank, The Maelstrom (a whirlpool drags the ship a row nearer every 3-4 s, only when that row is safe; ends with a full-crank shot down the throat). Gold help (3 hits in a stretch): a gold harpoon. Medal: gold 195 s, silver 310 s (a clean run is about 170 s, so Gold allows a slip and a few tries at the last shot). Deviation from the spec: the spec had the helm hold ▲ against the whirlpool; holding a row key made the computer helm flicker, so the whirlpool now pulls on its own, never into danger. A dive or tail slap also waits while a coil blocks two rows nearby, or while the rows next to the ship's are about to be filled by a coil or a rock, so there is always a row to steer to.
- **34-2 Ice Fortress Raid** (scrolling up, team, mode `icefort`, ownStep + ownHUD + noFalls, roomWord 'Stretch'): climb an ice cliff under a fortress. The wall is a grid (15 columns, 18 rows a stretch, a timber ledge every 6 rows). `G.mst.axe` taps the action to chop the cell in the held direction (ice 2 chops, blue ice 4, snow 1 but it crumbles 3 s after use, rock can't be chopped, rune stones 2, rope knots 2); the SHIELD hero holds the action to raise a big round shield (covers the partner on the same hold, beside or one row below; slow climbing while up; ◀ ▶ tilt it for rolling snowballs and barrels). After each chop there is a 0.1 s beat before the axe hero can climb on (so the last chop of a hold doesn't pull a hero who is holding a direction out from under the shield). Hazards with tells: arrow volleys (horn, shadows), icicles (shake, then fall; chopping knocks them loose), rolling snowballs and barrels, boiling-water spouts (the shield does not stop them; they melt the holds in that column for 4 s), wind gusts (hold ▼ to cling). Frost creeps up from the ledge you left and makes holds below it unusable. Jobs swap at every ledge. 20 rune stones. Stretches: The Ice Foot, Frozen Falls, The Scaffolds (a frozen rope), Under the Walls (gusts, and the gate rope at the top). Gold help (3 tumbles in a stretch): a gold shield twice as wide with no slow-down. Medal: gold 200 s, silver 340 s.
- **34-3 Mead Hall Feast Brawl** (single screen, team, mode `meadhall`, ownStep + ownHUD + noFalls, roomWord 'Feast'): feed the starving crew. Food comes out on a rack by the kitchen hatch (left, on the floor). Three long tables (A low, B middle, C top) with 4 seats each, and a serving step at both ends of every table (one-way platforms: jump straight up to go up, walk off the outer side or ▼ + jump to go down). One action does the obvious thing: pick up at the rack or from the floor, TOSS to the partner anywhere else (a homing arc, caught if their hands are free and within 480 px), and on a serving step HOLD to charge and LET GO to slide it down the table (a marker shows where it stops; the nearest Viking to that spot grabs it; too hard and it flies off the end, a Mess). Orders have a patience ring (12-18 s); right food = +1 Fed, wrong = he grumbles and gets half his patience back, patience out = a tantrum. A shared Rowdy meter (tantrums +2, messes +1.5, each Fed -0.8); full = a 4 s BRAWL and the feast goes back to two thirds of its Fed. SKOL (feast 2 on): a table raises its mugs for 7 s after a 4 s warning; a mead barrel rolled down it fills every mug (+1 Fed each), any other time it's a Mess. Feast 3 on: the food fight (a Viking winds up with a red "!", hold ▼ to duck or the carried food is knocked out of your hands), the hall dog (eats food left on the floor), arm-wrestles (the two middle Vikings of a table block it and need the same food, one from each END within 2.5 s: +2). Feast 4: two chieftain's platters (both grab a handle; it only moves when both carry it and only jumps when both press ▲ within 0.4 s; get it to a top step within 12 s: +3). Feasts: First Course (2 tables, 12 Fed), Second Helpings (16), The Rowdy Bench (20), The Chieftain's Feast (24). The kitchen restocks the most urgent missing food and swaps an unwanted item off a full rack; a hero who puts food back at a full rack swaps it with the nearest food on it. While an arm-wrestle or a platter is on, the other orders' patience runs at 30% (`watchSlow`), and when one of those or a SKOL ends every waiting Viking has at least 7 s of patience again (`settlePat`), so the two-hero jobs don't end in tantrums nobody could prevent. Gold help (3 tantrums in a feast): patience drains 40% slower. Medal: gold 190 s, silver 330 s. Deviation from the spec: tables have a step at both ends (the spec's arm-wrestle needs food "one from each end"), and tables are not walkable (the heroes walk the aisle in front).
- **34-4 Longship Race** (scrolling versus, best of 5, 60 s cap, mode `longshiprace`, ownStep, `act: {label: 'row'}`): two longships on one shared screen with 4 depth lanes. A drum beats every 0.5 s; only the first stroke in each beat interval counts (+26 px/s within 0.12 s of the beat, +12 off it), three strokes in under 0.3 s catch a crab (0.5 s no rowing), speed fades (k 0.3). ▲ ▼ change lane (a jump also steers up), ◀ back-waters. White wave crests roll in from behind: a stroke while one lifts your stern (a 0.4 s window) is a Surge (+90, Storm Run +110). Ramming: steering into the other ship's lane alongside shoves it a lane (or pins it at the edge); hitting its stern from behind spins its steering 0.6 s. SHIELD WALL: hold the action (after the stroke) for up to 1.5 s, 4 s cooldown: un-shovable, the rammer bounces. Hazards with a marker at the right edge 1.2 s ahead: rocks (stop 1 s, a shield flies off), ice floes (half speed), whirlpools (pull a lane, spin if you sit in one), a whale (a ramp if you're fast, a bump if not), fog (no markers), gates where only the middle two lanes are open. A ship a full screen behind is caught up with a 1 s penalty. Courses: Fjord Sprint, Skerry Slalom, Iceberg Alley, Whirlpool Sound, Storm Run. Time up: further by more than 20 px wins; tiebreak: more Surges. Gold help (a round behind): a gold sail, +8% top speed and a 0.18 s beat window. Deviation from the spec: the shield wall is a held action, not "hold down", because down steers. Brain: on-beat Easy 55% / Normal 78% / Hard 93%, crests 20/50/85%, lookahead 160/240/320 px and misses 30/10/3% of hazards, Easy bursts into crabs; Normal rams alongside in a gate, Hard rams whenever alongside; Normal braces half the time, Hard on time. Bot results: rounds 41-46 s; Normal vs Normal even (4-2 and 2-1 over 9 matches); Hard beats Normal and Normal beats Easy every match from both sides.
- Code: `js/levels/world34/` (the shared kit `viking-kit.js`, `VIKING`, reuses World 31's `SHRUNK` helpers and adds `K.viking` (Pip and Bo as Vikings), `K.helmet`, `K.shield`, `K.longship`, `K.sea`, `K.waveLine`, `K.splash`, the northern `K.sky` (night aurora, dusk, storm, sleet), `K.fjords`, `K.fog`, and `viking*` sounds; its header is the manual), brains in `js/cpu/levels/world34/`, previews in `js/previews/world34.js` (helpers `pw34*`), tools in `tools/w34/` (`check.js` held-key flips and end stats, `stuck.js` person-like partners, `trace.js`, `shots.js`). Each def sits at the bottom of its level file as `VIKING.defs.<mode>`; `world.js` adds the world (`WORLDS[33]`) and pushes the four in order. World 34's script tags sit right after `js/cpu/cpu-nav.js` in `index.html` (not after World 33's), so the two branches don't touch the same lines; `liveWorlds` skips the empty slot until World 33 is merged. Each team level has a `plan(st)` that the prompts, the HUD chips and the brain all read.
- Testing (computer pair, `node tools/w34/check.js <mode> --seeds=...`): 34-1 clears in about 170 s with Gold and 20/20 scales; 34-2 in 110-125 s with Gold and 19-20/20 runes; 34-3 in 154-209 s (Gold in about half the runs: brawls and the second platter decide it; after the review, below, 149-178 s and Gold every run). Held-key flips peak at 8-12 a second (12 only in brief steering moments). `--who=noisy` clears all three, `--who=idle` clears none (the level takes a break after 12 s with no person). `node tools/w34/stuck.js serpentchase,icefort,meadhall` clears every run; the few 8 s "stuck" spots left are the computer correctly waiting (for a brawl to end, or at the far end of an arm-wrestle table for its partner). In Mead Hall a computer server whose person runner brings nothing for 6 s fetches food itself.
- Review of World 34 (Nov 2026, a second agent, before the merge). Method: screenshots of all four levels and the previews, `tools/w34/check.js` and `stuck.js`, cpu-test pair / noisy / idle and the Easy / Normal / Hard ladder, a read of the level code, and for every bad event a computer pair took, a trace of the second before it (a perfect pair should take none, so each one is either a brain mistake or an unfair moment in the level). Fixed: 34-2 every Tumble of a computer pair (3-4 a run) was the last chop of a hold and the climb into it happening in the same instant, out from under the shield or into a spout's column (now a 0.1 s beat after a chop; 0 Tumbles in 5 of 6 runs); 34-1 tail slaps in The Skerries that the helm could not steer out of (a coil or rock alongside), and Gold 180 s was only 10 s more than a perfect run (now 195 s); 34-3 two computer deadlocks (both heroes holding food nobody wanted in front of a full rack for 15-30 s: the rack now swaps; a server waiting for a runner who stood at the far end of an arm-wrestle table with the first food: it now fetches the second itself), and the tantrums a perfect pair came out of every arm-wrestle, platter and SKOL with (above); 34-4 the computer rower broke lane ties upward, which sent Pip's ship to the edge and Bo's to the middle (now toward the middle), and the ram check now alternates which ship goes first. After the fixes a computer pair: 34-1 170 s, no hits in most runs, 20/20 scales; 34-2 114-121 s, 20/20 runes; 34-3 149-178 s, Gold in 8 of 8 runs (was about half), 1-6 tantrums (was 8-13), Gold help in about half the runs (was every run). Known and left alone: `stuck.js --human=lag` does not finish 34-3 (it did not on the original build either): that stand-in lets go of every charge 0.35 s late, which is two seats too far, and can't stop on a rack slot; it says nothing about the computer partner, and eager, slow and noisy partners all clear. With two Hard computers a single course can lean to one side (Storm Run to Pip, Skerry Slalom to Bo) because Hard rams the other ship toward the outer lanes, where that course's hazards differ; over a match it evens out (37-33 rounds) and it does not apply to two people.
- Status: built, bot-tested and reviewed (Nov 2026); not yet played by hand.

### World 35: Wizard Academy (team, one long level)
Gavyn's idea (Nov 2026): "one long level of learning to become a wizard". He approved the spec (project files `pip-and-bo/notes/world35-wizard-academy-spec.md`) and said a longer level is fine. Built like World 30's Time Chase: one level, a core file plus one file per room, checkpoints. Everything is original (no names, creatures, spell words, houses or looks from any existing wizard story; spells have plain names). World index 34 (it loads after World 34).

- **35-1 Moonwick Academy** (team, mode `wizschool`, `ownHUD`, `noFalls`, `roomWord: 'Class'`): Pip and Bo arrive at a crooked wizard school on a floating island as first-years, take ten classes (one spell each), sit the final exam and graduate with a friendly duel. Headmistress Thimblewick (tiny, very old, in a floating patchwork armchair with a self-refilling teacup) plays the part the Time Thief plays in 30-1: she announces each class, jokes, hands out the stars. Every class won adds a star in that spell's colour to both hats. Every fail is a soft "Oops!" with a pop-back; gold help is announced after 3 Oops in one part of a class.
  - The 12 classes (each about 2 minutes for a good pair, every one with two jobs that swap): 1 Broom Flying (steer / kick one broom, rings, hedges, gusts), 2 Levitation (one floats a stone slab up the library tower, the other rides it and grabs escaping books), 3 Runaway Cauldron (stir on the beat while the other runs the recipe down the cellar slopes), 4 Light Spell (one carries the wand light in pitch-black cellars, the other climbs to levers where the light falls, ink shadows creep in), 5 Shape Shifting (turn the partner into frog, fish or bat for each obstacle), 6 Shrink and Grow (zap the partner tiny or giant in the storeroom), 7 Frost and Flame (ice bridges, rafts, thorns, fire tiles, a geyser pillar to climb), 8 Shield Charm (one holds a bubble, the other fights from inside it), 9 Portal Class (draw an IN door and an OUT door up a clock tower; the jumper goes through), 10 Beast Taming (ride a stampeding thunderpuff: a rider jumps / ducks / charges, a feeder keeps it fed and calm), 11 Final Exam: The Big Mistake (a spell goes wrong and wakes the Blot, a huge ink monster: 8 spell gates with the Blot rolling behind, then the Great Hall fight: shield its bolts, jump its waves, hold its eye open and zap it, three rounds, then it shrinks into a jar), 12 Graduation Duel (versus inside the team level, best of 3: sparks across at your height, a held big bolt that arcs over the wall, a down-held shield that bounces a spark if raised just in time, spell tokens (frost, shrink, frog, door), round 2 a fountain, round 3 drifting stones and a sneezing thunderpuff; the loser of round 1 starts round 2 with a gold star). The duel is a results line ("Top of the Class"), not a pass or fail.
  - Checkpoints: the start of every class is saved in the browser (store key `wiz:cp`); classes keep mid-class safe spots too.
  - Trophy by play time and Oops (`WIZ.medal`), set from full-level bot runs (Gold about 1.25x the computer pair's time, Silver about 1.9x).
  - Computer partner: one brain per class in `js/cpu/levels/world35/`; every brain reads the class's own `plan(st)` (the same plan the hint bubbles use). In the duel the computer is the rival at the Opponent setting: Easy reacts in 0.45 s, shields 30% of sparks and never uses big bolts; Normal 0.28 s / 65% / sometimes; Hard 0.14 s / 90% / big bolts to break shields. Bot runs: Hard beats Normal and Easy every time, Normal vs Normal is about even, and a slow kid stand-in beats Easy.
- Code: core `js/levels/world35/wiz-school.js` (its header is the manual: phase contract, helpers, test hook), one file per class `wiz-<class>.js`, brains in `js/cpu/levels/world35/`, preview `js/previews/world35.js` (helpers `pw35*`, four quick scenes: broom, frog, the Blot, graduation), tools in `tools/w35/`: `play.js <class|all> --who=pair|noisy|kid|idle [--skills=easy,hard]`, `shot.js <class> <secs> out.png`, `stuck.js <class|all> --human=lag,eager,slow` (the computer with a person-like partner).
- Testing: every class ends for a computer pair, a noisy pair and a "kid" (0.5 s late, pauses, wanders) on either side, and never ends for an idle person. With `stuck.js` the eager and slow partners clear every class. The `lag` stand-in (the computer's own plan played 0.35 s late) still fails a few classes where its own jumps or steering go wrong (broom steering, the Frost and Flame geyser pillar); those are the stand-in's limits rather than the computer partner stopping, but worth a hand-play check. Fixed along the way: a broom kicker piling up kicks behind a slow press, a jump held down forever after a first press was eaten (Light Spell), Frost waiting on the near bank a few pixels short of starting the bridge, Frost not coming back when the ice behind it melted, Flame overshooting the lamp.
- Review (Fable, Nov 2026). Fixed: Broom Flying could loop on one obstacle for ever (a bonk put the broom back at the last ring's own height, sometimes straight into the next hedge): it now comes back at a height that clears what is next, and after 4 bonks from the same lantern ring Thimblewick gives an announced "gold tow" to the next ring (`WIZBROOM.towAfter`). Light Spell: the computer LIGHT shook left-right (dozens of turns a second) when the finder stood at the rim of the light; turning round now takes 0.45 s. Frost and Flame: a frozen geyser pillar lasts 9 s (`WIZFF.pillar`, was 6) so both can climb on one freeze. Checked and fine: checkpoints (resume after a reload at every class, restart this class, start over, cleared on finish, `tth:cp` untouched), the duel (even at equal skill, Hard > Normal > Easy over 20 matches each way), originality. The `stuck.js` "lag" stand-in fails many classes because it plays the computer's exact plan 0.35 s late and so sways around every spot it has to stand on; a person-like steerer that knows its own slowness clears the broom with 0.25-0.6 s reactions. It is not a difficulty measure.
- Final Exam rebuild (Nov 2026, after the Fable review). The exam now follows the spec's Class 11. Part 1 is 8 flood gates, each a small version of its class run with that class's own spell functions (`wizExWith` runs them on a small state shaped like the class's own): Frost and Flame (ice bridge over an ink pool, burn the thorn door), Levitation (float a slab beside a wall, the rider pulls the lever), Light Spell (glow in a pitch-dark stretch, the climber can only jump in the light), Shrink and Grow (shrink the partner through a keyhole), Shape Shifting (frog over the hedge, then the jobs swap and a fish swims the moat), Shield Charm (hold the bubble under ink rain, zap the door's 3 seals from inside), Portal Class (draw IN and OUT doors up a broken stair, drop the ladder), Broom Flying (steer and kick one broom under ink curtains and through the Great Hall window). Part 2 is the Great Hall with three different rounds: round 1 frost the Blot's ink falls into steps and FLAME the eye, round 2 stamp chalk-plate doors and cast DOOR at the eye, round 3 ride the thunderpuff, feed it a pepper at the gate of arms and toss the Brew into the eye. Attack sets before each eye: ink waves (frost or jump), bolts (shield), grabbing arms (flame or step out), falling candelabra, and LIGHTS OUT in rounds 2 and 3 (the lifter glows, ink shadows creep). The Blot is bigger, with Thimblewick and her armchair visible inside it.
  - Help: gold tea after 2 misses at a gate (slows the Blot, a kinder spell for that gate). After 3 misses the gate is "stuck": the Blot waits behind it until it opens and the Frost and Flame ice stops melting. After 35 s at one eye the round-1 steps stop melting and round-2 doors last longer. A safety net puts anyone who drops below the floor back at the gate's start.
  - Brain: `js/cpu/levels/world35/wiz-exam.js` is a plain plan follower (walk to x, face, hold or tap the action, up / down, jump, steer, kick); the plan lives in each gate's and round's `plan()`. Near a spot it walks in short steps so a slow reaction doesn't overshoot.
  - Review of the rebuild (Fable, Nov 2026): matches the spec and passes every bot check. Fixed two spots where the computer shook left-right many times a second: the Light Spell gate (its spot hopped sides when the climber stood at the rim of the light; turning round now takes 0.3 s in the exam brain) and "lights out" in the Great Hall (the glow holder and its partner chased each other; the light now stands still once the partner is within 80 px). The hardest spot for the slow-kid stand-in is gate 1's ice bridge (4-6 dips in the ink before the stuck-gate help); the help covers it.
  - Test tools: `play.js`, `stuck.js` and `shot.js` take `--at=g3` (start at gate 3, 0-7) or `--at=r1` (Great Hall round 1, 0-2), using `MODES.wizschool.test.examAt`.
  - Bot results for the exam: computer pair 172-181 s with no Oops; noisy 220-330 s; kid (0.5 s late, pauses, wanders) 255-483 s on either side; `stuck.js` lag 283-305 s, eager 188-254 s, slow 320-446 s; idle never ends at any gate or round. The whole level for a computer pair is about 1150-1190 s, so the trophy times stay (Gold 1400 s, Silver 2100 s).
- Status: built, bot-tested and reviewed (Nov 2026); Gavyn plays it next.

### World 36: Movie Studio (mixed)
Pip and Bo are stunt performers at Gigantic Pictures, a big, chaotic film studio where some things are fake (paper flats, foam, sugar glass, cardboard) and some are very real (steel, brick, a runaway tram). The running idea of the world is FAKE or REAL: every prop has a yellow PROP tag, a paper edge or a wobble; every real thing is glossy with rivets and a red-and-white REAL stripe (readable in greyscale). Gavyn picked the theme and these four levels on 2026-11-12 from ten ideas (spec: project file `pip-and-bo/notes/world36-movie-studio-spec.md`); he skipped Monster Movie Stomp, Collapsing Set Escape, Dolly Track Dash, Special Effects Gone Wrong, Lights Camera Action and Red Carpet Race. World index 35. Not a toddler world: the falls are big and the tram is fast, but nobody is hurt on screen (a failure is "CUT! From the top!" and a rewind).

- **Outfits** (Gavyn, 2026-11-12: "the 2 characters ... outfits unique to each level"): every World 36 outfit is drawn by `STUDIO.stunt(id, x, y, {look})` in `studio-kit.js`, so the levels declare `outfit: 'none'` for the shared outfit system (PR #39) and draw their own. Looks: 36-1 and 36-4 `stunt` / `show` (jumpsuit with a white stripe and knee pads, a helmet with goggles; the show suit adds a cape and a clear visor; both helmets are open-face so the eyes show), 36-2 `crew` (striped crew vest, peaked cap and headset), 36-3 `agent` (black suit, tie, sunglasses, harness straps). Pip is red / orange / purple cape, Bo blue / teal cape (no blue on Pip, no orange or red on Bo). Pip's sprout and Bo's ears always poke out through the helmet or cap (`topper()` in the kit; the previews do the same). If the shared outfit system later takes these over, `K.stunt` is the only place to change.
- **36-1 Car Chase, Take One** (scrolling team, mode `takeone`, ownStep + ownHUD + noFalls, roomWord 'Take'): one drives the stunt car (▲ ▼ lanes, ◀ ▶ ease, action = Stunt! hop, a long jump on a ramp), the other turns the scene dial (◀ ▶) and claps (action) so the painted flats ahead match the scene and are paper (burst through) instead of plywood (crash). Scenes CITY, DESERT, SNOW (slow drifting lane changes, snow covers cones), NIGHT (headlights only), and PREMIERE for the very last flat. Each scene's hazards are props that vanish when the scene changes; REAL hazards stay. Take meter instead of lives; empty = a Retake. Takes: Downtown, Location Shoot, Night Shoot, The Big Finish. 20 film reels. Gold help (3 crashes in a take): a gold clapper (short cooldown, flats show earlier). Medal: Gold 185 s, Silver 310 s.
- **36-2 Backlot Tram Runaway** (scrolling team, mode `tramrun`, roomWord 'Take'): the tour tram has no brakes. The brake hero stays aboard (hold the action on the front car = hand brake, which heats and cooks; ▲ ▼ throw the next switch; tap to catch a runner who comes up short). The runner jumps off, runs ahead and clears props (push, crank, unplug, hold a lever while the tram passes), calls the upper line ("Go up!" / "Stay low!") and jumps back on. A runner the tram catches is scooped onto the cowcatcher (an unfinished job is undone). Sets: Dusty Gulch, Planet Zog (floaty jumps), Jungle Temple (REAL mud, a trapdoor lever), Monster Island (the tail sweeps the line on a timer; both hold the big emergency lever at the end). 20 tickets. Gold help: gold brake shoes. Medal: Gold 215 s, Silver 330 s.
- **36-3 Stunt Double Fall** (scrolls DOWN, team, mode `stuntfall`, roomWord 'Take'): both fall off a fake skyscraper. Hold hands (action) to fall slow and wide and smash sugar glass; let go to fit through the gaps in REAL steel beams. Foam and real window boxes, bouncy awnings, flagpoles, crew fans (sideways or up), trapdoor chutes (take 3 on), floor markers as checkpoints, a camera drone frame with 20 stars, and a bullseye airbag in take 4 (landing scored out of 100). No roles. Gold help: gold wrist ribbons (longer link reach). Medal: Gold 185 s, Silver 290 s.
- **36-4 Stunt Show Showdown** (versus, best of 5, 45 s rounds, mode `stuntshow`): both perform the same stunt at once in their own half; three judges score 1-10 each (the number, Style, the finish). Stunts in order: Ramp Jump (rev band, flips, land level), High Dive (climb against a clock, somersaults, straighten), Wall Smash (pick the FAKE panel of three: jump, run or slide; charge), Fire Hoop Leap (trampoline timing, light five hoops, pikes), Grand Finale (all of it). Level on points: the better finish wins; still level: the same stunt once more, then it counts as a tie and the match moves on (`maxTies` 8). Gold help (a round behind): a gold helmet, every window 30% wider. Brain: rev error Easy 25% / Normal 10% / Hard 4% of the dial, flips 1 / 2 / 3, lands level 70 / 85 / 95%; dive height half / three-quarters / full and 1 / 2-3 / 4 turns; reads the fake panel 65 / 85 / 97%; bounce window 55 / 78 / 93%, Hard pikes.
- **Deviations from the spec:** 36-4 halves are not mirrored (both run left to right, so ◀ ▶ mean the same to both players); the tie rule above (the spec had no limit). 36-2: only the front car can be hit by the monster's tail, and its sweeps are further apart (with a 0.8 s sweep it was nearly impossible to brake for); 20 tickets (the spec placements left some out of reach). Gold times were raised to about 1.1x a clean computer pair (36-2 215 / 330 s, 36-3 185 / 290 s).
- Code: `js/levels/world36/` (`studio-kit.js`, global `STUDIO`, wraps World 31's `SHRUNK` kit and adds the outfits, `K.propTag`, `K.realTag`, `K.steel`, `K.wobble`, the clapper and studio scenery; its header is the manual; `world.js` adds `WORLDS[35]` and pushes the four levels), brains in `js/cpu/levels/world36/`, previews in `js/previews/world36.js` (helpers `pw36*`). World 36's script tags are their own block in `index.html` after World 35's, so a World 37 branch built alongside only touches the lines next to it. Each team level has a `plan(st)` that the prompts, HUD chips and brain all read (36-4: `plan(st, id)`); `mirror()` keeps `st.runPos` for the tools.
- Tools in `tools/w36/`: `check.js [modes] --seeds=` (computer pair: result, end stats, held-key flips a second), `stuck.js` (person-like partners: lag, eager, slow), `shots.js <code> t1,t2` (screenshots to `build/w36/`), `stunts.js` (36-4: each stunt alone many times at each skill, score spreads and judges).
- Bot results (Nov 2026): computer pair 36-1 176-187 s (0 crashes, 20/20 reels, Gold), 36-2 200 s (0 crashes, 20/20 tickets), 36-3 170 s (0 bonks, 20/20 stars, landing 99). Noisy pairs (either one clumsy) clear: 36-1 176-187 s, 36-2 209-213 s, 36-3 169-177 s. `stuck.js` lag, eager and slow partners clear all three (36-3 153-183 s). Idle never clears any. The `--who=solo` follow-and-copy bot doesn't clear 36-2 (the runner's jobs need a plan), clears 36-1 once in two and 36-3 in about 4 minutes. 36-4: Normal vs Normal is close (about 9 rounds to 8), Hard beats Normal and Normal beats Easy every match from both sides; per-stunt score spreads of neighbouring skills overlap a little. `check.js` reports 16 act flips a second for the 36-2 crank: that is the crank being tapped on purpose (0.12 s apart), not a twitch.
- **Fable review (Nov 2026, on top of the outfit system PR #40; World 36 merges cleanly after it and its levels keep `outfit: 'none'`).** New tools: `tools/w36/people.js` (the computer next to people who do the wrong thing: never clap, never leave the tram, call the wrong line, cook the brake, never steer, tap hands all the time or a moment after the computer; prints stats, stuck spots and the computer's key flips) and `tools/w36/outfits.js` (a contact sheet of the four looks). Fixed:
  - Outfits: the stunt and show helmets covered the whole face (no eyes, and Pip was all red and silver). They are open-face now (a dome on top of the head, goggles pushed up on it for 36-1, a clear visor for 36-4), Pip's lenses are amber (they were blue), and the previews match.
  - 36-3: two heroes who both tap for hands a moment apart used to link and at once let go again (and the same for letting go): the partner's tap within 0.5 s of a link or a let-go now counts as the same wish (`S.sameTap`). The computer no longer links while the pair, as wide as it would be, would hit a REAL box, awning or flagpole before the glass (a person pinned to the wall beside a REAL box was linked, bonked and linked again for ever, at no cost). The safety wire acts at 340 px (was 430, more than the gap between two rows, so a hero stuck on a beam was never brought down and the partner bounced on the next glass alone until the take was cut: an endless retake loop in Take 3). With a person, the computer takes the nearest gap when the plan's gap is too far to reach. Its steering has a start and a stop distance and lets go one stopping distance early (it flipped a key up to 48 times a second on the deadband's edge, and overshot gaps). A star is taken before steering round a danger row well below it (the star under the 620 gap in Take 4).
  - 36-2: the computer brake hero let go and grabbed the brake every other step when the heat sat at its cap (100 flips a second, with a runner who dawdles): a pull now ends at the cap and the next waits until the brake has cooled 12% below it (`st.brkRest`). A REAL thing the brake hero can see for itself now beats the runner's call (a person can call the wrong way; a call for the boulder line is still obeyed).
  - 36-1 and 36-2: reels and tickets picked up in a take that was cut were counted again on the retake ("58 of 20").
  - 36-4: flips are counted on landing as the ones all but finished (a level landing at 1078 degrees was scored as two flips), and the bike spins at 660 degrees a second (was 600: three flips needed a perfect rev and spinning for the whole flight, so nobody, the Hard computer included, ever got more than two; Hard now does its three as the spec says). A lit hoop can't be clipped (the top hoop could not be reached without brushing the lower ones, so Style was 1 for nearly everyone). A rider who never revs is waved off after 10 s instead of holding the round for the 45 s cap.
  - Results after the review: computer pair 36-1 173-179 s, 36-2 200 s, 36-3 170 s, all with no bad events, every collectable and at most 8 key flips a second (the 36-2 crank's 16 is the crank). `stuck.js` and noisy partners clear all three; every `people.js` case clears, or fails only where the person never does a job the level needs from them (never claps a full-width flat, never drives, never holds the last lever). 36-4: Normal v Normal 14-16 over 30 matches, Hard beats Normal and Normal beats Easy 10 of 10 from both sides.
  - Left for Gavyn: in 36-1 and 36-3 the screen moves at a fixed speed, so the time (and the medal) only changes with Retakes: a run with 10 crashes and no Retake is still Gold.

- Status: built, bot-tested and reviewed (Nov 2026); waiting for Gavyn to merge (after the outfit system, PR #40).

### World 37: Medieval Tournament (mixed)
The King's Grand Tournament: striped pavilions, pennants, a royal stand, a castle on the hill. Not a toddler world (Gavyn): bandits, a burning siege tower, lances that splinter; nobody gets hurt on screen (a failure is a tumble into hay, a dented helm with stars, or a restart of the stretch). Gavyn picked the theme and these four levels on 2026-11-12 from ideas written by a Fable planning thread (spec: project file `pip-and-bo/notes/world37-medieval-tournament-spec.md`); he skipped Dragon Kite Parade Gone Wrong, Castle Wall Dash, Dungeon Flood Escape, Squire Scramble, Trebuchet Team and Archery Showdown. World index 36. First world built under Gavyn's outfit rule (2026-11-12): each level has its own outfit for Pip and Bo, drawn by `js/levels/world37/tourney-outfits.js` (`TOURNEY_OUTFITS.draw`, a pure function, also used by the previews): 37-1 royal coach livery, 37-2 the overslept knight's borrowed armour (shared badly: Pip a pot-helm and breastplate, Bo the far-too-big great helm), 37-3 siege engineers (padded straps or a wet hide; soot and arrows in the hat build up), 37-4 full champions (plate with orange or blue enamel, crested helms whose ribbons and plume tear as Wobble rises, a laurel for the match winner). Pip wears no blue and Bo no orange or red; Pip's sprout and Bo's ears stay visible (Bo's helms have ear-shaped blue rondels). Each def says `outfit: 'none'` because the levels draw these costumes themselves (the shared outfit system of PR #39 must not add a second hat).

- **37-1 Runaway Royal Carriage** (scrolling team, mode `royalcarriage`, ownStep + ownHUD + noFalls, roomWord 'Stretch'): the King's carriage has bolted with the King inside. `G.mst.drv` drives (▶ "Hyah!" faster, ◀ haul the reins, ▲ ▼ lean against the tilt gauge, action = whip: a 1 s burst that scatters geese and sheep, 5 s cooldown; ▲ / ▼ pick the road at a fork); the ROOF HERO runs the roof, hops to the harness pole and the lead horses, ducks (▼) low signs and branches (a "Duck!" marker 1.1 s ahead), shoves bandits off (the big one needs 2 shoves in 1 s) and HOLDS the action on the pole to re-hitch a loose strap while the driver holds the tilt steady. Ramps and gaps need speed and the roof hero on the roof at take-off. 20 favours (ribbons): high ones from the roof, low ones from the pole or a horse. A shared Royal Nerves meter (Spills, Tumbles, stolen bags, the crown, long tilts); full = a Royal Fit, the stretch restarts. Stretches: Tournament Gate, Market Day, The King's Forest (dusk), The Unfinished Bridge (ends hauling the reins to stop at the royal stand; the King climbs out dizzy and waves). Gold help (3 bad events in a stretch): gold reins (tilt 40% slower, Duck markers 0.4 s earlier). Medal: gold 185 s, silver 320 s. Computer pair 170 s, 20/20 favours.
- **37-2 Tandem Joust Run** (scrolling team, mode `tandemjoust`, ownStep + ownHUD + noFalls, roomWord 'Stretch'): two squires, one horse, one very long lance. `G.mst.rider` rides (▶ gallop 340 px/s, ◀ canter 220, ▲ jump, hold for longer, ▼ duck under beams and ropes); the LANCER sets the lance height (▲ ▼ three heights, one step per 0.15 s; airborne the lance is a step higher) and HOLDS the action to couch (stamina: 1.6 s, rests to recover). Targets: 24 rings on posts, quintains (a gallop hit is Perfect, a miss at a gallop spins round and Bonks you), straw knights (strike where the shield is NOT; some switch with a 1.3 s tell), melons and skins, the straw dummy (3 passes; a miss rewinds you for another go). Ground: hurdles, hay, troughs, mud (slows to a canter), crowd crossings (canter), rough ground (lift the lance before landing). 3 Bonks with no clean hit in between = Unhorsed: restart from the half-way flag. Gold help (3 Bonks in a stretch): a gold lance (couch 2.6 s, heights glow 0.4 s earlier), and a jump up to 0.35 s late still clears. Medal: gold 175 s, silver 300 s. Computer pair 167 s, 24/24 rings, no Bonks. Deviation from the spec: a quintain missed at a canter is only a pat, not a Bonk (so the computer rider can protect a person lancer who doesn't couch by cantering).
- **37-3 Siege Tower Push** (scrolling team, mode `siegetower`, ownStep + ownHUD + noFalls, roomWord 'Stretch'): push a siege tower up to the baron's wall. `G.mst.push` is on the bar (▶ push, ◀ brake, action = heave over a rut, ▼ duck, ▲ let go to fetch a ground shield); the TOWER HERO climbs the ladders inside (▲ ▼), HOLDS the action on the top deck to raise the wet-hide roof against arrow volleys (a horn and shadows first), taps out fires (big ones need a bucket from the deck 2 winch), kicks a jammed wheel, drops planks over trenches and swings the ram at barrels (deck 1 front), sets the chock on slopes (deck 1 back, a checkpoint), and comes down to the bar for mud. Castle hazards: volleys, fire arrows, boulders (a shadow 1.6 s ahead: stop short), rolling barrels and hay wheels, porridge spouts (time the push). 20 shields to collect (high ones from the tower, ground ones by the pusher). At the wall the hero drops the bridge while the pusher holds the tower, then both climb and cross within 8 s. Gold help (3 Knocks, Burns, Smashes or Rollbacks in a stretch): gold wheels (no rollback on slopes, no heave cooldown). Medal: gold 200 s, silver 340 s. Computer pair 185-193 s, 20/20 shields. Deviation from the spec: push speed 70 px/s with coasting momentum (a person who lets go for a moment doesn't stop dead), and the hero on the top deck can hold the roof anywhere up there.
- **37-4 Grand Joust** (versus, best of 5, mode `grandjoust`, ownStep, `act: {label: 'strike'}`): the tournament final, one screen, a 3 s charge per pass. GALLOP: tap forward (Pip ▶, Bo ◀) on the hoofbeats (every 0.3 s; on the beat +0.42, off +0.1, a second tap in a beat nothing): Trot, Canter, Gallop, Thunder. AIM AND GUARD: ▲ ▼ move the lance between Low, Middle and High; BACK (Pip ◀, Bo ▶) swaps the shield to the other free height. Both riders' ladders are on screen, so each sees the other's lance and shield: a feint game. The LOCK ribbon 0.5 s before contact fixes them; a change in the last 0.15 s before it has a 50% chance to fumble (lance to Middle). STRIKE: a ring closes; press the action on it: Perfect (0.06 s), Good (0.14 s), else Glance. A lance hits unless the other shield is at its height. Wobble: Glance 1, Good 2, Perfect 3, +1 at Thunder, +1 for High, +1 with Champion's Lances; a Low hit rattles the other horse a tier down next pass; a High Perfect spins the helm off. Wobble 5 = unhorsed into the hay (helm on backwards, birds). Both at once: the one who took less stays on; level = both remount at 3. Wobble drains 1 for a rider not hit. After 6 passes fewer Wobble wins, level = a tie; a second tie goes to more hits this match, then sudden death (passes at Thunder until one is steadier). Twists by round: plain, Crosswind (a gust drops both lances a height), Mud Lists, Sun in the Eyes (one rider can't see the other's shield in the middle second, alternating), Champion's Lances (slow to aim, +1). Gold help (a round behind): a 30% wider strike ring and the first hit each round 1 lighter. Deviations from the spec: the guard is swapped with the BACK key, not jump (jump is ▲, aim up, on the keyboard); both shields start High and lances Middle (an uneven start leaned every match to Bo). Brain (`plan(st, id)` shows only what a person sees; reaction Easy 0.5 / Normal 0.3 / Hard 0.2 s, nothing from after the lock): Easy stops at Canter, picks early (favours Middle) and holds, strike spread 0.16 s; Normal reaches Gallop, keeps its shield on the other lance until 0.4 s before the lock, answers the other shield once 60% of the time, sometimes feints, spread 0.09 s, handles twists half the rounds; Hard rides for Thunder on 65% of passes, remembers which height the other rider leaves open, feints, and on 70% of passes makes its last move just before the fumble window, spread 0.045 s. Bot results: Normal vs Normal 24-24 over 48 matches (rounds 91-94 over 40), rounds about 17-24 s for Normal pairs (12-14 s when Hard lands Thunder hits); Hard beats Normal (90-6 rounds) and Normal beats Easy (82-48 and 90-11) from both sides.
- Code: `js/levels/world37/` (the kit `tourney-kit.js`, `TOURNEY`, reuses World 31's `SHRUNK` helpers and adds `K.squire`, `K.horse`, `K.lance`, `K.heraldShield`, `K.sky`, `K.hills`, `K.pavilions`, `K.pennants`, `K.crowd`, `K.hay`, `K.herald`, `K.timber`, `K.stoneBlock`, `K.dustTrail`, `K.goldHelp` and `tourney*` sounds; its header is the manual; outfits in `tourney-outfits.js`), brains in `js/cpu/levels/world37/`, previews in `js/previews/world37.js` (helpers `pw37*`, heroes drawn through `TOURNEY_OUTFITS`), tools in `tools/w37/` (`check.js`, `stuck.js` person-like partners, `trace.js`, `shots.js` with `--until`, `outfits.js` the outfit sheet). Each def sits at the bottom of its level file as `TOURNEY.defs.<mode>`; `world.js` sets `WORLDS[36]` by index and pushes the four. World 37's script tags sit in one block after `js/cpu/levels/golf.js` in `index.html` (World 36 was built in parallel); `liveWorlds` skips the empty slot until World 36 lands. Each team level has a `plan(st)` that the prompts, the HUD chips and the brain all read.
- Review (Fable, Nov 2026): matches the spec and passes every bot check on top of the outfit system (PR #40) and World 36 (PR #43). Fixed: 37-3 soft lock at the wall (a pusher who had let go of the bar or was knocked as the tower rolled up to the wall could never take it again, because the pusher's tick stopped in the bridge state, so the bridge could never drop; the pusher now works the bar until the bridge is down, and the plan sends a computer pusher back to it); 37-1 the royal stand (a driver who tapped the reins instead of holding them never got below a canter and lapped the lists for ever: near the stand the horses no longer speed up between tugs); 37-4 the hoofbeat had no cue until a tap landed on it (now a soft thud on every beat and a lamp in each rider's panel: white on the beat, green ✓ after a tap on it, red ✗ after one off it), and the message line lay over the round pill (it sits under the rider panels now); 37-1 outfit: Bo's postilion cap covered both ears (the cap sits between them now). Checked and left: a computer pair takes no bad events and all 64 collectables; late, eager, slow and clumsy partners clear all three team levels and an idle one never does; Normal v Normal 34-38 over 72 matches, Hard beats Normal 24-0, Normal beats Easy 22-2. For Gavyn to judge by hand: in 37-2 a tap jump at a canter only just clears a hurdle (hold the jump), and in 37-4 a Perfect High hit at Thunder unhorses in one pass (the spec's numbers). The four preview blurbs are a little over 80 characters.
- Status: built, bot-tested and reviewed (Nov 2026); not yet played by hand.

### World 38: Operation Midnight (team, one heist in four acts)
Secret agents at night: Pip and Bo steal the Sunrise Diamond back from Count Zero (round, bowler hat, dark glasses, a white 0 on his tie; seen only in glimpses, and he gets away shouting). All four levels are team levels in Gavyn's story order, and each one's first frame continues the last frame of the one before (the cut glass circle, the ceiling vent, the boat). The briefcase with the diamond glows gold at the seams and stays on screen through 38-3 and 38-4. Each level opens with a typed radio line from "Control" (`K.STORY`), and 38-4's results add "Mission complete. The Sunrise Diamond is going home." Spec: `pip-and-bo/notes/world38-secret-agent-spec.md` (Fable, 2026-11-14).

- **38-1 Skyscraper Window Climb** (mode `spyclimb`, a scrolling climb up a glass tower): both do the same job on suction gloves, tied by a rope. Hold the action to clamp. Clamped, you're safe from searchlights, gusts and the helicopter, and a still agent is safe from lights. Don't step on a glowing window: it opens. Hold still while a Zero leans out. Creep past pigeons. At a gap, one clamps at the gold ring and the other swings across. Collect 20 Intel envelopes. Medal goals: time (Gold 205, Silver 340), Slips + Spotted, envelopes.
- **38-2 Laser Vault Heist** (mode `spyvault`, side view, four stretches): roles are MIRROR and RUNNER. The mirror agent raises a brass hand mirror (hold the action, then ▲ ▼ to tilt it 0/45/90/135/180°) to bend or block beams, which are traced for real against the walls, doors, mirrors and agents. The runner ducks or slides under beams, jumps the low ones, presses far-side buttons and grabs keycards. Timed sweepers and blinkers run on the level's clock (`pathClear` predicts them). The Alarm meter or the shutter means a lockdown. It ends in the Diamond Room with the cat, the big button and the alarm (`Music.cue({song: 'spychase'})`). Medal goals: time (Gold 185, Silver 320), Zaps, keycards and gems (20).
- **38-3 Air Vent Crawl** (mode `spyvents`, a dark duct maze on a grid of `S.Z` px cells, player-paced): roles are LIGHT (the torch; hold the action to cover it over grates when a guard is below) and HACKER (carries the briefcase; the action at a panel opens the wrist screen with 4-6 arrows, readable only inside the torch cone). The ducts have thin metal (a full-speed crawl booms), steam, fans (hacked: stopped `S.fanStop` s, and held while one of you is through and the other isn't; failing-circuit fans stop after each strobe), drop shafts (hold ▼ to brake), squeezes where the case is passed through, and 20 microfilm reels. The Sniffer robot (stretch 2 on, two in stretch 4) follows by BFS; a shut door holds it for `S.cutT` s. Heard or Caught sends you back to the checkpoint. After a restart the Sniffer comes back 12 cells behind (forward BFS from the start), only once both have left the checkpoint (or after a grace period). It is slower after every catch at the same checkpoint (down to 60%), so a slow team always gets through. Medal goals: time (Gold 240, Silver 400; the spec had 210/360, but the computer pair takes about 200 s), Heard or Caught, reels.
- **38-4 Speedboat Getaway** (mode `spyboat`, fast auto-scroll, three channels drawn in depth; stretch 1 has two): roles are STEERER (▲ ▼ channel, ▶ ◀ throttle, action = horn for gulls and fishing boats) and GADGETS (◀ ▶ pick, action = use: Smoke, Oil, Boost, and Magnet from stretch 3; brass crates refill +1 of each).
  - Chasers copy our channel `S.lagT` s of travel late (by distance, `trackLane`), so a late dodge wrecks a follower. One that draws level is a Bump.
  - The helicopter's light locks on, then the net drops on its channel. The net never comes within 1900 px of a ramp, gate or bridge.
  - Ramps clear the chain or mine line behind them at full speed or on a Boost. Closing gates need a Boost. Each gate and ramp before stretch 4 has a slow way round over sandbars.
  - The grab in stretch 4: hold the action on the tug bar and take the marked low arch of the bridge.
  - The last ramp needs a Boost; you land in the seaplane.
  - The Chase meter full means Caught. A shut gate, the claw winning, or falling short of the seaplane all go back to the checkpoint, with at least 1 Boost.
  - Gold help comes after 3 bumps.
  - Medal goals: time (Gold 195, Silver 300; the spec had 175, but the 3.4 s stretch intros count, and the computer pair takes 177 s at full throttle, so the review gave people 18 s of room), Bumps, Intel bottles (20).
- Code:
  - `js/levels/world38/`:
    - the kit `spy-kit.js` (global `SPY`), which wraps World 31's `SHRUNK` helpers and adds the Control radio, `K.alertBar`, `K.zero`, `K.briefcase`, `K.laser`, `K.intel`, `K.nightSky`, `K.skyline`, `K.searchBeam`, the outfit parts and outfits, and the music and ambience (`spysneak`, `spysneakLow`, `spychase`, `spychaseUp`; `spyHeights`, `spyVaultHum`, `spyDucts`, `spyHarbour`), registered from the kit so World 38 never touches `music-tracks.js`. Its header is the manual.
    - one file per level, with its def at the bottom as `SPY.defs.<mode>`.
    - `world.js` sets `WORLDS[37]` and pushes the four levels.
  - Brains are in `js/cpu/levels/world38/` and previews in `js/previews/world38.js` (helpers `pw38*`).
  - Tools are in `tools/w38/`: `check.js` for a computer pair, `stuck.js` for person-like partners (lag, eager, slow, noact), `trace.js`. `tools/w37/shots.js` works for 38-x codes.
  - Each level has a `plan(st)` that the prompts, the HUD and the brain all read. Level sounds are `Sfx.extra.<mode>*`; kit sounds are `spy*`.
- Status: built and bot-tested (Nov 2026). The computer pair clears all four (38-1 Gold, 38-2 99 s with 0 zaps, 38-3 199 s with 0 heard, 38-4 177 s with 0 bumps), with every collectable. The person-like partners clear all four; the extreme "slow" partner takes 650-800 s in 38-3. Waiting for a Fable review; not yet played by hand.
- Review (Fable, Nov 2026): matches the spec; the computer pair still clears all four with no bad events and 20/20 pick-ups. Fixed, all found by playing a person-like partner next to the computer:
  - 38-1: a route leg only counted when a climber stood within 16 px of an unmarked point, so two people who climbed their own way never got the checkpoint, the gold rings or the hook prompt, and the computer waited at the hook for ever. A leg now also counts once a climber is above it, and a computer held short by the rope gives up on the point after 5 s (`a.tautT`) and asks the person over. The computer no longer lets go while its partner is dangling. The hook prompt shows whenever the partner is within rope reach of the hook.
  - 38-2: a slide that ended under a beam stood up for one frame and got zapped even with duck held; the mirror could go up facing backward (then "tilt up-forward" bounced the wrong way with no hint), so it now always goes up facing into the vault; a zap can no longer knock anyone back through a shut door.
  - 38-3: the optional reels blocked the route steps, so people who skipped one got no checkpoint or prompts and the computer waited in a side duct (each waiting for the other). A reel step now also ends when someone is at the next real step, or when the leash holds the computer back (`a.leashT`). Steam hopped a crawler into solid metal on a diagonal; the facing now follows real movement; restarts before the first checkpoint count toward the slower Sniffer; the torch holder is asked to light whichever panel is open.
  - 38-4: Gold time 185 to 195 s. Ready cards: the action key was listed twice (the legend and the automatic action chip), so the legends now only list movement; the horn is in the how-to. Bo's earpiece light is green (no red on Bo).
  - Slow, stop-and-go partners take 12 minutes in 38-3 next to the computer (it waits for them, never stuck). An idle partner cannot finish any of the four, as in every team level.

### World 39: Crystal Caverns (mixed)
One trip underground in four parts, each level ending where the next begins. **Story:** the lamps of the old mining town of Glimmer Gulch have gone out, because the glow crystals stopped coming up from the deep mine. Pip and Bo ride the hundred-year-old hand-cranked lift down (39-1), find the Crystal Cavern's guardian, a giant crystal golem, stomping about in pain with dark shards stuck in it, and climb it to pull them out (39-2). The shards are broken teeth: a colossal crystal-eating cave worm bursts from the wall and chases them, and at the dead end the calmed golem steps out and flings the worm down a bottomless shaft (39-3). The grateful golem claps a chamber of soft earth full of gems and Pip and Bo race each other for them (39-4); the last results card says "The lamps of Glimmer Gulch are glowing again." Old Mabel, the town's last miner, shouts one line down a brass speaking tube on each intro card. Gavyn picked the theme and these four levels on 2026-11-14 from a Fable spec (project file `pip-and-bo/notes/world39-crystal-caverns-spec.md`). World index 38. Not a toddler world: the lift really drops, the golem is huge and angry, the worm has rings of crystal teeth; nobody gets hurt on screen (a lurch, a tumble into glow-mushrooms, a gulp-and-spit-out, or a restart of the stretch). No purple as the main crystal colour (Gavyn's menu rule). Each level has its own outfit (shared outfit system, parts `cv*` in the kit): 39-1 old-time miners (Pip's yellow hard hat with a brass lamp, Bo's flat cap and candle lantern; soot builds up), 39-2 crystal climbers (role props: brass tongs for the puller, a tuning-fork chime for the soother), 39-3 on the run (goggles up, torn scarf, bandana, satchel; worm slime after a Gulp), 39-4 prize diggers (gold hard hat with an orange stripe vs a wide-brimmed silver hat; dirt builds up, the gem sack fills, the round loser sits with the hat over the eyes, the match winner gets a crystal crown).

- **39-1 Mine Lift Plunge** (scrolling down, team, mode `cavelift`, ownStep + ownHUD + noFalls, roomWord 'Stretch'): an open lift on two ropes with a crank at each end. Both do the same job, one side each: ▼ lets your rope out, ▲ winds it in (tiring), neither brakes. The platform tilts when the ends differ; past 22 degrees for 0.6 s is a Lurch. The high end swings in toward the middle, so tipping the lift is how you slip past a shelf. ◀ ▶ walk the platform (the crank brakes itself), and the action shoos bats, stamps sparks and pushes the lamp crate; at the crank it slam-brakes. Trouble: shelves (Snag), falling rocks (Bonk), bats nibbling the rope, sparks, narrows (level and slow), rope knots, a waterfall, updrafts. Shared Fray meter: full = Old Mabel winches you back to the checkpoint. Stretches: The Old Shaft, The Wet Rock, The Crystal Throat (ends with Bo's rope snapping), The Swing (Pip lowers the whole lift; Bo runs to swing it round crystal spikes and grabs chains to stop). 20 glow gems on the walls, plus Mabel's lamp crate as a bonus. Gold help: gold cranks. Medals: time 190 / 330 s, Lurches (none / 3), glow gems (20 / 12). Computer pair about 168 s, no Lurch or Snag, 20/20 gems, crate delivered.
- **39-2 Sleeping Golem** (team, mode `cavegolem`, the giant is the level): the golem (three screens tall) walks right; the camera stays on it. Ledges on its body bob with its stride, the legs swing, the forearm is a lift and the palm closes into a fist. Roles swap each stretch: the PULLER holds the action at a shard (a 3 s bar), the SOOTHER rings the chime at that shard's teal ear crystal (Rage falls, flinches are cancelled). Big shards need a two-person heave. Trouble: Stomps every 4 s (hold on, hop, or stand in a hollow), sweeping hands (crouch), the fist, crystal mites on ear crystals, roof crystals, Rage geysers, low arches. Falling is a Tumble (back to the stretch's base ledge). Shared Rage meter: full = a Tantrum, both flung down and the current shard back in. Stretches: The Legs, The Belt and Back, The Chest and Arms (the pool wade), The Head (the crown shard). Gold help: a gold chime. Medals: time 215 / 370 s, Tumbles (1 / 4), glow gems (20 / 12). Computer pair 170-201 s with 1-2 Tumbles, 20/20 gems.
- **39-3 Cave Worm Chase** (scrolling right, team, mode `caveworm`): the worm follows light, so it can be steered. The LURER throws glow gems (fwd, ▲ up, ▼ low, ◀ back); the PATHFINDER swings a pick at echo crystals (they ring to show which tunnel at a fork is true), walls and timber bridges. Forks (a gem lured down the false tunnel makes the worm take it; the true one shows only after the echo crystal rings), bursts out of walls, floor and ceiling ahead (a tell), crossing plates, drops, the inhale (hold on to an anchor), seams (a gem thrown up into a seam opens a shortcut), dead ends. A Gulp spits you out slimy; the Jaws meter full = the worm chomps and the stretch restarts from its halfway point. Stretches: The Crack, The Crystal Bridges, The Hollow, Dead End (the golem's hands close on the worm and fling it down a bottomless shaft). 20 glow gems to collect. Gold help after 3 bad events. Medals: time 175 / 300 s, Gulps (none / 4), glow gems (20 / 12). Computer pair 168 s, Gold, 20/20, no Gulps. Brain: reads `plan(st)` (tx per hero, jump, slide, strike, throw); with a person pathfinder it waits for the echo before trusting a fork (if unrung it says "Which way?" and takes the high tunnel), a computer pathfinder says "Gem! That way!", and it follows a person who has gone into a tunnel rather than splitting up.
